- 10.2 grilled 2026-10-24 (all recommended): two parts. 10.2a, by namespace: a console Cost page and `aiops sizing cost --by namespace [--json]`, each namespace's request cost (every container) and waste cost (sized ones), or cores and GiB unpriced, ranked by the requests, linking its Workloads; the digest unchanged; then v0.9.0. 10.2b, labels and teams: `allocation: {labels: [...], teams: [...]}`, label values from kube-state-metrics' `kube_pod_labels` (only the keys listed, a per-cluster warning when one isn't allowlisted), the newest pod's value when pods disagree, teams by first-match rules on cluster, namespace glob and label values, else "(no team)"; the lab allowlists `team`; then v0.10.0.
- 10.2a built 2026-10-24. Changed from the grilling: a namespace is its cluster's (`kind/lab`), so two clusters' `shop` stay apart.
- 10.2a checked live 2026-10-24 on the Mac Hub (v0.9.0): `kind/lab` requests 147.29 USD/month and waste 75.29, the waste equal to its ranked Workloads' and the requests to the ready ones' (117.32), the learning `db`'s (3.84) and the excluded `idle`'s (26.13); the namespaces add up to the Contract's 148.50.
- 10.2b built 2026-10-24. Changed from the grilling: a team rule names its team in `team:`; a team or a label value spans clusters; the newest pod is the latest `kube_pod_created` (pods without one go by name); the warning is worked out when shown, from the stored rows (a cluster where no pod has the label), on the Cost page and in `sizing cost`; `--by team` exists only with team rules; a Workload without the label goes to `(no <key>)`; team and label groups don't link to the Workloads page, which filters by namespace only. The lab's pods carry `team` (one, `idle`, none).
- 10.2b checked live 2026-10-24 on the Mac Hub (v0.10.0), with demo-shop's four team rules on the `team` label: storefront 103.00 USD/month of requests (70.25 waste, 2 Workloads), payments 9.22, data 8.61, platform 0.33 and `(no team)` 27.34 (`idle` and the 4 Workloads of `demo` and `orders`) add up to the Contract's 148.50, and their waste to the namespaces' 74.65; `--by label:team` gives the same groups, and no warning.
- 10.3 grilled 2026-10-24 (all recommended, 2 rounds): two parts. 10.3a: available Savings is the waste's cost under its glossary name (a Contract and group total on the Cost page and in the CLI; what under-sized containers need stays a risk, never netted); compare periods as snapshots, each group's monthly request and waste cost now against N days ago (7, 30, 90; `latest_sizings(before=)`), with "no run that old" and the oldest date when there is none, and runs before 0.8.0 comparing only containers whose requests are known; Cost page picker and Δ column, `aiops sizing cost --compare 30d`; then v0.11.0. 10.3b: realized Savings, found by each hourly run comparing a container with the previous run and storing a drop (migration: container, when, CPU and memory before and after; no backfill) when the new request is within 10% of a Sizing shown in the 14 days before, CPU and memory apart; worth (before - after) x the pods now x the price, priced when shown, as a monthly run rate and as accrued since the change (run rate x hours, today's prices); a later rise cancels that much and a deleted Workload ends it; fewer pods and raised requests never count; `aiops sizing savings [--json]`, the Cost page's Savings summary (available, realized), one digest line "Realized this week"; cores and GiB unpriced; the agent's tool unchanged; then v0.12.0. Live checks on the Mac Hub: 10.3a `sizing cost --compare 1d` matching a hand difference of two runs; 10.3b lab `api` set to its Sizing with `kubectl set resources` (live only), realized after the next run equal to a hand computation, and a later `make lab` cancelling it.
- 10.3a built 2026-10-24. Changed from the grilling: the picker also offers 1 day (the live check's), the CLI takes any `m`, `h` or `d` and the API 1 to 400 days; each cluster is compared on its own, and one with no run that old is left out of both sides and named with its oldest run (the comparison is never empty for the others); when the grouping reads labels (`label:<key>`, or team rules with labels), every container of a cluster where no pod had them then but some do now is left out too (stored before the Contract listed them, or before kube-state-metrics exported them), like one whose requests weren't stored, and both are listed; a group gone since stays listed with its whole drop; only the Cost page and `sizing cost` say "savings available", the Workloads page, the digest and `sizing waste` still say waste.
- 10.3a checked live 2026-10-24 on the Mac Hub (v0.11.0): `sizing cost --compare 1d` compared the 23:09 UTC run with 2026-10-23 23:45 UTC (the latest at or before 00:09), left out the 4 containers stored before 0.8.0 (`db`, `idle`, `checkout`, `reports`), and its changes equal a hand computation from both runs' rows in Postgres: `kind/lab` requests +0.00 and Savings +0.73 USD/month, `kind/demo` and `kind/orders` 0; the team comparison waits for a day of labelled runs.
- 10.3b built 2026-10-24. Changed from the grilling: requests and limits are now read from the live pods (seen in the last 5 minutes), the window's largest only for a container with none, since the window's largest across pods hid a lowered request for up to the whole window (a correction to 9.1); the matched Sizing must be below the old request (one raising it can't make a saving); a drop is stored once per previous run, so a `sizing refresh` overlapping the hourly run can't store it twice; each row says whether its requests are its live pods', and only a live row starts, takes back or ends a drop (a run with kube-state-metrics down or the Workload scaled to 0 reads the window's old request), so only drops between two 0.12.0 runs count; "the pods now" are the latest run's average pods, as its waste's; a removed request ends its drops; the digest leaves the line out while nothing was realized; the Cost page's summary comes with the cost API, with no API of its own.
- 10.3b checked live 2026-10-25 on the Mac Hub (v0.12.0): `kubectl set resources` lowered lab `api` to its Sizing (116m, 102Mi, 3 pods) and the next run stored it; `sizing savings` showed 61.18 + 8.28 = 69.46 USD/month, equal to a hand computation ((1 - 0.116) x 3 x 0.0316 x 730 and (1 GiB - 102Mi) x 3 x 0.0042 x 730), and the digest said "Realized this week: 69.46 USD/month". `make lab` raised it back to 1 core and 1Gi and the run 5 minutes later ended it ("no realized Savings").
- 10.4 grilled 2026-10-25 (all recommended, 2 rounds): the Hub reads on-demand list prices from the public APIs (AWS price list, Azure Retail Prices; GCP's catalog only when the Contract names an API key secret, else skipped with a message), refreshed daily and stored with their date, so the hourly run reads the stored copy; a cluster's provider, region and instance types come from kube-state-metrics `kube_node_labels` (no new RBAC; the KSM allowlist must expose them); a node's hourly price splits into a CPU price and a GiB price by one fixed weight (default 1 vCPU = 7 GiB, per Contract), so they sum to the node price; a cluster with mixed node types gets the capacity-weighted average (total node price over total vCPU and GiB now), one price per cluster like `prices`; an optional per-Contract `discount` factor multiplies the list price (on-demand only, spot waits for Phase 16); typed `prices` win and the list fills only the gaps, so upgrading changes no shown cost, and the console says which source a price has; a cluster without labels (kind, bare metal) or a failed fetch gets no list price and says why, keeping the last good price with its age, never a made-up one. Done when hand-computed tests (split, average, discount, precedence, fallbacks) pass, the AWS price file is parsed streaming for only the instance types in use, and the Mac Hub's kind node, labelled by hand as an m5.large in us-east-1, shows the real list price equal to a hand computation, then v0.13.0.
- 10.4 built 2026-10-25. Changed from the grilling: a cluster may declare `provider: aws|azure|gcp` (kind and bare metal have a `kind://` provider_id, and the check needs a cloud name); `list_prices: {weight, discount, gcp_key_secret}` and a Contract-level `prices` are refused together (the Contract's prices already price every cluster, so the list has nothing to fill; a cluster's own `prices` are allowed beside `list_prices`); one cluster the list can't price leaves the whole Contract in cores and GiB (money for one cluster and cores for another can't be ranked), with a note per cluster; the daily lookup rides on the hourly Sizing tick (a price is looked up again after 24 h, a failed one after 1 h) and `aiops sizing prices [--refresh]` shows or forces it; the AWS offer file is streamed to disk and parsed with `ijson` (a new dependency: the file is hundreds of MB); GCP's key goes in the `X-Goog-Api-Key` header, never the URL, and a failed lookup stores only `HTTP <status> from <host>`; a cluster on a store shared with another Contract and without labels reads no nodes; the Cost and Workloads pages, `sizing cost` and `sizing waste` say where each price comes from. GCP is checked against recorded fixtures only (no Billing API key yet).
- 10.4 checked live 2026-10-25 on the Mac Hub (the local v0.13.0 image): the kind node labelled by hand as an m5.large in us-east-1 (`aiops sizing prices --refresh` read AWS's real offer file: 0.096 USD/h, the real m5.large Linux price) with the node's real capacity of 10 vCPU and 31.29 GiB gave 0.00094776 USD per GiB-hour and 7 times that per vCPU-hour; demo-shop's total requests of 5.70 cores and 5.55 GiB cost 31.45 USD/month, equal to a hand computation ((5.7 x 7 + 5.55) x 0.00094776 x 730), and `sizing cost` printed its price line.
- 10.5 grilled 2026-10-26 (all recommended, 3 rounds): reconcile only, "estimated vs billed" per cluster and period, never changing a shown cost; three slices, one release each: 10.5a AWS, 10.5b Azure, 10.5c GCP. 10.5a reads Cost Explorer `GetCostAndUsage` with the Contract's `aws` integration key (plus `ce:GetCostAndUsage`); a cluster names its bill lines by `billing: {tag: "key=value"}` (EC2 compute, usage before credits, unblended, daily); the estimate is the cluster's node-hours from a Prometheus range query at its effective price (typed or list); the period is 7 full UTC days ending 2 days ago (`--days 1` to `30`); the bill is fetched daily on the hourly Sizing tick and stored per cluster and day (a failure retried after 1 h, `--refresh` forces); 5% is the tolerance; shown by `aiops sizing bill [--json]` and a Cost page line per cluster, with the digest and the agent unchanged; a currency that differs from the Contract's is shown without a gap, never converted. Live check: one t3.small in us-east-1 tagged `aiops-cluster=kind` for about 48 hours (about 1 USD from credits, with the Operator's yes), the kind node labelled t3.small, billed equal to 0.0208 x hours within 5% of the estimate. Then v0.14.0.
- 10.5a built 2026-10-26. Changed from the grilling: the bill has its own endpoint, `GET /contracts/{id}/billing?days=`, not a field of the cost API (it asks Prometheus for node-hours, and the Cost page's other requests shouldn't wait for that); the estimate is node capacity-hours (hourly samples of `kube_node_status_capacity`) at the cluster's unit prices, so a changing node count is counted, not only the latest nodes; `billing:` is refused without the `aws` integration or without a Prometheus; the read covers the last 30 full days and leaves today out; a failed read keeps the stored days and the last good read's time; the CLI exits 1 when any cluster is outside 5%. 964 Python tests (36 new, 27 mutations caught) and 12 console tests pass. The live check (a tagged t3.small for about 48 hours) waits for the Operator's yes; AWS GovCloud and China partitions are not read.
- 10.5a checked live 2026-10-26 against the AWS sandbox's real Cost Explorer (API only): `ce:GetCostAndUsage` added to `aiops-readonly`; `billing.fetch` with that key, signed by the Hub's own SigV4, read 30 days in USD and parsed them, an unknown tag answered with zero days and a wrong secret with `HTTP 400 from ce.us-east-1.amazonaws.com` (no secret in it). The sandbox cannot show a nonzero tagged bill: its SCP allows EC2 only in us-east-2, free-plan usage costs 0 even before credits (the Operator's one-minute instance of 2026-10-23 shows 0.0175 hours and 0 USD), and a linked account cannot activate cost-allocation tags. Phase 10's "one bill reconciles" therefore waits for an account that bills (reconciliation itself is proven by fixtures and hand computations).
- 10.5b grilled 2026-10-26 (2 rounds, all recommended): the same bill check for Azure Cost Management with the Contract's `azure` integration (tenant, client id, subscription from its env, client secret from env_secrets; Reader or Cost Management Reader); `billing: {tag}` only; `ActualCost`, `PreTaxCost`, Virtual Machines, usage charges, daily; a billed cluster reads its `provider`'s bill, else the only cloud integration (both and no `provider` is refused); `list_prices.currency` (Azure's lookup passes `currencyCode`; non-USD refused unless every cluster sets `provider: azure`) so a BRL subscription compares with BRL prices; migration 0022 keys `list_prices` by currency. Live check: one Standard_B1s Linux VM in centralus in the Free Trial tagged `aiops-cluster=kind` for 24 to 48 hours (about 3.1 BRL for 48 h, with the Operator's yes), billed near 0.0652 x hours and the estimate within 5%. Then v0.15.0.
- 10.5b built 2026-10-26: `billing.aws_fetch` and `azure_fetch` (token per read, never stored; a day before the latest row without one reads as 0), `Contract.bill_provider`, `ListPrices.currency`, migration 0022; the console needs no change. The live check waits for the Operator's yes.
- 10.5b checked live 2026-10-29 to 31 against the Azure sandbox (Free Trial, BRL): a Standard_B2ps_v2 VM in centralus tagged `aiops-cluster=kind`, read with the sandbox service principal, priced from the Retail Prices API in BRL (0.3962 per hour). The free 750 hours of B2pts_v2 bill 0.0 before credits (like AWS's free plan), so the first VM was resized to the paid size; B1s was unavailable in the trial and the paid size needed the `UseStandardSecurityType` feature registered (undone). Billed for the full UTC day 2026-10-30 was 9.5077 BRL = 24 h x 0.3962, to the cent; the partial day 10-29 was 8.16 = 20.6 h x 0.3962. The estimate did not reconcile (5.15 BRL, -45.8%) because Prometheus held only 14 of 25 hourly points that day (the lab's Mac slept), and per sampled hour the estimate equals the bill. So the price, the query and the BRL currency path are proven live; the 5% rule on a day with complete samples is proven by fixtures only. Finding: `sizing bill` hides how many hours the estimate rests on; showing node-hour coverage is a follow-up. The VM, resource group and the demo-shop edits were removed. Cost about 26 BRL.
- 10.5c grilled 2026-10-31 (3 rounds, all recommended), built after 10.5d: GCP bill from the BigQuery billing export (the only route; the client enables the export, a dataset, BigQuery Data Viewer on it and Job User on the project). `billing: {tag: "key=value", table: "project.dataset.table"}` on the cluster (table required for GCP, refused for AWS and Azure; the tag must be a valid GCP label); provider chosen by `provider`, else the only cloud integration of aws, azure, gcp; the `gcp` integration's key mints a separate `bigquery.readonly` token per read (the agent's token is unchanged); the query runs in the integration's project, the table name is checked against a strict pattern and the label goes in as query parameters; `cost` before credits per UTC usage day and the `currency` column, core and RAM SKUs of Compute Engine only, Spot excluded, days without rows before the latest day read as 0; same daily read, hourly retry and 2-day lag; `list_prices.currency` also for GCP (the catalog's `currencyCode`), AWS stays USD. Not matched, and said in the docs: shared-core VMs (e2-micro, small, medium), Spot, committed-use lines, disks and network. Ships on fixtures first as v0.17.0; live check afterwards (an e2-standard-2 labelled `aiops-cluster=kind` for 24 to 48 hours, about 3.2 USD, after the Operator links billing in the sandbox and enables the export, with the Operator's yes).
- 10.5d built 2026-10-31 (v0.16.0, from the 10.5b live finding): `sizing bill` rows carry `hours`, `sampled` and `coverage`; under 100% the note says "the estimate rests on N of M sampled hours"; under 90% (`billing.MIN_COVERAGE`) `within` is null, the gap is still shown, the CLI prints "no verdict" and doesn't exit 1 for that alone, the Cost page shows it as a notice. `usage.node_hours` also returns how many hourly samples there were.
- 10.5c built 2026-10-31 (v0.17.0), as grilled: `billing.gcp_fetch` mints a `bigquery.readonly` token from the `gcp` integration's key (`GoogleKey` takes a scope; the MCP servers keep theirs), runs one `jobs.query` in `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT` and sums `cost` per UTC day and currency; a query that did not finish in 60 s, came in pages or billed a day in two currencies is refused with a message rather than read in part. `Billing.table` (strict pattern, it goes into the SQL), `Contract.bill_provider` over aws, azure and gcp, GCP label rules, and the GCP catalog's `currencyCode` (a price in another currency is refused). No migration. The live check waits for the Operator to link billing in the sandbox and turn the export on.
- 10.5c checked live 2026-11-01 to 04 against the GCP sandbox (USD): an e2-standard-2 VM in us-central1 labelled `aiops-cluster=kind`, running k3s and Prometheus (kind on the Mac would have reported its own 10 vCPU and 31 GiB, and GCP's list price is capacity x core and RAM rates, so the estimate was 4.6x the bill), read with the sandbox service account (BigQuery Job User on the project, reader on the export dataset), priced from the Cloud Billing catalog with an API key. For the full UTC day 2026-11-02, with 24 of 24 hours sampled, the bill was 1.61 USD (48 vCPU-hours 1.0469 + 192 GiB-hours 0.5613, the list rates to the cent) against an estimate of 1.59: gap -1.0%, within 5%. Finding: a newly enabled export was still filling that day 28 hours after it ended (the first read saw 20.6 of 24 VM-hours, 1.38 USD, +15.3% "outside"); it was complete 30 to 38 hours after. The 2-day lag counts from the end of the day, so it usually covers this, but a day with fewer billed hours than sampled hours could be shown as still settling instead of outside: a possible follow-up. The VM, firewall rule, API key, the service account's BigQuery grants and the demo-shop edits were removed and the sandbox's billing unlinked. Cost 2.59 USD before credits, 0 after.
- **Later slice (the Operator asked to remember it): the billed effective rate feeds prices.** After reconciliation shows the gap, a billed rate (discounts, savings plans) can replace the list price, with its own precedence rules against typed `prices` and `list_prices`.

### Phase 11: The Hub as a source, and the whole console in the app (7 slices, grill before building)

- 11.1 The Hub MCP server for the Operator: reads across Contracts, each call scoped to one. 11.2 Its writes: Approval decisions, single Sizings, the kill switch, audited as made through MCP. 11.3 Pull requests to the contracts repo for Automation and policy changes (a token limited to opening pull requests; the Operator merges). 11.4 A "what should I do now" Check ranking waste, risk and open Investigations. 11.5 to 11.7 The remaining console pages rebuilt in the app (Investigations and approvals, Teachings and Checks, Questions), then the Jinja pages deleted.
- *Done when:* Claude Code lists a Contract's top 5 Sizings and approves one through the Hub MCP server, an Automation change arrives as a pull request, and the Jinja templates are gone with every console test still passing.
- 11 grilled 2026-11-05 (4 rounds, all recommended except that asking a Question moves into 11.1): the MCP track first (11.1 to 11.4), then the console. The Hub MCP server is `/mcp` (streamable HTTP) in the Hub process, reached only through `tailscale serve` (Caddy's public port answers 404); a call needs the bearer `AIOPS_MCP_TOKEN` (refused at startup if it equals another token) and a `Tailscale-User-Login` listed in `AIOPS_CONSOLE_OPERATORS`, which the audit names. Every tool takes a required `contract` (plus `list_contracts`), its output follows that Contract's Masking, and a placeholder in an argument is mapped back only if the Hub handed it out; every call, reads included, is an `aiops.audit` line with `channel: mcp` and the login. The Investigation agent never gets these tools. 11.1a: the endpoint, both checks, audit, masking, `list_contracts`, `sizings` (ranked by waste cost, `top`, filters) and `sizing`. 11.1b: `cost`, `savings`, `bill`, `investigations`, `investigation`, `checks` (each the same function as its `/api/v1` route) and `ask`, which stores a Question like the console's (not posted to Slack) and returns its id at once. 11.2: `decide(contract, investigation_id, action_ids, approve, message)` with `destructiveHint`, naming the exact Proposed actions seen, recorded as "<login> via MCP"; approving a Sizing stores an Approved Sizing with its exact values, superseded by a newer Sizing, for 12.2 to apply (nothing changes in a cluster before the Actuator); the Kill switch is a per-Contract flag in the database that refuses every write for that Contract, set through MCP or the console and cleared only in the console. 11.3: a Proposed change is a pull request to the contracts repo on a private GitHub repo (the Operator creates it; a fine-grained token for that repo only; branch protection so only the Operator merges), made through GitHub's REST API with `httpx`, refused when GitHub's `main` is not the commit the Hub loaded; `propose_change` takes typed edits to the Sizing policy only (Automation joins in 12.1), edited with `ruamel.yaml` so comments survive and validated with `contracts.build` before it opens. So the done-when's Automation change becomes a policy change. 11.4: the Agenda is a ranking without an LLM (pending approvals, failing Checks, open Investigations, top waste cost, Bills outside, stale data): `now(contract)`, `now()` with counts per Contract only, and a console panel. Console: 11.5 Investigations, approvals, the Scenario download and Token; 11.6 Teachings, Checks and Health; 11.7 Questions, then the last Jinja code goes; the Kill switch and the Agenda go straight into the app. One release per slice; no eval rerun (the Investigation agent's prompt and tools don't change). Live checks on the Mac Hub from Claude Code, under 0.10 USD for the phase with the Operator's standing yes. Glossary first: Hub MCP server, Kill switch, Approved Sizing, Proposed change, Agenda.
- 11.1a built 2026-11-06: `hubmcp.py` (FastMCP, stateless HTTP with JSON answers) mounted at `/mcp/` behind both doors, its lifespan entered in the Hub's; `list_contracts`, `sizings` (`top` 1 to 100, `cluster`, `namespace`, `risky`) and `sizing`, sharing `webapp.workloads_of` with the Workloads page and `servers.sizing.workload` with the agent's tool (whose definition is byte-for-byte unchanged, so no eval rerun). Changed from the grilling: the token must be 32 characters or more; the URL ends in `/mcp/`; the placeholders handed out are kept in memory per Contract, so after a restart the client lists again. hub-smoke turns it on and checks it from the Hub's port (401 without the token, its tools with it) and that Caddy answers 404.
- 11.1a checked live 2026-11-06 on the Mac Hub (v0.18.0) through `tailscale serve`: `/mcp/` answered 401 without the token; with it, `list_contracts` named demo-bank and demo-shop, and `sizings(demo-shop, top=5)` ranked kind/lab's api first (2653m and 2800Mi of waste, 69.58 USD/month), with the kind cluster's reads failing since 2026-11-01 shown as `failing`. Found: no audit line reached the Hub's output, MCP's or the agent's, ever: `aiops serve` never set up logging, so Python dropped every `aiops` INFO line. Fixed in v0.18.1: `serve` gives the `aiops` loggers a stderr handler at INFO.
- 11.1b built 2026-11-06: `cost`, `savings` (both from `webapp.allocation_of`, shared with the Cost page), `bill` (`billing.report`), `investigations` (`open`, `limit`), `investigation` (refused for another Contract's id), `checks` and `ask` (the console's ask, not marked read-only, the question's placeholders put back for the agent). Changed from the grilling: a comparison's clusters, like `sizings`', come as a list, because masking leaves dict keys alone and a cluster's name can be the client's; a `by` grouping named after the client (a label key like `acme.io/team`) comes back from its placeholder. A listed Answer is masked before it is cut to 500 characters. Known limit: a title is cut at 200 when it is stored, so a term cut in two there goes out as its fragment.
- 11.1b checked live 2026-11-06 on the Mac Hub (v0.19.0) from Claude Code through `tailscale serve`: `cost(demo-shop, by=team, compare_days=7)` gave storefront 103.00 USD/month of requests and 70.34 of waste, the five teams adding up to 145.79 and 75.30, with each team's change; `savings` the same 75.30 available and nothing realized; `bill` no billed cluster; `investigations` the four Triages newest first; `checks` none. Claude Code asked before `ask` (not read-only); the Question ran on claude-haiku-5.5 (6 model calls, 0.0052 USD recorded), answered that the cluster API and Prometheus were unreachable (the kind cluster is stopped) and fell back to the stored Sizing of lab/leaky (OOM-killed at 256Mi, needs about 308Mi), and `investigation` returned it. Every call was an `aiops.audit` line with the login; the Hub has a Slack bot, and the Question has no Slack thread.
- 11.2 split 2026-11-06 (the Operator's choice): 11.2a `decide` and the Kill switch, 11.2b Approved Sizings. While a Kill switch is on, approving is refused and the pause kept (the Operator's choice over turning it into a rejection).
- 11.2a built 2026-11-06: migration 0023 `kill_switches` (a row while on: who, when); `settle`, the one decision path, refuses an approval while it is on (`KillSwitchOn`, said as such by the API, the console, Slack and the Hub MCP server) and claims nothing; `agent.refuse_when_killed`, inside the Executed-action recorder, reads it on every call the approval gate covers (vendors' tools too) and answers an error instead of running, so a change approved just before the switch went on is refused and recorded as FAILED. The Hub MCP server: `decide(contract, id, pause, approve, reason)` and `turn_on_kill_switch(contract)`, `list_contracts` shows each switch, `investigation` a `pause` token. The console app: a Kill switch bar on every page and `GET`/`POST`/`DELETE /api/v1/contracts/{id}/kill-switch`, the API's first changes (same-origin, identity, cookie). Changed from the grilling: `decide` names the pause by its exact deadline as text, because the agent's Proposed actions carry no id of their own and one decision covers the whole pause.
- 11.2a checked live 2026-11-07 on the Mac Hub (v0.20.0, migration 0023 applied): from Claude Code, `turn_on_kill_switch(demo-shop)` answered on, `list_contracts` showed it on for demo-shop only, and the Hub logged it on by `mcp:<login>` with its audit line; the console app's bar showed who and since when, and its "Turn it off" sent `DELETE /api/v1/contracts/demo-shop/kill-switch`, logged off by `console:<login>`, after which `list_contracts` showed it off. No Investigation was paused at the time, so the refusal of an approval was proven by the tests only (every path, and a change refused where it would run).
- 11.2b grilled 2026-11-07 (the Operator's choice, the recommended one): an Approved Sizing is superseded by any later stored Sizing of its container, whatever its values; a run that failed on its cluster, or is still going, supersedes nothing. Defaults: one container per approval, requests only (limits stay `keep` until Phase 12), refused while learning, excluded, with no change, or under a Kill switch; no revoke (the next run supersedes it); shown through the Hub MCP server only.
- 11.2b built 2026-11-07: migration 0024 `approved_sizings` (the run, the requests it changes, who and when; no foreign key, so pruning a run keeps the record), stored only where the Contract has no Kill switch in the same statement; current while no later run read its cluster. The Hub MCP server: `approve_sizing(contract, cluster, namespace, name, container, cpu, memory, kind)` with `destructiveHint`, the values as `sizing` showed them (a mismatch answers with the latest), and `approved` (who, when) on a container in `sizing` and `sizings`. The agent's `sizing_for_workload` and the console's API are unchanged (no eval rerun, no regenerated types); `servers.sizing.allowed` is shared with it.
- 11.2b checked live 2026-11-07 on the Mac Hub (v0.21.1, migration 0024 applied) from Claude Code: `sizings(demo-shop, top=5)` ranked kind/lab's api first (69.58 USD/month of waste), and `approve_sizing` with its shown 116m and 91Mi answered approved; `sizing` then showed it approved by `mcp:<login>` with the time, the row stored cpu 0.116 and memory 91Mi against run 374, and the call was an `aiops.audit` line with the login. A deliberately wrong value (db's cpu as 70m) was refused with the latest (68m, 286Mi), and nothing was stored for it. The kind cluster is stopped, so the approval is of the last run that read it (2026-11-01 11:34 UTC), current until a run reads kind again. Phase 11's first done-when (list the top 5 Sizings and approve one through the Hub MCP server) is met; nothing changes in the cluster until the Actuator (12.2). Also released: v0.21.1 (oauthlib 4.0.1, Dependabot #23); the console's dev-only minimatch alert (#24) dismissed as tolerable risk (eslint still requires minimatch 10).
- 11.3 changed 2026-11-07 (the Operator's choice): GitHub Free enforces no branch protection or ruleset on a private repo, so nothing on GitHub stops the token from pushing to `main`. Instead the Hub's code only ever pushes an `aiops/` branch and opens a pull request (a test asserts no write touches `main`; reading its ref is how the commit is checked), and the Hub runs only the contracts the Operator pulls onto its machine, where a commit nobody reviewed shows in `git log` first. GitHub Pro (enforced protection) and Operator-signed commits checked at load were held back. The contracts repo is the private `aiops-contracts`, pushed 2026-11-07.
- 11.3 asked 2026-11-07: the edits are `set_rule`, `remove_rule`, `set_default` and `set_policy` (the Operator's choice over the two recommended, so a change can be undone through a pull request too), and the Kill switch does not refuse a proposal (recommended: a pull request changes nothing until merged).
- 11.3 built 2026-11-07: `proposals.py` and `propose_change(contract, edits, title, why)` on the Hub MCP server. At start the Hub notes the commit its clone's HEAD names (a loose ref, `packed-refs` or a detached HEAD) and each `<id>/contract.yaml`'s git blob id; a proposal reads main's ref and the file at that commit and refuses unless both match, makes the edits with `ruamel.yaml` (comments kept; new rules in flow style, first), reads the result with PyYAML as the Hub will and refuses if anything but `sizing` changed, nothing did, or the Contract would not build, all before the first write; then a branch `aiops/<id>-<UTC time>`, the commit there (the Contents API writes the default branch when `branch` is missing, so a test checks every write names it), and the pull request. `AIOPS_CONTRACTS_REPO` and `AIOPS_CONTRACTS_TOKEN`; the token only in the `Authorization` header to `api.github.com`, an HTTP failure shown as its status and host. A leftover branch after a failed write is deleted by hand.
- 11.3 checked live 2026-11-07 on the Mac Hub (v0.22.0) from Claude Code: with the private `aiops-contracts` cloned at the Hub's contracts dir (its `main` the commit the Hub loaded, `1f86160`), `propose_change(demo-shop, set_rule lab/web -> high-availability)` opened pull request #1 from `aiops/demo-shop-20261107-205027` to `main`, changing one file by one line: the new rule first under `rules:`, in the file's own flow style, every comment and every other line untouched (`policy: off` included). `main` stayed at `1f86160`; the description carried the reason, the edit in words and the login; the Hub logged the URL and the call's `aiops.audit` line. Phase 11's second done-when (a change arrives as a pull request, a policy change since the grilling) is met. The pull request was closed unmerged and its branch deleted.
- 11.4 asked 2026-11-08 (both recommended): a fixed order of urgency, not a score (Kill switch on, pauses by deadline, failing Checks newest first, stale data oldest first, Bills outside by biggest gap, running Investigations oldest first, the top 3 Workloads by waste cost), and the console's panel above the Workloads rather than a tab of its own. Stale data is Sizing reads failing per cluster and bill reads failing (a cluster no longer in the Contract left out).
- 11.4 built 2026-11-08: `agenda.py` (`of`, `counts`) from what the Hub stores (the Kill switch, unfinished Investigations, Check schedules, the latest Sizing runs, bill tries, `billing.report`'s rows, the Workloads ranking); the Hub MCP server's `now(contract)` and `now()` (counts per Contract, each Contract's id and name under its own Masking); `GET /api/v1/contracts/{id}/agenda` and the console app's Now panel, each item linking its Investigation, Workload or the Cost page. A Check whose Run is still going is a running Investigation, not a failing Check; one whose Run errored is failing ("could not run"), as the Hub's Slack thread counts it.
- 11.4 checked live 2026-11-08 on the Mac Hub (v0.23.0) from Claude Code: `now()` gave Demo bank 1 stale and Demo shop 1 stale and 3 waste (17 ms); `now(demo-shop)` the kind cluster's Sizing reads failing since 2026-11-01 13:56 UTC (the cluster is stopped), then kind/lab's api (69.58 USD a month of waste), report (2.62) and db (1.41) (7 ms), each an `aiops.audit` line. The console app's Now panel showed the same four items, in the same order, above the Workloads, the waste items as links. api is listed although its Sizing was approved in 11.2b: the Agenda ranks by waste cost alone. The MCP track of Phase 11 (11.1 to 11.4) is done.
- 11.5 asked 2026-11-08 (all recommended): split into 11.5a (read: the Investigations list and page, the Scenario download, the Token page) and 11.5b (act: approving and rejecting, Report feedback, Jira filing, which move together as actions on the page); a Report, Answer or Check verdict is shown as its fields, as text, with no Markdown rendering and no new dependency (the model's bold and tables are lost, the app's no-HTML rule kept).
- 11.5a built 2026-11-08: `GET /api/v1/contracts/{id}/investigations`, `GET /api/v1/investigations/{id}` (an Investigation of a Contract no longer loaded answers 404), `GET /api/v1/investigations/{id}/scenario.yaml` (`console.scenario_file`, shared with the Jinja page, so the two files are the same) and `POST /api/v1/token` (identity and same origin, `console.set_cookie` shared with the Jinja form; a 401 now names `/app/?view=token`). The app's `Investigations.tsx`: the list, the page (updates every 5 s while running) and the token page; the header's Investigations and Approver token, and the Agenda's items, link the app's pages. The Jinja pages stay, linked from the app's page for deciding, until 11.7.
- 11.5a checked live 2026-11-08 on the Mac Hub (v0.24.0) in the console app: demo-shop's Investigations listed newest first (the 2026-11-06 Question, then four Triages), each a link; the Question's page showed its Answer and three Evidence lines as text, its tokens (41516 in, 2014 out), cost (0.0052 USD) and one masked name, no decisions, and its nine tool calls with their arguments (seven failed: the kind cluster is stopped); Save as Scenario downloaded `demo-shop-706cb355.yaml` (7.8 KB, a Question Scenario with eight tools' recordings) with a 200 in the Hub's log; the token page answered a wrong token with "that is not the approver token" (`POST /api/v1/token` 403) and left the browser's cookie as it was.
- 11.5b built 2026-11-08: `POST /api/v1/investigations/{id}/decision` (the pause's deadline as the page showed it, approve, reason), `/feedback` (right, or wrong with 1 to 2000 characters) and `/jira`, each a change (same origin, identity, cookie) through the Jinja console's own functions, so both record `console:<login>` and a Kill switch, a Stale pause or a filed issue refuse alike, with the Hub's words shown on the page; the page gains `jira` (project, issue and its link, who is filing since when) and `can_judge` (a finished Triage's or Check's Report). The app's page: Approve, Reject with a reason, Right, Wrong with what was wrong, Create Jira issue; the page as it is after each comes back and replaces it. The link to the older page for deciding is gone.
- 11.5b checked live 2026-11-08 on the Mac Hub (v0.25.0) in the console app: the 2026-10-24 03:05 Triage's page offered Right and Wrong, no decision panel and no Jira line (demo-shop has no Jira); Right added "console:<login>: right" under the buttons without a reload. Wrong (it drafts a Teaching with the model), Approve, Reject and Create Jira issue were left to the tests: no Investigation was paused (the kind cluster is stopped) and no Contract has Jira.
- 11.6 asked 2026-11-08 (all recommended): split like 11.5, 11.6a Teachings (their actions), 11.6b Checks with the Deploy verifications and Health with the month's model spend, so the old main page's leftovers move by the end of 11.6; Health checks the Contract shown only, like every app page.

Follow-ups before 0.28.1 aren't recorded.

Released as v0.18.0 to v0.29.0.

### Phase 12: Actuator (8 slices, grilled 2026-11-09)

- 12.1 Thin slice: `aiops actuator` with its own token, pulling over HTTPS, honouring the Kill switch; an Approved Sizing resized in place on a Deployment's pods, with its ConfigMap, Kubernetes Events and the Hub's `actuations` audit trail. 12.2 The pod-creation webhook (fail open) so new pods keep the values held, and handing a container back when a person changes its template. 12.3 Pre-checks: QoS class, HPA and VPA, ResourceQuota and LimitRange, a canary pod then one at a time, `Infeasible` and `Deferred` resizes. 12.4 Auto-rollback with backoff, and Slack messages. 12.5 Automation: the Contract's `actuator:` block, `automate: true` on Sizing policy rules, the churn rule, the pause annotation, the Hub unreachable. 12.6 Revert from the console and the Hub MCP server, and the console's Automation view. 12.7 PDB-safe eviction behind the flag, in windows. 12.8 Other kinds: StatefulSet, DaemonSet, Argo Rollout in place; Job and CronJob through the webhook only.
- Grilled 2026-11-09 (all recommended, three rounds):
  - **Shape.** The Actuator is its own Deployment in each client cluster whose Contract names an `actuator_token_secret` for it, with its own ServiceAccount and token (bound to the Contract and the cluster), so the Relay keeps its read-only identity; it works for Direct clusters too. It refuses to start below Kubernetes 1.33. Every minute it pulls its desired state (`GET /actuator/desired`: the Approved Sizings, later the Automation Sizings, and the Kill switch) and reports outcomes (`POST /actuator/reports`), plain HTTPS through the Hub's public boundary; the Hub never pushes. This amends ADR 0004's "over the Relay connection".
  - **No CRDs.** The Hub is the only source of truth: a client's GitOps can't read the private contracts repo, and Approved Sizings never pass through git, so CRDs would be a second copy kept in sync by our own code. The Actuator keeps what it applied in one ConfigMap in its own namespace, writes Kubernetes Events on the Workloads, and the Hub keeps the audit trail. Its RBAC: `patch pods/resize`, `create pods/eviction` (12.7), its own ConfigMap, `create events`, its own webhook's `caBundle`; never `patch deployments`, which could change images.
  - **What it changes.** Requests only (limits stay `keep`), never above the limit, never a change of QoS class (skipped, with an event). In place first; the fail-open webhook gives new pods the values held; eviction only behind the Contract's flag (off), inside a window, only where an in-place resize is `Infeasible`, one pod at a time, at least 5 minutes apart, through the Eviction API so PDBs hold, never to make room.
  - **How long values last.** An applied Approved Sizing stays held after it stops being current, until a later approval, Automation or a Revert (resized back to the template's values in place). "Current" now means waiting to be applied: one superseded before it was applied never is. A person changing the template's requests wins: the container is handed back (`HandedBack`). While the Hub is unreachable, the Actuator keeps what it holds and changes nothing new (an event after 15 minutes). The Kill switch stops resizes and evictions, and the webhook lets pods through unchanged; nothing is reverted at once. Locally: scale the Actuator to zero (the webhook fails open) or annotate a Workload `aiops-actuator/pause: "true"`.
  - **Safety.** An HPA on a resource's utilization: that request is left alone. A VPA in any mode but `Off`: the Workload is skipped. Over ResourceQuota or outside LimitRange: skipped, never clamped. A canary pod for 10 minutes, then one at a time; `Infeasible` stops that Workload, `Deferred` waits 10 minutes. Rollback within an hour of a resize on an OOM kill, a restart, or 2 minutes NotReady: resized back in place, then no new Sizing of that container for 24 hours, doubling; 3 rollbacks in 7 days end Automation of the Workload. "Adoption threshold" is dropped: the canary covers it.
  - **Automation.** `automate: true` on a Sizing policy rule, under the Contract's `actuator:` block (`eviction`, `timezone`, `windows` of days and hours, which gate eviction only). Changes under 10% are ignored; increases apply at once, decreases at most once a day per container.
  - **Seen.** The Hub's `actuations` (90 days), the console's Automation view with Revert, `revert_sizing` on the Hub MCP server; Slack only for a rollback or an end of Automation, resizes in the daily digest.
  - CRDs and their rendering from the contracts repo are dropped; KWOK goes to Deferred. The live check on kind comes after 12.4.
- 12.1 built 2026-11-09: a cluster's `actuator_token_secret` (32+ characters, never a Relay's, the approver's, the MCP or a webhook token; the Hub refuses to start otherwise). `GET /actuator/desired`: the token's cluster's current Approved Sizings of Deployments in its namespaces, none while the Kill switch is on; `POST /actuator/reports`: Actuations stored as the token's Contract and cluster (migration 0026 `actuations`, kept 90 days, a resent report kept once), refused whole (422) for a namespace outside the cluster's; both public through Caddy. `aiops actuator` (`deploy/actuator`: `pods/resize`, Events and its own ConfigMap, no `patch deployments`) refuses Kubernetes below 1.33, pulls every minute and applies each Approved Sizing newer than what it applied to that container once, whatever the outcome: every live pod of the Deployment's own ReplicaSets resized in place, never above the limit, skipped when the container's `resizePolicy` would restart it or there is no live pod, failed alone when its pods can't be listed; its ConfigMap, an Event on the Deployment and the report, resent until the Hub takes it (one it refuses with a 4xx other than 401, 403 or 429 is dropped with an error, so later ones aren't stuck behind it). Thin on purpose: "applied" is the API server's yes (the kubelet's verdict is 12.3), all pods at once (the canary is 12.3), Deployments only, a refused approval waits for the next one, and new pods start from their template until 12.2. An integration test on kind resizes `inventory` and checks the kubelet allocated it without a restart (skipped below 1.33; it passed in CI on kind v1.35.0). Changed from the grilling: 12.1 keeps no template requests yet (12.2's hand-back needs them).
- 12.2 built 2026-11-09: the Actuator's pod-creation webhook (`deploy/actuator/webhook.yaml`: `failurePolicy: Ignore`, 2 s, CREATE of pods in the Contract's namespaces): a new pod of a Deployment whose container it holds as applied gets the requests it applied (a JSON Patch, never above the pod's limit), only while the pod still asks what the template asked when it applied, never in `kube-*` or its own namespace, nothing while the Kill switch is on, and the pod is created whatever goes wrong. Its Deployment is told from the pod's ReplicaSet and `pod-template-hash`, with no API call. TLS with a self-signed certificate made at each start and patched into its own webhook's `caBundle` (RBAC on that one object). Each minute, Hub reachable or not, a held container whose template's requests changed, or whose Deployment or container is gone, is handed back: `HandedBack` Event, an Actuation `handed_back` (migration 0027), never held again. A hold from 12.1 (no template recorded) is handed back. `demo/actuator-smoke.sh` (in `make deploy-smoke`) runs the real image and manifests on kind with no Hub: a replaced pod of a held Deployment came up with the held 37m, and with the Actuator at zero with its template's 10m. Found by it on kind: the API server calls `/mutate?timeout=2s`, which the webhook first answered 404 (so it failed open).
- 12.3 built 2026-11-10: pre-checks before any resize, each a skip with its reason, never a clamp: the pods' QoS class would change (Kubernetes refuses it; a Guaranteed container needs its limits changed too), a VPA in any mode but `Off` (none installed is none), the namespace's LimitRange (container minimum, maximum, limit-to-request ratio), an increase times the live pods passing a ResourceQuota (`requests.<r>` or `<r>`; a decrease never does). An HPA of the Deployment scaling on a resource's utilization (`Resource` or this container's `ContainerResource`; cpu for one without metrics) leaves that request alone. The webhook keeps a pod's QoS class too. Then a rollout held in its ConfigMap (`rolling`): one canary pod, watched 10 minutes, then the others one at a time, each once the kubelet allocated the last (no `PodResizePending` or `PodResizeInProgress`, its container's status at the new requests, the pod Ready); `Infeasible`, or 10 minutes without that (`Deferred` included), ends it failed, its resized pods left as they are (rollback is 12.4); one replaced meanwhile is done, its replacement one still to do. It goes on only while the Hub answers and the Kill switch is off, and a person changing the template mid-rollout gets the container back. Applied is reported once every live pod has the requests. Read RBAC for HPAs, VPAs, LimitRanges and ResourceQuotas. ponytail: one pod a minute after the canary (a 50-pod Deployment takes about an hour); init containers left out of the QoS class. The integration test now waits for the kubelet's own verdict, and passed on the local kind lab (v1.37).
- 12.4 built 2026-11-10: each resized pod's restart count is kept when it is resized, and its pods are watched from the canary until an hour after the rollout ends (applied or failed): a container with the Actuator's requests that restarted since (OOM-killed or not; a new pod from the webhook counted from zero), or a pod it resized NotReady for 2 minutes since its resize, rolls it back: every pod with its requests back to the template's in place at once (a request the template doesn't set left as it is, said), `SizingRolledBack` (Warning), Actuation `rolled_back` (migration 0028). Backoff: no new Sizing of that container for 24 h, doubling with each rollback within 7 days; an approval meanwhile is skipped with the date, and the rollbacks are kept across approvals. It rolls back with the Hub unreachable, never under the Kill switch (a resize is a change). The Hub posts each new `rolled_back` Actuation to the Contract's operator channel, once however often it is resent. The integration test now rolls inventory back on kind too (no restart, back at its template's 10m). Not built from the grilling: "3 rollbacks in 7 days end Automation" waits for Automation (12.5); "resizes in the daily digest": the Sizing digest is weekly, so that waits for a decision (decided 2026-11-10: one line in the weekly digest, slice A13).
- Phase 12 (12.1 to 12.4) checked live 2026-11-10 (v0.38.0, Mac Hub and the kind lab, no model calls): demo-shop's kind cluster with an Actuator token and lab `api` on a 4 h-history policy for the check, the Actuator in kind reaching the Hub through Caddy. Approved `app` at 115m and 91Mi (from 1 core and 1Gi, 3 pods) through the Hub MCP server: the canary was resized in place 30 s later, the other two after its 10 minutes and one minute apart, same pod uids and no restarts, the kubelet allocating each; `SizingApplied` "3 of 3 pods resized in place, one at a time" and the Actuation `applied` on the Hub. A deleted pod came back at 115m and 91Mi from the webhook while the template said 1 core and 1Gi. With the Kill switch on the Actuator logged it and a new pod started from the template. A person's `kubectl set resources` (CPU 900m) was handed back (`HandedBack`, Actuation `handed_back`) and the new pods kept 900m. A fresh approval's canary, its container stopped on the node, was rolled back in place to 900m and 1Gi (Warning `SizingRolledBack`, Actuation `rolled_back`, no new Sizing for 24 h) and the operator channel got one message. Found: (1) a pod started from its template while the Kill switch is on, or while the Actuator is down (the webhook fails open), stays there after: the Kill switch went off and the pod kept 1 core while the other two held 115m, since an approval is applied once; (2) a rollback's event and Slack line start with the template's values next to "Approved Sizing, run N", which reads as if those were approved. Decided the same day: (1) such pods catch up, (2) the message says what went back to what; built as 12.4b below. Removed after: the Actuator, the Contract's changes (reverted), lab `api` back at 1 core and 1Gi.
- 12.4b built 2026-11-10 (the live check's findings, the Operator's choice): each minute, while it may change things (the Hub reachable, the Kill switch off), a Sizing it applied whose Deployment has pods without its requests (started from the template while the Kill switch was on or the webhook failed open) is applied again through the same path as an approval: the pre-checks, a canary, one pod at a time, another `SizingApplied`; the pods it was watching stay watched with their hour and the rollbacks are kept; a pre-check that fails now ends the hold (`SizingSkipped`); a Sizing rolled back, skipped or handed back never catches up. A rollback's reason says what went back to what ("2 pods back from cpu 115m to their template's cpu 1"), and its Event no longer starts with the template's values beside "Approved Sizing, run N". 10 mutations caught.
- 12.5 built 2026-11-10: `automate: true` on a Sizing rule is Automation (the rule that picks the policy decides; refused with policy `off`, or without a cluster that has an `actuator_token_secret`, the named one if the rule names one). `/actuator/desired` gains `automated`: the newest Sizing of each automated container on the token's cluster, ready and changing something (`keep` sent as none), Deployments in its namespaces, none where an Approved Sizing of that container goes, none of a Workload with 3 Rollbacks there in 7 days, nothing under the Kill switch. A list of its own, so an older Actuator never applies them as approvals. The Hub posts once to the operator channel when a rollback is the third in 7 days of a Workload it automates, with the date Automation resumes (the oldest of the three plus 7 days). In the Actuator: the churn rule (a change under 10% of what its pods ask now ignored, an increase at once, a decrease at most once a day per container, an increase beside a waiting decrease going alone); a new Automation Sizing waits unsaid while its container rolls or backs off; a skip is said once per reason (its numbers aside: a quota's skip at next hour's values is the same skip) and keeps what it holds; an approval of the run Automation applied is taken as approved in place, applying only what the churn rule left out (after a rollout under way). A pull request from `propose_change` keeps a rule's `automate` but can't set it yet. A Sizing that leaves a resource alone, approved or not, keeps the value held for it (found while building: before, it dropped that resource from the hold, so new pods got their template's value for it). The pause annotation `squero-actuator/pause: "true"`, read each minute: no resize, catch-up or rollout on that Deployment, its new pods unpatched by the webhook, rollbacks still; an approval waits. The Hub unanswered 15 minutes: one `HubUnreachable` Warning Event on its ConfigMap (Events in its own namespace added to its Role). Events say `(Automation, run N)`. 46 mutations caught. Decided while building (2026-11-10, the Operator's choice, all recommended): the Contract's `actuator:` block waits for 12.7, where its fields (eviction, timezone, windows) do something, and the cluster's Actuator token is the per-Contract opt-in; an ended Automation resumes by itself once its rollbacks are 7 days old; under Automation a hand-back is followed by the next Sizing, measured from the person's values; the pause is the Kill switch for one Deployment.
- 12.6 grilled 2026-11-10 (all recommended): a Revert stops Automation of that container until the Operator's next approval of it; refused while the Kill switch is on, as approving is; split into 12.6a (Revert end to end through the Hub MCP server) and 12.6b (the console's Automation view with Revert); `propose_change`'s `set_rule` gains `automate` in 12.6a.
- 12.6a built 2026-11-10: migration 0029 `reverts` (who and when; `reverted` added to the Actuations' outcomes). `revert_sizing(contract, cluster, namespace, name, container, kind)` on the Hub MCP server, marked destructive: refused for a cluster without an Actuator, a namespace not its, a container the Contract's Actuator never reported on, and under the Kill switch. `/actuator/desired` gains `reverts`: the cluster's, in its namespaces, until a `reverted` Actuation of that container is received after it was asked; none under the Kill switch. Automation leaves a container alone while a Revert of it is newer than every approval of it. The Actuator does each Revert newer than the last it did of that container: its pods back to their template's requests, as a rollback but without the backoff (the shared `put_back`), held no more (`SizingReverted`, Actuation `reverted`), and says "nothing held" otherwise; a paused Deployment's waits. An approval older than the container's last Revert is no longer sent, so a Revert is the last word until a new approval; the Actuator keeps the last Revert it did across new holds, so one listed again changes nothing, and an approval of the run a Revert undid applies again. `set_rule` takes `automate` (true sets it, false removes it, left out keeps it). 26 mutations caught. A container whose first rollout is still under way has no Actuation yet, so `revert_sizing` refuses it ("reported nothing") until the rollout ends: kept so, the Operator's choice 2026-11-10.
- 12.6b built 2026-11-10: the console's Automation page (`/app/?view=automation`, `GET /api/v1/contracts/{id}/automation`): per cluster with an Actuator, each container its Actuator reported on in 90 days with its last Actuation, and its Automation (off, on, ended by Rollbacks until a date, stopped by a Revert until an approval), a Revert under way, and the Kill switch. Revert (`POST .../automation/revert`, from the console's own page) on a container whose last Actuation is applied or failed, through the same check as `revert_sizing` (moved to `webapp.revert`) and stored as `console:<login>`. The Rollback count behind "ended" is one helper for the Hub's desired state, its Slack line and this page (`actuator.rollbacks_of`, `resumes`). 14 mutations caught.
- 12.7 built 2026-11-11: the Contract's `actuator:` block: `eviction` (off), `timezone` (IANA, UTC when left out) and `windows` (`days`, `start`, `end` as HH:MM, `24:00` allowed, an end at or before its start running past midnight, the night belonging to the day it starts); refused: eviction without a window or without a cluster that has an `actuator_token_secret`, an unknown timezone, a window from a time to itself. `/actuator/desired` gains `eviction`: `off`, or on with the window `closed` or `open` now (`off` under the Kill switch). In the Actuator a pod whose resize is `Infeasible` is, with eviction off, failed as before; closed, waited on without a time limit (one `EvictionWaiting` Event), a newer Automation Sizing still replacing that rollout; open, evicted through the Eviction API (`create pods/eviction` added to its per-namespace ClusterRole; a 429 from a PodDisruptionBudget keeps it waiting, any other refusal fails it; a `PodEvicted` Event), one at a time across the cluster (never while a replacement is awaited) and at least 5 minutes apart (the last eviction's time kept in the ConfigMap, through new holds and catch-ups). Its webhook gives new pods the rollout's requests while an evicted pod's replacement is awaited. The replacement (a pod not there before the eviction) Ready with the requests stands for it (watched for a Rollback; an evicted canary's 10 minutes start again); one from its template (the webhook missed it) fails the rollout, never a second eviction; none Ready in 10 minutes is a Rollback (in `trouble()`, so it happens paused or with the Hub down too), and on any put back (Rollback or Revert) a replacement still `Pending` is evicted again, the hold let go first, so it starts from its template (a Pending pod's eviction ignores budgets). The rollout's Actuation says `<pod> evicted, its resize Infeasible`; no new Sizing replaces a hold while its replacement is awaited; a Deployment scaled down meanwhile (no new pod, as many live pods as it wants) owes none, and the rollout goes on. A Revert still needs an Actuation of the container, so a first rollout waiting for a window can't be reverted until it ends (a newer approval, the pause or the Kill switch stop it). From Kubernetes 1.35 the API server refuses at once a resize its node can never fit (403, "node didn't have enough allocatable resources") instead of the kubelet marking it Infeasible; found in the live check, the Actuator treats that refusal as Infeasible (evicted in a window; with eviction off, failed at once as before), and it keeps the Hub's last word on eviction for new rollouts. 60 mutations, 59 caught, one equivalent (a window from a time to itself is refused). Decided while building (2026-11-11, the Operator's choice, all recommended): an Infeasible pod outside a window waits for one; Automation evicts too; eviction without windows is refused; a replacement not Ready in 10 minutes is a Rollback rather than a pre-check of node room (which would need cluster-wide reads). `Deferred` (a node full now) is not evicted: it still fails after 10 minutes.
- 12.7b built 2026-11-11: never evicted, a pod of a Deployment with one replica (none set counts as one) or that no PodDisruptionBudget in its namespace selects (`matchLabels` and `matchExpressions`; a null selector selects nothing, an empty one everything); its rollout fails at once, window open or closed, as with eviction off, the Actuation saying why (`it has one replica` / `no PodDisruptionBudget covers it`, `so it is never evicted`). A failed read of the Deployment or its budgets fails it too, never evicts (an Actuator whose RBAC lacks the new `get, list poddisruptionbudgets` evicts nothing). 21 mutations, 20 caught, one equivalent (`In` on a missing key). Decided 2026-11-11, the Operator's choice: such a Deployment is never evicted. Not checked live: the eviction path itself is unchanged from 12.7's live check.
- 12.7 checked live 2026-11-11 on a throwaway kind cluster (Kubernetes 1.37, a worker with 3 CPUs allocatable and one with 10), the working tree's image, its Actuator pulling from a stand-in Hub on the Mac serving `/actuator/desired` from a file (the Hub's side is covered by its tests; no model calls, no cloud): `api` (2 pods at 500m on the small worker, a PDB) approved at 4 cores. Found first: the API server refused the canary's resize with 403 "node didn't have enough allocatable resources" where 1.33 marked it Infeasible, so the rollout failed; fixed in 12.7 before its commit (above). Then: window closed, the rollout waited (`EvictionWaiting`); window open with the PDB at `minAvailable: 2`, the eviction was refused and it waited (`EvictionWaiting`, a PodDisruptionBudget allows no eviction now), pods untouched; the PDB at 1, it evicted the canary (`PodEvicted`). That replacement started from its template, because the check's webhook still selected the manifests' namespace `demo` (the setup's mistake, not the Actuator's): the rollout failed once ("started from its template") and evicted nothing more. With the webhook on `shop` and a new approval, it waited out the 5 minutes since that eviction, evicted the canary, whose replacement started on the big worker at 4 cores allocated and Ready; after the canary's 10 minutes again the other pod was resized in place, `SizingApplied` "2 of 2 pods resized in place, one at a time; <pod> evicted, its resize Infeasible", Actuation `applied`, no restarts. With the big worker filled (8 cores), a new approval's evicted canary left its replacement Pending at 4 cores; 10 minutes on, a Rollback: `SizingRolledBack` "evicted: no replacement Ready in 10 minutes ... 1 Pending replacement evicted to start from its template", the replacement evicted past the PDB (Pending), back at the template's 499m and Running, no new Sizing for 24 h. Removed after: the cluster, the stand-in Hub, the image.
- 12.8 grilled 2026-11-11 (the Operator's choice, all recommended): three slices, 12.8a StatefulSets and DaemonSets, 12.8b Argo Rollouts, 12.8c CronJobs through the webhook only; a StatefulSet's pod is never evicted (it keeps its state, and a zonal volume would pin its replacement to a zone that may have no room); a DaemonSet's new pod gets from the webhook only requests lower than its template's, a raise reaching it in place where its node has room, so no node is left without its daemon; a CronJob's held requests are watched over its first run under them, until it ends (7 days at most), a pod of it OOM-killed or Failed being a Rollback; a Job of no CronJob is skipped (it runs once).
- 12.8a built 2026-11-11: the Actuator resizes StatefulSets' and DaemonSets' pods as Deployments' (pre-checks, canary, one at a time, rollback, hand-back, catch-up, Revert): their pods found by their owner's uid, VPAs and HPAs counted only when they target that kind, the pause annotation read per kind (`ns/kind/name`, so a Deployment and a StatefulSet of one name stay apart). Neither kind's pod is ever evicted (`a StatefulSet's pod is never evicted`). Its webhook tells a pod's owner from its `ownerReferences` (a StatefulSet or DaemonSet; else a ReplicaSet with the `pod-template-hash` suffix, a Deployment's) and gives a DaemonSet's new pod only the held requests below its template's. The Actuator names its kinds on each pull (`kinds=Deployment,StatefulSet,DaemonSet`) and `/actuator/desired` sends only Approved Sizings, Automation's and Reverts of those it also knows (`actuator.KINDS`); one naming none gets Deployments only, since an Actuator of 0.43.0 or older reads every Sizing as a Deployment's and would resize a Deployment of the same name (found in review: the first cut sent them to every Actuator). Its RBAC reads `statefulsets` and `daemonsets`. A DaemonSet's pod whose node has no room (its resize `Infeasible`, refused at once on 1.35+, or `Deferred` for 10 minutes), in a rollout or a catch-up, is left without its raise: its pending resize undone (back to what the kubelet allocated) but for the requests it lowers, set in place (found in the live check: the first cut left the lowering out too, the API server refusing the whole resize), a `PodLeft` Event, the Actuation's reason saying `<pod> left without its raise: its node has no room`, never tried again until a new Sizing (`left` in its hold, kept through catch-ups); a left canary hands the canary's 10 minutes to the next pod; only when every pod is left does the rollout fail (`no node of its N pods has room for them`). Decided 2026-11-11, the Operator's choice (B, recommended): on a DaemonSet a mix of node sizes is normal, so one small node must not end the Sizing for all (the first cut failed the whole hold, as for a Deployment, which also stopped the webhook's lowered requests). 59 mutations, 58 caught, one equivalent (a DaemonSet request equal to its template's, which the webhook leaves alone anyway); a Hub filter it made redundant was removed.
- 12.8a checked live 2026-11-11 on a throwaway kind cluster (Kubernetes 1.37, a worker with 3 CPUs allocatable and one with 10), the working tree's image, its Actuator pulling from a stand-in Hub on the Mac (no model calls, no cloud): every pull named `kinds=Deployment,StatefulSet,DaemonSet`. Approved: StatefulSet `db` (2 pods, 100m/64Mi) at 200m/96Mi, and DaemonSet `agent` (a pod on each worker, 500m/64Mi) at 4 cores/32Mi, a Deployment also named `db` beside them. `db` resized in place, canary then the other, `SizingApplied` "2 of 2 pods resized in place"; `agent`'s big-node pod resized to 4 cores, its small-node pod refused by the API server ("requested: 4000, allocatable: 3000") and left (`PodLeft`), `SizingApplied` "1 of 2 pods resized in place, one at a time; ... left"; the Deployment `db` untouched. Its webhook, pods deleted: the StatefulSet's new `db-1` started at 200m/96Mi; the big node's new DaemonSet pod at the template's 500m with the lowered 32Mi, its cpu then raised in place to 4 cores by the catch-up. The small node's new pod, created while that catch-up rolled (so from its template, as any pod made mid-rollout), was refused and left with the hold still applied (`left` in the ConfigMap) but kept 64Mi: the finding fixed above, after which a run 2 of the same Sizing left it at 500m with memory lowered in place to 32Mi ("lowered in place to memory 32Mi" in its `PodLeft`). No pod restarted. Removed after: the cluster, the stand-in Hub, the image.
- 12.8b built 2026-11-11: the Actuator resizes an Argo Rollout's pods as a Deployment's (pre-checks, canary, one at a time, rollback, hand-back, catch-up, Revert, the pause annotation): read through the custom objects API (`rollouts.argoproj.io`, get and list added to its RBAC, read only) and shaped as a Deployment, its template its own or, with `workloadRef`, its Deployment's (the Rollout's own selector first), its status left out; its pods those of the ReplicaSets it owns, stable and canary alike; HPAs and VPAs counted when they target a Rollout; Events on the Rollout (`argoproj.io/v1alpha1`). Never evicted (`a Rollout's pod is never evicted`): in place only, as grilled. Its webhook tells a Rollout's pod by Argo's `rollouts-pod-template-hash` label on a ReplicaSet named `<rollout>-<hash>`, so a Deployment and a Rollout of one name stay apart. The Actuator names `Rollout` on each pull; one of 0.44.0 names three kinds and gets no Rollout's Sizing. Argo Rollouts not installed: a Rollout's Sizing is skipped (`no such Rollout`); one it can't read as a Deployment (a `workloadRef` of another kind) fails (`reading the Rollout: 422: not read as a Deployment`), and the pause check lists Rollouts by their metadata only, so such a Rollout never hides another's pause (found in review). 20 mutations, 19 caught; the survivor is `main`'s wiring (its CustomObjectsApi), which the unit tests don't run (the live check below runs it).
- 12.8b checked live 2026-11-11 on a throwaway kind cluster (Kubernetes 1.37, Argo Rollouts v1.8.3), the working tree's image, its Actuator pulling from a stand-in Hub on the Mac (no model calls, no cloud). Argo's layout as the code assumed: ReplicaSets `<rollout>-<hash>` owned by the Rollout, pods labelled `rollouts-pod-template-hash` and no `pod-template-hash`. Every pull named `kinds=Deployment,StatefulSet,DaemonSet,Rollout`. Approved: Rollout `web` (2 pods, a canary strategy, 100m/64Mi) at 200m/96Mi beside a Deployment also named `web`, and Rollout `ref` (1 pod, `workloadRef` to a Deployment `base` at 300m scaled to zero) at 150m. Both resized in place, canary then the other, `SizingApplied` Events on the Rollouts (`argoproj.io/v1alpha1`) beside Argo's own, no restarts; the Deployment `web` untouched throughout. Pods deleted: their replacements started with the held requests (`ref`'s at 150m, its template asking 300m). An Argo canary update (a template annotation, requests unchanged): the canary ReplicaSet's pod started at 200m/96Mi during the 50% step, and after promotion both new pods held them, Argo `Healthy`, no hand-back. Paused (`squero-actuator/pause`): a replaced pod started from its template at 100m/64Mi; unpaused, caught up in place to 200m/96Mi. A person's change of `web`'s template cpu (100m to 120m) and of `base`'s (300m to 250m, through the `workloadRef`): both handed back (`HandedBack` on the Rollouts, Actuations `handed_back`), Argo's new pods at the person's values. Nothing found. Not exercised: eviction (off; a Rollout's pod is never evicted, unit-tested). Removed after: the cluster, the stand-in Hub, the image.
- 12.8c built 2026-11-11: a CronJob's Sizing, after the same pre-checks on its `jobTemplate` (a ResourceQuota counted for its `parallelism`), is held at once (`SizingApplied` "held for its next run's pods, given them as they start", Actuation `applied`, 0 pods); none of its pods is resized in place, none caught up, none evicted. Its webhook tells a run's pod by its Job's name, `<cronjob>-<digits>` as the CronJob controller names them (no API call, as for a Deployment); a lone Job's pod falls through and is refused. Its first run under the hold is watched (`first_run` in its hold, saved each minute): the first Job it owns created since whose pods got the requests (one whose pods all started from the template doesn't count), its pods listed by `batch.kubernetes.io/job-name` (Succeeded and Failed ones too); a pod of it with the held requests `Failed`, or OOM-killed (`state` with `restartPolicy: Never`, `last_state` with `OnFailure`), is a Rollback: the hold dropped, so the next run starts from its template, the usual backoff ("its next run's pods start from its template, not ..."); the Job finished or gone ends the watch, as do 7 days without a run. Revert and hand-back let it go the same way; Automation's churn rule holds. Events on the CronJob (`batch/v1`); `get, list` of `cronjobs` and `jobs` added to its RBAC. A Job of no CronJob is never sent ("a Job is not resized"). 34 mutations, 33 caught, one equivalent (a pod owned by a StatefulSet and a Job). Decided while building (the advisor's review, recommended defaults): `applied` is reported when held, not after the run; the watched run is the first whose pods got the requests; the Job-name match.
- 12.8c checked live 2026-11-11 on a throwaway kind cluster (Kubernetes 1.37), the working tree's image, its Actuator pulling from a stand-in Hub on the Mac (no model calls, no cloud); every pull named `CronJob`. Two CronJobs every minute (`restartPolicy: Never`, `backoffLimit: 0`): `nightly` (`sleep 5`, 100m/64Mi, limit 128Mi) approved at 200m/96Mi, `hungry` (`tail /dev/zero`, 100m/16Mi, limit 32Mi, OOM-killed from its template already) at 150m; and a lone Job's Sizing, which only a stand-in Hub would send. The CronJob controller named its Jobs `<cronjob>-<minute>` (`nightly-29907290`), as the webhook assumes. Both held at once (`SizingApplied` "held for its next run's pods" on the CronJobs, `batch/v1`, Actuations `applied`, 0 pods); the Job skipped ("a Job is not resized"). The next run's pods started from the webhook: `nightly` at 200m/96Mi, Completed, its watch ended and the hold kept (the run after at 200m/96Mi too); `hungry` at 150m, OOM-killed: `SizingRolledBack` "hungry-29907292-7bm7x: app was OOM-killed: its next run's pods start from its template, not cpu 150m; no new Sizing of it until ...", Actuation `rolled_back`, and its next run at its template's 100m. A person's change of `nightly`'s template cpu (100m to 120m): `HandedBack`, its next runs at 120m/64Mi. No error in its log. Nothing found. Removed after: the cluster, the stand-in Hub, the image.
- 12.9a built 2026-11-11 (the audit's five findings that can harm a Workload). (1) A catch-up or a new rollout counts restarts from when it begins for every pod that already has the requests (kept in its hold's `pods`), not from zero (those pods get no NotReady rule: that stays "a pod it resized"): before, once a hold's hour was over, a catch-up read any old restart of such a pod as new and rolled a healthy Sizing back (24 h of backoff, one of Automation's three); a pod still watched keeps its count. (2) A Rollback or Revert some pod refused is `unfinished` in its hold and tried again each minute (the Hub reachable or not, never under the Kill switch, kept through an approval skipped in its backoff) pod by pod, until none refuses with a failure that may pass (another refusal is the API server's verdict, said once in its log), the Workload is gone, or its template no longer asks what it did (a person's); it outlives a later Sizing that is skipped, and ends with one that rolls; before, it was recorded as done and that pod kept the requests for good. (3) The API clients are wrapped (`Calm`): a network failure (urllib3's, which the Kubernetes client lets through) is an ApiException (status 0) like a refusal, and each call has a timeout (15 s, tried once: the live check below); a minute that still fails saves what it changed and reports what it did before failing (so a Rollback reaches the Hub even with a bug each minute), and the process goes on to the next (before, it died before saving: a Sizing just rolled back could be applied again, its backoff lost). (4) Going to a rollout's next pod, a failure that may pass (status 0, 429, 5xx) is waited out for 10 minutes (`stalled`) instead of failing the rollout at once. (5) An approval skipped or failed leaves an earlier applied, rolling or failed hold as it is (`refused` marks the run, said once, "run N's requests stay held"): before, the hold was replaced by the skip, so new pods lost its requests and a Revert found nothing held while the pods kept them. 47 mutations, all caught. Found by its own test: a finished put back wasn't saved. Found in review: the retry stopped at the first pod that refused, a failed minute never reported, and the restart fix had widened the NotReady rule. `Calm` checked over a real client against a closed port (urllib3's MaxRetryError becomes status 0), and the integration test's resize and rollback go through it on kind. Not checked live otherwise (failure paths, unit-tested with refused and failing API calls). Until 12.9b, a network failure at the moment of an approval consumes it (`failed`, "checking it") where the process used to die and retry: 12.9a and 12.9b are released together.
- 12.9b built 2026-11-11 (the audit's narrower findings). (1) Its ConfigMap gains `state.json`: the Kill switch as the Hub last said it, and the reports not yet taken; read at start (what it can't read, another version's, dropped, never its holds with it), saved when either changes (a minute with reports writes it twice: with them before sending, without them once taken), the newest 200 reports only, the ConfigMap's megabyte being its holds' first. Before, a restart with the Hub unreachable took the Kill switch for off (its webhook patched pods, Rollbacks ran) and lost its unsent reports (a lost `reverted` left that container's Automation stopped on the Hub). (2) An approval, or a catch-up, that a passing failure meets before anything changed isn't used up: tried again each minute (`trying` in its hold, what it held before still held), 10 minutes at most, then `failed` as before; a catch-up just waits, its hold still applied. This closes what 12.9a opened (a network failure as an ApiException). (3) Approving again a Sizing whose container was Reverted since moves the approval's time past the Revert (`approve_sizing`: before, a no-op answered "already", the Revert staying the last word) and `/actuator/desired` marks an approval made after a Revert of its container `again`; the Actuator then applies it even if it is the run that Revert undid, which it still never does for an approval from before the Revert; a Sizing whose container has a Revert listed in the same pull waits (found in review: the Hub reads its Reverts and approvals in separate queries, and `again` is true after any earlier Revert, so a new Revert could arrive beside such an approval and be undone the same minute). An older Actuator ignores the flag. (4) A rollout whose Workload has no pods left ends `applied` and held, not `failed` ("no node of its 0 pods"). (5) Its webhook takes a Job for a CronJob's run only if the digits ending its name are a recent minute (5 minutes ahead to 30 days back): a lone Job named `x-123` no longer gets CronJob `x`'s hold. (6) The Automation page offers Revert by `revertable` (the container's last Actuation that isn't a skip applied or failed), since 12.9a a skipped approval leaves the hold in place. Also: the integration test `test_finds_all_three_failures` raced the demo's first OOM kill in CI (it failed once on 12.9a's push, the Actuator's own test passing through `Calm`); it now waits for it. 48 mutations, all caught, after a redundant condition one of them showed was removed; another Contract's or cluster's Revert never marks an approval `again` nor lets it be approved again (tested with both). Decided while building (recommended defaults): the `again` field on `/actuator/desired`, the 30-day window on a run's minute, zero pods ending `applied`, two ConfigMap writes in a minute with reports. Not checked live.
- 12.9a and 12.9b checked live 2026-11-11 on a throwaway kind cluster (Kubernetes 1.37), the working tree's image, its Actuator pulling from a stand-in Hub on the Mac that could refuse reports and be stopped (no model calls, no cloud).
