Step 1 built 2026-11-14: `make lab-remote` creates a kind cluster `lab` on another host over ssh, its API server and Prometheus on one private address only, its node capped (2.5 CPUs, 6 GiB) and restarting with Docker; `make lab LAB=small` installs the lab for a 4-CPU host (`demo/lab/small`, a kustomize overlay): the full lab asks 7.9 to 8.2 CPUs of its node with Kubernetes' own, the small one 3.3 to 3.6, the big Workloads asking and burning a quarter to a third, every ratio of request to load kept but `idle`'s (100 to 25), `weekly` and all memory unchanged. No address or host name is in the repository. Steps 2 to 6 done 2026-11-14 on a mini PC of the Operator's (4 cores, 15 GiB, Debian, running his own services): kind installed there, the cluster created with `make lab-remote` and the small lab installed with `make lab LAB=small` from the Mac. All 29 pods ran, none Pending; the node used 0.8 cores of its cap of 2.5 and 3.6 GiB of 6, with 3.07 CPUs requested of 4; `weekly`'s HPA read 26% of 50% at 2 replicas, as on the Mac; the host kept 9 GiB available and a load of 1.1. The working tree's hourly read against its Prometheus took a second and gave `weekly` a Need of 1.04. The Hub reads it with a read-only kubeconfig (`demo/lab/hub-read.yaml`: the shipped role in namespace `lab` only; secrets, ConfigMaps, writes and other namespaces refused, as `kubectl auth can-i` said) kept in the Contract's SOPS file, and demo-shop has it as cluster `homelab` beside `kind`. Step 7 checked 2026-11-14: the Mac Hub's (0.48.0) hourly run of 05:55 UTC read both clusters with no error and stored `homelab`'s 10 containers and `weekly`'s Need for the 05:00 hour, 1.04. Step 8 done 2026-11-14: the lab's Workloads and namespace deleted from the Mac's cluster, whose node went from 1.17 cores to 0.25; its `demo` and `orders` namespaces and its Prometheus stay. The Hub moved to the mini PC the same day (0.48.0, its battery cost on the Mac being the reason): its compose folder, settings, age key and contracts clone copied, the database restored from a final dump with every table's row count equal, the alert and Actuator ports on the private address only, the console and the MCP endpoint served by `tailscale serve` on their own port, Contract updates pulled with a read-only deploy key. Its first run there failed, `Prometheus unreachable: timed out`: a container cannot reach another's published port through the host's own address. Fixed as it was on the Mac: the Hub joins kind's Docker network and demo-shop names the node (`lab-control-plane`), in its Prometheus URL and in the read-only kubeconfig's server; the next run stored the lab's 10 containers, and the Hub listed the lab's pods and was refused its secrets. demo-shop's cluster on the Mac (`kind`, the Triage demo) is no longer a cluster of it: the demo's move to the mini PC's cluster is its own step. Then everything of the Hub and the kind cluster on the Mac was stopped, their data kept as the way back; only the test Postgres of the unit suite runs there. The Triage demo followed the same day (`make demo CLUSTER=lab`: its five Workloads broken as designed, the GitOps stand-ins), demo-shop's `homelab` gaining `demo`, `orders` and the two GitOps namespaces; and demo-bank, which had named a cluster on the Mac and never had data, became a real second Contract on the same cluster and Prometheus: a Workload `ledger` in namespace `bank` (`demo/lab/bank.yaml`) and an account of its own bound there only (`demo/lab/hub-read-demo.yaml`), its actions switched off, the account being read-only. Checked: a run of each Contract stored its own namespaces only (demo-shop `lab`, `demo`, `orders`; demo-bank `bank`), `kubectl auth can-i` and the Hub itself were refused the other Contract's pods (403), and the Hub MCP listed both with nothing stale. A Question through the Hub MCP then checked the moved demo end to end (one model call): asked why `checkout` crash loops, the agent read the rollout's change, the pod's log and its restarts with the Kubernetes tools and answered that the rollout had removed `DATABASE_URL`, citing two earlier Reports of the same. Not checked: a Triage from an alert. To watch: the node's memory against its cap as Prometheus fills, and the host's low inotify limit.
- *Done when:* backtests pre-scale before a synthetic Monday peak, and a request cut doesn't change HPA replica counts.

### Phase 15: Nodes (6 slices, grilled 2026-11-12)

- 15.1 What blocks scale-down (PDBs, `safe-to-evict=false`, local storage, ownerless and kube-system pods). 15.2 Placing unevictable pods together. 15.3 DaemonSet Sizing per node. 15.4 Consolidation recommendations. 15.5 Compaction with the descheduler. 15.6 Karpenter NodePool and Cluster Autoscaler proposals.
- Grilled 2026-11-12 (three rounds, all recommended; compaction by the Actuator, first chosen as a last slice, went to Deferred once the facts were in):
  - **Scope.** Read-only first, one write last: Placement through the webhook. Cluster Autoscaler and Karpenter both, named per cluster in the Contract (`cluster-autoscaler`, `karpenter` or none: their rules differ, and guessing from which metrics exist is a guess); a cluster with neither still gets the report ("a person could remove this node"). Computed by the Hub each hour and stored, as Sizings are; a new Nodes page in the console and one line in the weekly digest. The Triage agent's tools are unchanged: no eval rerun. No new permission in a client's cluster and no spend.
  - **Blockers (15.1).** Read from Prometheus (each pod's node, each node's room and taints, PodDisruptionBudgets' allowed disruptions, pods with no owner) plus what the Actuator already reads in its namespaces (pods and PodDisruptionBudgets): it sends the Hub, hourly, only the pods that have a Blocker, capped in size and checked as its reports are (12.9c). kube-state-metrics has no series for emptyDir or hostPath volumes or for which pods a PodDisruptionBudget covers, and pod annotations only when allowlisted, so a cluster without an Actuator gets what Prometheus has and "unknown" for the rest. Pods in namespaces outside the Actuator's (kube-system included) are counted per node as "not visible"; the report counts but never names a pod in a namespace outside the Contract. Cluster Autoscaler's Blockers are its documented list (a PodDisruptionBudget allowing no disruption, kube-system pods without one, a pod with no controller, local storage, `safe-to-evict: "false"`, a pod that fits nowhere else); Karpenter's are its `karpenter.sh/do-not-disrupt` annotation and PodDisruptionBudgets only (its docs don't say a pod with no controller blocks it). The verdict is the Hub's own, with the autoscaler's own count of unremovable nodes beside it where Prometheus has it (Cluster Autoscaler's `unremovable_nodes_count` labels its reasons with numbers in 1.30.7: translated, "unknown reason" for one the Hub doesn't know; dropped in 15.1c, once its source showed what the gauge is).
  - **Nodes that could go (15.4).** A requests-fit: a node with no Blocker whose pods fit in the free room of the others (tainted nodes only among themselves; a DaemonSet's pods aren't moved), an upper bound and shown as one ("up to 3 nodes"): affinity and spread rules can't be seen from Prometheus. Computed twice: with today's requests, and with the requests after the Sizings, which says which nodes go if the Sizings are applied (a Sizing frees capacity, but money is saved only when a node leaves). Its own figure with its monthly cost, never summed with Savings: after the Sizings it is partly the same money.
  - **Proposals (15.6).** Per Blocker, the change that removes it, ready to copy (a PodDisruptionBudget for a Workload with none, the annotation that lets a local volume be dropped, and so on), and the autoscaler's setting to check, named but not compared: the Hub can't read it (kube-system, or objects of no namespace). The Operator turns one into a pull request with the Hub MCP server's existing tool (changed in 15.6: that tool edits only a Contract's Sizing policy, so the change is to copy). The descheduler isn't proposed: its `HighNodeUtilization` must be paired with a scheduler scoring `MostAllocated`, and the default scores `LeastAllocated`.
  - **DaemonSet Sizing per node (15.3).** One Sizing per group of nodes of the same instance type (the label the Hub already reads for list prices) instead of one pooled over every node, which fits the busiest and is too large on the rest. The Actuator can't read a node, so the Hub sends each group's node names with its Sizing and the Actuator applies each pod's by its node, in place; a new pod carries its target node from its creation (the DaemonSet controller's node affinity), so the webhook gives it its group's requests when lower than its template's, as today's rule has it. A pod on a node the Hub hasn't seen yet keeps the template's requests until the next run names its node. One approval covers all of a DaemonSet's groups; a group with too little history is still learning.
  - **The node-capacity cap** (parked here since 9.1): a Sizing's request is never above the largest node's room after its DaemonSets, with the risk "needs a larger node".
  - **Placement (15.2).** The webhook adds a preference, never a requirement (a required rule can leave a pod Pending), for nodes with a label the client names in the Contract (`actuator: placement`, absent: off), such as a small node pool kept for pods that can't move; the report treats those nodes as where Blockers belong and never proposes removing them. No pod-to-pod affinity (the Kubernetes docs advise against it above a few hundred nodes). For every Workload the report names as blocking, in the Actuator's namespaces, which the Hub lists for it as it does Sizings; the pause annotation and the Kill switch stop it. A running pod's affinity can't be changed and an unevictable pod is one nothing may evict, so pods already running move only at their next rollout or restart: the report says which Workload to restart to free which node, and the Actuator never evicts for it. With Karpenter a preference is treated as required first and relaxed when it can't be met, so the label should be one a NodePool carries. An ADR is written with it: the first time the Actuator influences where a pod runs. Grilled further 2026-11-15 (two rounds, all recommended): the label is per cluster (`placement: "pool=pinned"` on a cluster with an `actuator_token_secret`, one `key=value`; node labels differ per cluster, as `autoscaler` is set), with an optional taint whose toleration the webhook adds to placed pods only (a toleration permits a node and requires nothing; an untainted pool fills with pods that can move). A Workload is placed for a Blocker fixed in its pods' spec only: `safe-to-evict: "false"`, `do-not-disrupt: "true"`, local storage or emptyDir, a custom controller; never for a PodDisruptionBudget allowing no disruption or a timed `do-not-disrupt` (both come and go, and would place a Workload mid-rollout; 15.6 already says how to loosen the budget), nor a pod with no controller (no Workload to restart) or a DaemonSet's. The Contract's field is the consent, as for Reflexes: no approval per Workload; each placed pod gets an Event. The webhook appends one preferred node affinity term of weight 100 at CREATE, never replacing the pod's own, nothing where it already prefers that label. A *Pinned node* (a node with the label) is never one that could go nor a destination in 15.4's requests-fit; its Blockers are shown as expected, with no 15.6 proposal, its verdict `pinned`. The report names "restart X to free node N" only when every Blocker on N is a placed Workload's pod and the pinned nodes' free room fits them all; otherwise it says what still holds N ("pod Y", or "the pinned nodes lack room: about 1.2 cores and 3 GiB short"). A placed pod that started elsewhere is marked "placed, on N: the pinned nodes had no room when it started". Shown on the Nodes page and by the Hub MCP's `nodes`; the digest's Nodes line is unchanged.
  - **Compaction (15.5): Deferred.** The default scheduler spreads pods, so one evicted to empty a node tends to land back unless steered away; Karpenter consolidates by itself and Cluster Autoscaler drains a node under its threshold (0.5 by default, the client's setting), which the proposals name; and it would be the first time the Actuator evicts healthy pods to save money.
  - **Terms.** *Blocker*: what keeps a node from being removed. *Placement*: the webhook's preference for the nodes the client named.
  - **Slices.** 15.1a: each pod's node and each node's room and taints, the node-capacity cap, the autoscaler in the Contract, the Nodes page. 15.1b: the Actuator's Blockers, stored and shown per node; live check on a multi-node kind cluster. 15.1c (added 2026-11-14): the fallback from Prometheus for a cluster without an Actuator (the autoscaler's own count of unremovable nodes, first part of it, was dropped). 15.4: nodes that could go, now and after the Sizings, priced, and the digest line. 15.6: the proposals. 15.3: DaemonSet Sizings per group of nodes (15.3a the Hub's side, 15.3b the Actuator's, 15.3c the money by type: split 2026-11-14; 15.3c built 2026-11-15). 15.2: Placement (split 2026-11-15: 15.2a the Hub's side, the Contract field, the list sent to the Actuator, the report's Pinned nodes and restart advice, the Nodes page; 15.2b the Actuator's webhook, with its ADR); live check on multi-node kind, the phase's done-when (the named Workload restarted by hand). Karpenter's rules are tested from its documented behaviour and checked live in Phase 16's sandbox.
  - Looked up for it (2026-11-12; Kubernetes, Karpenter, descheduler and kube-state-metrics docs, Cluster Autoscaler's FAQ and 1.30.7 source): the Blocker lists above; Cluster Autoscaler's `skip-nodes-with-local-storage`, `-system-pods` and `-custom-controller-pods` all default to true; Karpenter's `consolidationPolicy` (`WhenEmptyOrUnderutilized` by default), `consolidateAfter` (0s), budgets (10% of nodes) and its `Unconsolidatable` Event on the Node; a pod's updatable fields don't include `affinity` or `nodeSelector`; a preferred affinity still schedules the pod when no node matches; kind takes worker nodes in its config. 12.8a's live check had left two pods of one DaemonSet at different sizes with nothing undoing it.
- 15.1a grilled 2026-11-14 (all recommended): a node's Room counts every pod on it, the Contract's own by name and the rest as one sum and one count per node, never named or split by namespace (so on a cluster two Contracts share, each sees the total the other asks per node); the cap is per container, and a pod whose containers together ask more gets the risk with nothing cut in proportion; the largest Room is over every node not cordoned, tainted ones included (too generous changes nothing, too strict shrinks a correct Sizing); no Hub MCP tool until 15.4 gives something to act on.
- 15.1a built 2026-11-14, not checked live: the glossary's Room first. `usage.rooms` reads each cluster's nodes as they are at the run (allocatable, taints, cordoned, instance type) and their pods' requests, Running or Pending only, joined on the pod's `uid` (checked read-only on the lab's Prometheus: every pod series carries it, 34 of its 36 pods were live, and grouping by a label that isn't there is no error); pods outside the Contract's namespaces are summed and counted by Prometheus, so none reaches the Hub by name. `sizing.largest_room` and a `room` in `sizing.size` cap each Sizing with the risk `needs_larger_node`; `sizing.rows` adds the pod-level risk. Stored per cluster, the latest only (`cluster_rooms`, migration 0037); `GET /api/v1/contracts/{id}/nodes` and the console's Nodes page; `clusters[].autoscaler` (`cluster-autoscaler` or `karpenter`), shown and nothing more yet. The Triage agent's tools are unchanged (a capped Sizing's risk is text in its `sizing` tool's output, not a change of its schema): no eval rerun. Chosen beyond the grill: the guard list prices have is now one check for every read of node series (a store shared with another Contract without cluster labels reads none of this, so the lab on the mini PC, where both demo Contracts read one unlabelled Prometheus, shows the refusal and not its node); the cap never lowers a request (a node cordoned or missing from the read would otherwise turn it into a cut that Automation applies); a cluster read in part has no cap; a DaemonSet's own Sizing is exempt; CPU's and memory's largest Room may be of different nodes; the Contract's own pods come from `kube_pod_info`, so one asking nothing is not counted as not visible; init containers left out; a failed read of the nodes is stored as the cluster's problem and stops nothing; a container kept above the largest Room says so ("kept at 2000m, though no node has room for more than 1000m"), not that it was capped; the pod-level risk goes on the Workload's containers that have a Sizing, not on one still learning; a cluster behind a Relay is read the same way through its Relay, which passes the Hub's queries on as before (the Relay test's rule "its namespaces only" now names the exception: node series and per-node sums and counts). The fake Prometheus of the tests now evaluates the `uid` join, `== 1` and an outer `sum by` or `count by`, so the tests see what would leave Prometheus. Mutation check: 64 mutations, all killed after five tests were sharpened; one more showed a redundant join on the Contract's own requests, which was removed. Not checked live: any of it; its live check is 15.1b's, on a multi-node kind cluster with one Contract (decided 2026-11-14).
- 15.1b grilled 2026-11-14 (all recommended): 15.1b holds only the Actuator's path (its hourly report, stored, the Hub's verdict per node on the Nodes page), and a 15.1c adds the fallback from Prometheus for a cluster without an Actuator (pods with no owner, PodDisruptionBudgets at 0) and the autoscaler's own count of unremovable nodes; a cluster that names no autoscaler is judged by what stops `kubectl drain` without force flags (a budget at 0, a pod with no controller, emptyDir data); a node with no known Blocker but pods the Hub can't see (outside the Contract's namespaces, not a DaemonSet's) is "unknown: N pods not visible", so 15.4 never counts it as one that could go.
- 15.1b built 2026-11-14, not checked live: the glossary's Blocker first. `/actuator/desired` gains `blockers`, the cluster's namespaces, under the Kill switch too (a read is no change; absent from an older Hub, and then nothing is reported). The Actuator, hourly and last in its minute in its own try (a failure there costs no Rollback), lists pods and PodDisruptionBudgets in those namespaces a page of 200 at a time (its memory limit is 256Mi) and posts `blockers.Report` to `POST /actuator/blockers`: facts per pod and no verdict, the namespaces it read in full, and whether it was cut at 1000 pods. No new permission (`deploy/actuator/rbac.yaml` untouched). The Hub refuses whole a report over the cap (413), naming a namespace outside the cluster's, or carrying a value no Actuator sends (422), and stores the cluster's latest dated by its own clock (`cluster_blockers`, migration 0038). `blockers.reasons` holds the rules; `blockers.verdicts` gives each node blocked, clear or unknown; `usage.rooms` counts per node the pods that stay with it (a DaemonSet's and static pods, one more sum by Prometheus over all namespaces: the isolation and Relay tests of 15.1a pass unchanged). Shown on the Nodes page; the API's node gains `verdict`. The Triage agent's tools are unchanged: no eval rerun. Looked up for it (2026-11-14, a research pass over primary sources): Cluster Autoscaler's FAQ (the list of pods that prevent removal; local storage is a hostPath or an emptyDir not in memory; `safe-to-evict: "true"` lifts the kube-system, no-controller and local-storage rules and is not said to lift a budget; `safe-to-evict-local-volumes` must name every local volume); `kubectl drain`'s reference (refuses pods "neither mirror pods nor managed by a replication controller, replica set, daemon set, stateful set, or job" without `--force`, and pods using emptyDir without `--delete-emptydir-data`; hostPath not mentioned; evictions respect budgets); Karpenter's disruption page, from its raw source (the pod annotation: "true" for permanent protection, a Go duration like "30m" for that long after the pod starts running, an invalid one ignored; blocking budgets, and every budget of a pod must allow; the same annotation on a Node; nothing on pods without a controller or with local storage); Cluster Autoscaler's drainability source could not be reached, so its rules follow the FAQ alone. Chosen beyond the grill: facts not conclusions, so the rules can change without redeploying an Actuator; a custom controller's pod blocks under Cluster Autoscaler (its flag's default) and `kubectl drain`; Karpenter's annotation blocks as "true", and as a duration until that long after the pod started (its start sent as a fact; one whose start isn't known still blocks), any other value ignored as Karpenter ignores it; a report is also cut by size, under the megabyte the Hub's proxy lets through, and the proxy's list of the Actuator's routes gained this one; a budget whose status isn't written counts as allowing none; clear needs positive evidence: stale after two hours by the Hub's clock, a cut report, an unread namespace, hidden pods, a node read by a 15.1a run, each unknown; a pod of a namespace the Contract has since left is not shown; a report that didn't reach the Hub goes again in five minutes, one refused waits the hour. Mutation check: 108 mutations, 107 killed after six tests were sharpened; one equivalent (a blocked node has no reason for being unknown, so which is asked first can't matter). An integration test reads two namespaces a pod a page through the real client (it runs in CI's kind job, not locally). Limits: Karpenter's annotation on a Node is not read; Cluster Autoscaler's flags are taken at their defaults; no verdict without an Actuator until 15.1c. Not checked live.
- 15.1a and 15.1b checked live 2026-11-14 18:30 to 18:55 UTC on a throwaway kind cluster on the Mac (v1.37, a control plane and three workers, one cordoned; kube-prometheus-stack with the lab's values; a namespace `shop` as the Contract's, with a pod per Blocker fact, and a namespace `other` outside it), with one Contract, no Hub process and no model calls; cluster, database and credentials deleted after. Room: for all four nodes the requests summed, the pods counted and the pods that stay with the node (DaemonSets' and the control plane's static pods: 8 of its 11) were equal to the API server's own pods, the Contract's 18 live pods listed by name, a finished Job's pod (500m) in neither, and no pod of `other` or `monitoring` named. The cordoned node read as cordoned with its taint and was left out of the largest Room (9.88 cores, 31.2 GiB after 120m of DaemonSets); a synthetic container using more was capped there with the risk, and the stored run's 8 real Sizings were not (no real pod can use more than a node has, so the cap itself is only shown on synthetic usage). The Actuator's read, as a ServiceAccount bound to its ClusterRole in `shop` only and through the real client: 19 pods in four pages of 5 (`PAGE` set to 5 for the check; `_continue` works), `other` refused with a 403 and left out of the namespaces read, 5 pods sent with their facts (a budget at 0 on both `web` pods, the emptyDir volumes with the one in memory marked, both annotations, no controller). The Hub, in process on a throwaway database after one stored run: `/actuator/desired` named `shop`, the report was taken (204; 401 with a wrong token), and the verdicts were as the rules say under each of the three: the two workers with a `web` pod blocked by its budget under all, `solo` by having no controller under `kubectl drain`'s and by its annotation under Cluster Autoscaler's, the emptyDir pod under those two (the in-memory volume only under drain's), the `do-not-disrupt` pod under Karpenter's alone; the control plane unknown ("3 pods not visible": CoreDNS and the storage provisioner); the cordoned worker, holding one Deployment's pod and DaemonSet pods, clear; and all four unknown two hours and a minute later. CI's kind job also ran the paged read through the real client. Not checked live: a deployed Actuator sending on its own hourly clock, the Nodes page in a browser against a running Hub, the route through a running Hub's Caddy beyond the smoke test, a report cut at 1000 pods, a cluster behind a Relay, and what Cluster Autoscaler or Karpenter themselves do with these pods (the rules are from their documentation).
- 15.1c grilled 2026-11-14 (all recommended): Cluster Autoscaler's own count shows as its total and its counts per reason number as they are, untranslated (changed from the phase's grill, which had them translated: its source labels each reason with the number of a list that grew between releases, so a number means different things on different versions, and the Hub can't tell the version); a pod with no controller seen only through Prometheus is blocked under `kubectl drain`'s and Cluster Autoscaler's rules, the latter saying "unless annotated safe-to-evict: true, which only an Actuator can see"; the fallback applies whenever there is no usable report (no Actuator, none yet, one older than two hours).
- 15.1c built 2026-11-14, not checked live: `usage.rooms` gives each of the Contract's pods its controller's kind (first from the `kube_pod_info` it already read; from `kube_pod_owner` since the live check, which showed that a pod with no controller has no `created_by_kind` label at all, so the fallback never fired on a real cluster), and reads the PodDisruptionBudgets of its namespaces that allow no disruption; both stored with the cluster's nodes, no migration. `blockers.seen` gives a node's verdict without a usable report: blocked by a pod with no controller or a custom one (not under Karpenter's rules), else unknown, never clear, with a budget at 0 of its pods' namespaces named, since Prometheus doesn't say which pods it covers. Looked up for it (2026-11-14, Cluster Autoscaler's source at tags 1.30.7 and 1.34.1): the gauge's label `reason` is set with `fmt.Sprintf("%v", reason)` on an int type with no String method found, so it is the number; 1.34.1 has two reasons more, inserted after the seventh, so every later number is 2 higher ("blocked by a pod": 13, then 15). Found while building, in its source at the same tags (`core/static_autoscaler.go`): the gauge is set from a map that holds only the reasons with nodes in that loop, so a reason that fell to zero keeps its last value, and not at all while scale-down is in cooldown (nor, in 1.34.1, without candidates); and its reasons include nodes simply in use. So it is no cross-check of the Hub's blocked nodes, as the grill's answer had assumed: put back to the Operator, who dropped it (2026-11-14); the read, the stored field and the page's line were built and then removed, and the guide names why under Limits. Chosen beyond the grill: without a usable report a node is never clear; a pod whose controller's kind kube-state-metrics doesn't give is not known, not taken for one with none; a pod read before 15.1c, whose kind isn't stored, blocks nothing; a fresh report is believed over Prometheus (it sees the annotation that lifts a rule). Mutation check: 22 mutations, all killed. Not checked live: the budget series on a real Prometheus (the lab has no PodDisruptionBudget).
- 15.4 built 2026-11-14, not checked live, on the phase's grill (no new round: its answers and 15.1a's "the Hub MCP tool comes with 15.4" and 15.1b's "an unknown node is never counted" covered it): the glossary's Node that could go first. `usage.rooms` also reads each node's capacity and pod slots, and gives each of the Contract's pods its containers' requests one by one and the Workload its owner series resolve to (`resolve`, as the Sizings name it). `blockers.could_go`: a clear node, not a control-plane one, each of whose moving pods fits whole (CPU, memory, a slot) on one other node of its taint group that isn't cordoned; fewest requests first, largest pod first, first node by name; `blockers.after` gives the same read with the ready Sizings applied container by container. `sizing.nodes_report` prices the nodes at their capacity and counts the verdicts; `sizing.nodes_line` is the digest's line; `webapp.nodes_of` serves the page and the Hub MCP's new `nodes` tool (blocked nodes first, `top` of them, a node's blockers capped at 5). No migration. The Triage agent's tools are unchanged: no eval rerun. Changed from the grilling: computed when asked instead of each hour and stored, so it always agrees with the verdicts beside it, which depend on the Actuator's report and its age (measured: 300 nodes of 30 pods, both scenarios, 0.18 s). Chosen beyond the grill: every gap means the node stays (a node without its allocatable or its slots neither goes nor receives; a read from before 15.4 says nothing); a pod fits pod by pod, not by a sum; the taints Kubernetes sets itself and `PreferNoSchedule` are no group, so a cordoned node's pods can move; a cordoned node may go and receives nothing, nor does one under a condition taint (not ready, under pressure); a control-plane node never goes (clear and empty, it would head the list); a node that received pods and then goes must place those too (a bug its test caught while building); after the Sizings a container without a ready Sizing, or of a pod whose Workload isn't known, keeps its own request, one that asks nothing today (so the pod doesn't list it) asks its Sizing, and held Sizings and raises count as applied; priced at capacity (as the Bill check counts nodes), allocatable where it wasn't read; the digest's line only when a node could go, with the nodes not judged counted in it; the `nodes` tool being the first path from an Actuator's strings to a model, a report whose namespace, pod, node or budget name isn't shaped like a Kubernetes name is refused on arrival, and a kind or a volume name that isn't is counted, not repeated. Mutation check: 80 mutations, all killed after twelve tests were sharpened. Limits: an upper bound (affinity, spread, tolerations and the autoscaler's settings unseen); the Hub's own unit prices, not a node pool's actual price. Not checked live.
- 15.1c and 15.4 checked live 2026-11-14 20:35 to 21:10 UTC on a throwaway kind cluster on the Mac (as 15.1b's: a control plane and three workers, one cordoned; the same Workloads in `shop`), one Contract with typed prices, no Hub process and no model calls; cluster, database and credentials deleted after. The read: for all four nodes the capacity and pod slots, and for each of the Contract's pods its Workload (through the owner series: `Deployment/web` behind its ReplicaSet, `DaemonSet/agent`, `Pod/solo`) and its containers' requests, were equal to the API server's; the budget `web` of `shop` read as allowing no disruption. Found by it: kube-state-metrics gives a pod with no controller no `created_by_kind` on `kube_pod_info` (the label is simply absent; the tests' fixture had it as `<none>`), so without an Actuator `solo` was unknown, not blocked: fixed by taking each pod's controller from `kube_pod_owner`, which says `<none>` outright, the fixture changed to what the cluster showed; after it the worker with `solo` was blocked under `kubectl drain`'s and Cluster Autoscaler's rules (the latter with its "unless annotated"), unknown under Karpenter's, and the other workers unknown with the budget named. The nodes that could go, with the Actuator's report (read as a ServiceAccount bound in `shop`) posted to the Hub in process: one clear node (the cordoned worker, holding one Deployment's pod), two blocked, the control plane unknown; it could go now, priced 406.21 EUR a month, equal by hand to its capacity (10 cores, 31.29 GiB) at the Contract's prices; the stored digest carried the line with "1 not judged". Then a pod asking 9.6 cores was put on that worker: nothing could go now (the others have 9.44 and 9.39 cores of Room), the digest had no line, and with a made-up ready Sizing of that Workload at 100m it could go after the Sizings. The Hub MCP's cluster view listed the blocked nodes first with `more_nodes` counted. Not checked live: Sizings from real usage changing the list (the cluster's containers were all still learning, so that Sizing was synthetic); several taint groups; masking through the MCP itself; the page in a browser.
- 15.6 grilled 2026-11-14 (one question, the recommended answer): a Blocker someone set on purpose (a PodDisruptionBudget allowing no disruption, `safe-to-evict: "false"`, `do-not-disrupt`) gets words and no manifest, since a budget at 0 is also a live status (a pod that is down) and a manifest that removes a protection is easy to paste mid-incident; the others get a manifest.
- 15.6 built 2026-11-14, not checked live: the glossary's Blocker gains "the change that removes it" (not "proposal", which is `propose_change`'s pull request). `blockers._found` gives each reason with its rule, so `reasons` and `changes` come from one source; `blockers.changes` gives, per reason, the change in words with what it costs and a manifest where it adds something (the annotation `safe-to-evict: "true"` for a pod with no controller or a custom one under Cluster Autoscaler, `safe-to-evict-local-volumes` naming every local volume, a PodDisruptionBudget for a kube-system pod), and under `kubectl drain`'s rules the flag that does it and what it deletes; `blockers.SETTINGS` names the autoscaler's own settings to check. On each blocking pod of a verdict (with its Workload, from the Hub's read), on the Nodes page ("To remove it"), and in the Hub MCP's `nodes`, where a Workload's replicas blocking for the same reasons are one entry. No migration, no new fact in the Actuator's report (an older Hub keeps taking a newer Actuator's). The Triage agent's tools are unchanged: no eval rerun. Changed from the grilling: "the Operator turns one into a pull request with the Hub MCP server's existing tool" does not hold, `propose_change` editing only a Contract's Sizing policy in the contracts repo: the change is to copy, and the tool's description says no tool writes it anywhere (writing to a client's repo would be a decision of its own). Chosen beyond the grill: what it costs is in the manifest's comments, with what changing a pod template does (it restarts every pod of the Workload; a CronJob's reaches only later Jobs; a Job's can't be changed); an annotation goes on the Workload's pod template where the read resolved a kind that has one (a CronJob's under `jobTemplate`), else on the pod, said in it; a pod with no controller is annotated itself, having no template; the kube-system budget's selector is a placeholder label that selects no pod until replaced, never an empty selector (which in policy/v1 covers the namespace); the local-volumes annotation names every local volume, also those a partial annotation had; a pod's, a Workload's or a volume's name not shaped like a Kubernetes name never enters a manifest, also on the path without a report, where they are Prometheus' labels; for a Contract that masks, the Hub MCP gives a manifest with placeholders and the page has the real names; a duration of `do-not-disrupt` needs no change; the settings carry their documented defaults (looked up 2026-11-12), not the cluster's. Each manifest is tested as a round trip (applied to the pod's facts, the reason is gone) and as YAML. Mutation check: 50 mutations, all killed. Not checked live: no manifest has met an API server.
- 15.6 checked live 2026-11-14 22:25 to 22:50 UTC on a throwaway kind cluster on the Mac (v1.37, as before, plus a bare pod, a pod owned by a ConfigMap as its controller, a CronJob with an emptyDir, and the Actuator's ServiceAccount bound in `kube-system` too), a Contract naming `cluster-autoscaler` over `shop` and `kube-system`, no Hub process and no model calls; cluster and credentials deleted after. From the read and the Actuator's report the Hub made five manifests and words for two more (the budget at 0 on `web`, `safe-to-evict: "false"` on a pod). The API server took all five as server-side dry runs, each on the object its comment names: the local-volumes annotation as a merge patch of the Deployment's pod template and of the CronJob's `jobTemplate`, `safe-to-evict: "true"` as a patch of the bare pod and of the custom controller's pod, and the PodDisruptionBudget for CoreDNS as an apply (with its placeholder selector, which is a valid label). Three were then applied for real (the budget with its selector set to CoreDNS's label, after which it allowed 1 disruption; the Deployment, which rolled out; the bare pod): the next report still carried those pods' facts and the Hub no longer gave them a reason, the four not applied remaining. A Job's template was refused by the API server as immutable, as the manifest's comment says. Found by it: "from its pod template" was said of a bare pod too, now "where it is set (its Workload's pod template, or the pod itself)". Not checked live: a StatefulSet's and a DaemonSet's template, a Contract that masks, the page in a browser, and what Cluster Autoscaler itself then does (none ran there).
- 15.3 grilled 2026-11-14 (all recommended): two slices, 15.3a the Hub's side (the Sizings per instance type computed, stored inside the container's row and shown, the Actuator still applying the pooled one) and 15.3b the Actuator's (told each type's nodes and requests, resizing each pod by its node, its webhook giving a new pod its type's); only on clusters that run an Actuator, where Waste counts each type against its own Sizing, since one template can't be sized per type by hand; nodes without an instance-type label are one type, which is today's pooled Sizing.
- 15.3b grilled 2026-11-14 (all recommended): three slices in all, 15.3b the Actuator applying each type's Sizing by a pod's node and 15.3c the money by type (Waste, the cost of requests, realized Savings), which until then stay on the pooled Sizing; a pod whose type has no Sizing yet (learning, or its node's type not known) gets its template's requests, not the pooled Sizing.
- 15.3b built 2026-11-14. The Hub: `Wanted.types` (each ready type's requests, none where it keeps one, `sizing.by_type`) and `Desired.node_types` (node to type, `usage.node_types` over the longest window, stored with the cluster's rooms, behind the guard of every node read and only for a cluster with an Actuator), both only to an Actuator that says `types=1` on its pull; an approval stores the types of its run (`approved_sizings.types`, migration 0039); a row goes to such an Actuator when some type changes a request, to an older one when the pooled Sizing does. The Actuator: a hold with `types`, every look at a pod through `wants` (the pod's node's type's, its template's for a type with none, nothing while it knows no node's type) and `at_hold` (any type's), so the canary, the rollout, catch-ups, the watch, Rollback and Revert follow; `apply` builds one set per type (the limit's cap and Automation's churn rule each type's own, a type not in the new Sizing keeping what it holds, one first sized starting from the pooled hold where there was one), runs the QoS and LimitRange checks for each type and the quota for the most any type asks, and skips, tried again, while it has no node's type; the webhook reads a new pod's node from its node affinity (`node_of`; the shape read from a real DaemonSet pod on the lab's kind cluster, v1.37); a Step-up takes every type's memory and the lowered-memory Rollback rule looks at every type. Chosen beyond the grill: the Hub sends the nodes' types because the Actuator has no access to Nodes and gets none; an Actuation of a hold with types carries no `cpu` or `memory` and names the types in its reason, three at most; `approve_sizing` refuses a container with more types than the ten `sizings` shows (never approved unseen), which Automation still applies; a quota is checked conservatively (every pod at the most any type asks), so it can skip a container an exact sum would pass; the nodes' types are not kept across an Actuator's restart (the Hub sends them with every answer to an Actuator that knows types, whatever Sizings go with it, so a node that joins later and a restarted Actuator are caught up). An Event of such an Actuation says "requests by instance type" where it said the numbers. Tests for every branch, 57 mutations all caught, two Contracts' nodes kept apart; no model-visible change but `approve_sizing`'s description and answer (no Scenario uses it: no eval rerun). Open, for 15.3c: once the types are applied the pooled row can go on saying "lower" (its pods' requests now differ by type), so its Waste shows money no approval reaches and `approve_sizing` answers "approved" for a container whose types change nothing, which an Actuator that knows types is never sent.
- 15.3b, and 15.3a's Sizings per type, checked live 2026-11-15 00:58 to 02:43 UTC on a throwaway kind cluster on the Mac (v1.37, a control plane and three workers given the instance types `m5.large`, `m5.4xlarge` and `c5.large` by hand, the control plane none; kube-prometheus-stack with the lab's values; a DaemonSet asking cpu 500m and memory 64Mi whose pod works only on the `m5.4xlarge` node and reached the `c5.large` node only after 57 minutes; the Actuator from the working tree's image with the shipped manifests; the working tree's Hub app on localhost with a throwaway database, a synthetic Contract built in code and a scripted model: no model calls, no cloud); everything deleted after. The Hub's first run, with 1.1 hours of usage under a policy asking one: the pooled Sizing cpu 349m and memory 32Mi; `m5.large` and the node with no type cpu 10m, `m5.4xlarge` cpu 349m, each memory 32Mi; `c5.large` learning (0.2 h). Before any approval the Hub's answer to an Actuator that says it knows types already had the four nodes' types, and to one that doesn't none. Approved with its three types (the store call `approve_sizing` makes; the tool itself not driven): the Actuator that knows types got them, the other the pooled 349m. The Actuator resized a canary (the pod on the node with no type, to 10m and 32Mi), then after its ten minutes the other two, each to its node's type's; the `c5.large` pod kept its template's; "3 of 4 pods resized", its Actuation stored with no `cpu` or `memory` and the three types in its reason, its Event "requests by instance type". A deleted pod's replacement was caught up to its type's within a minute; with the hold applied again, new pods were created with their type's requests by the webhook (never resized: generation 1), the `c5.large` one with its template's. The Actuator restarted: it held the container, resized nothing, and a pod created after it still got its type's. A Revert put the three pods back to cpu 500m and memory 64Mi and a new pod started from its template. Found by the check: a put back's reason read "back from by instance type", now "back from each instance type's requests: ...". Mistakes of the check's own setup, not the code's: the webhook's namespace selector left on the shipped `demo` (so the first new pods were never offered to it), and new pods made while a catch-up's canary still waited (the webhook gives nothing while a hold rolls, as for any Workload). Not checked live: the risk where the pooled CPU asks less than a type needs (here the pooled Sizing was the busy type's), a pre-check failing for one type, Automation's churn rule by type, a Step-up on such a hold, a node joining the cluster (kind adds none), `approve_sizing` through the Hub MCP, more than a few nodes.
- 15.3c grilled 2026-11-15 (all recommended): realized Savings one drop per type (a container's drop in place when its types come to be applied becomes one per type; a type's drop ends with its type or when types are no longer applied); the Hub remembers per cluster whether its Actuator applies types, and only there a container with types goes by them (its pooled Sizing shown, not applied, raising no type risk, `approve_sizing` refusing it when no type changes a request), a cluster with an older Actuator staying as it was; nodes that could go after the Sizings give a DaemonSet's pod its node's type's Sizing, in this slice. Decided without asking (said with the grill): a type still learning counts no Waste, pods on no known node cost the container's request, the first digest after the switch compares a week by type with a pooled one, no eval rerun.
- 15.3c built 2026-11-15. The glossary's Waste, Savings and Sizing say it first. Migration 0040: `cluster_actuators` (each cluster's `types`, written when a pull says otherwise; the route keeps the last said in memory, so the store is written only on a change) and `realized.type` (null: the container's, as every drop stored before), its unique key replaced by one with the type, nulls not distinct (Postgres 15 and later; the compose stack runs 17). `sizing.rows(..., typed)`: on those clusters a row with groups is `by_type`, its Waste the sum of its ready types' own (each type stored with its `waste` and `live`), `_short_of` not raised; `sizing.requested` sums each type's pods' requests and its pods on no known node at the row's, the remainder never below zero; `realize` per type (new drops from each type's previous run, matched against that type's Sizings shown, the conversion and the endings above, a type not live leaving its drop as it is); `savings` values a type's drop by that type's pods and says its `type` (`Drop.type` in the API); `blockers.after` gives a DaemonSet pod of a `by_type` row its node's type's targets, its own request on a type with none. Automation sends nothing to a typed Actuator while every type of a container is learning (in 15.3b it fell back to the pooled Sizing). The console opens "By instance type" on such a container and says the line above is not applied; the Hub MCP's `sizings` says `applied`. Chosen beyond the grill: a container's drops converted keep their run, request and matched Sizing; of a chain of them (each lowering a drop of its own) only the one with the lowest `after` goes down to what each type's pods ask at the conversion and the others never below their own, so the chain adds up to the first request less the type's (a type's lowering since the last run is in it, and no second drop is made that run); a run that can't read the pods' instance types (`Collected.untyped`) leaves the cluster's DaemonSets' drops as they are and makes none, their rows' missing groups saying nothing (that hour's Waste is the pooled one); a type's pods not read live keep its drop, as a container's do; the Triage agent's `sizing` tool is unchanged in shape (its Waste is the row's, so by type where applied; adding the types to it would be a model-visible change and an eval rerun). `squero sizing savings` names a drop's type. Tests for every branch, 45 mutations of which 44 caught and one equivalent (a learning type left in `blockers.after`'s map has no targets, so its pods keep their own request either way), two Contracts' flags and drops kept apart; the existing store_run test's stand-ins for `rows` and the collected read take the new argument and field.
- 15.3c checked live 2026-11-15 04:19 to 05:49 UTC on a throwaway kind cluster on the Mac, set up as 15.3b's check (instance types `m5.large`, `m5.4xlarge`, `c5.large` by hand, the control plane none; the DaemonSet busy only on the `m5.4xlarge` node, its pod on `c5.large` started 56 minutes in) with synthetic unit prices (0.04 USD a vCPU-hour, 0.005 a GiB-hour) in the Contract built in code; the working tree's Actuator image and Hub app, a throwaway database, a scripted model: no model calls, no cloud; the second and third Sizing runs taken on demand through `sizing.store_run`, the hourly tick's own function; everything deleted after. The Actuator's first pull stored its cluster as applying types before the Hub's first tick, so every run was by type. Found by the check: a type's pods were averaged over that type's own time, so the `c5.large` pod, 12 minutes old, counted as a whole pod over the window, and the cost of requests read 2.0 cores for a container of 3.2 pods. Fixed: each type keeps `pods` as shown (its own while it runs) and a `share` of the container's pods (averaged over the container's span), which its Waste, the cost of requests and its drops' worth count, so the types add up to the container; tested and mutation-checked. Then, before the approval: Waste 1.131 cores (33.37 USD/month: the idle types against their 10m, the busy one against its 349m, the learning type none; the pooled Sizing alone would have counted about 0.48), the cost of requests 1.607 cores (47.66 USD/month), the API's `by_type` and the Hub MCP's `applied` saying the types are applied, and after the Sizings each agent pod at its node's type's (10m, 10m, 349m, the learning type's 500m kept). Approved with its three types; the Actuator rolled them out (canary, then one at a time). The next run: Waste 0, the cost of requests 0.569 cores (17.05 USD/month), and six realized drops, cpu and memory for each ready type, each worth its own pod, together 1.131 cores and 96 MiB, 33.37 USD/month: the Waste the approval took away. The container's pooled request still read 500m (the learning type's pod), so before this slice no drop at all would have been seen. Not checked live: a container's drop converted into one per type (and a chain of them), a failed read of the types, a type's drop ended, the console page in a browser, `approve_sizing` through the Hub MCP, `squero sizing savings` (each unit-tested).
- 15.2b grilled 2026-11-15 (one round, all recommended): the Actuator reports each blocking pod's own Workload, as its webhook names it from the pod's owners and labels, so the Hub's list comes from one snapshot (15.2a's open gap after a rollout; not a day's grace on the Actuator, not accepted); one Event on a Workload when it joins the list and one when it leaves, not one per pod (the webhook has no pod name or uid to put it on, and the minute loop would post one per pod at every rollout); a pod carries the preference when its own affinity has a preferred term with the label's key and value, whoever put it there (no annotation of the Actuator's). Not asked, each with one sensible answer: `key=value:Effect` tolerated `Equal`, `key:Effect` `Exists`; a Workload leaving the list leaves its running pods as they are; the Kill switch and the pause annotation stop it; no new permission.
- 15.2b checked live 2026-11-15 on a throwaway kind cluster (one control plane, three workers; `worker3` labelled `squero.io/pinned=yes` and tainted with it, plus `example.com/PinnedPool=Yes`; kube-prometheus-stack 92.11.0 with every node label allowlisted; the working tree's Actuator image and Hub; a Deployment `keep` of 3 pods annotated `safe-to-evict: "false"` on the two other workers, `web` beside it), the phase's done-when: before a read the Hub sent the label with no list; after it, `worker3` pinned, `keep` listed, both nodes it held "Restart shop/Deployment/keep"; a `Placed` Event on `keep` within the minute; `keep` restarted by hand, all three new pods on `worker3` with the preference (weight 100) and the `Equal` toleration, and once its report came both nodes it held without a Blocker (unknown, not clear: pods of other namespaces, as 15.1b has it), its pods reported `placed` with their own Workload; the Actuator restarted, `Placed` said again. The pause annotation on `keep`, which nothing else held, read and its new pod left alone; the Kill switch the same. With room on `worker3` every placed pod went there (10 of 10, the node at 21% CPU): none landed elsewhere while it had room, so "had no room when it started" stands. With `worker3` filled by a pod outside the Contract, the next two placed pods started on the other workers (a preference, nothing Pending), each with the note, the two started under the pause and the Kill switch without it, and both nodes "the pinned nodes lack room: about 0.7 cores short" (4 × 200m off it, 100m free). The Contract's Placement removed: the Hub sent none, `PlacementEnded` on `keep`, its next pod not placed. Found: kube-state-metrics names `example.com/PinnedPool` `label_example_com_pinned_pool`, snake case after the character substitution, so a label key with a capital letter was never found; `contracts.ksm_label` (shared with 10.2b's team labels on pods, which had the same flaw) now does both, tested with that name. Everything deleted; the Mac's `squero` cluster untouched.
- 15.2b built 2026-11-15: ADR 0006 (Placement is a preference, never a requirement, and never moves a running pod) first. The webhook (`actuator.placed`): one preferred node affinity term of weight 100 for the label, added at the end of the pod's own (`/spec/affinity`, `/nodeAffinity`, the list or its end, whichever the pod lacks), none where a term prefers that key and value already; the named taint's toleration where the pod has none matching it; only for a Workload in the list, never a DaemonSet's, and under every rule the webhook already keeps (CREATE only, no `kube-*` or own namespace, a paused Workload, the Kill switch). The Actuator keeps the Hub's Placement in memory (`place`): None turns it off, a list the Hub can't make now (`workloads` None: no usable report or read) keeps the last, under the Kill switch it is kept and nothing placed; an Event `Placed` or `PlacementEnded` on each Workload that joins or leaves (said again after a restart). Its report: `placing` with a Placement, and per pod `placed` (`carries`) and `workload` (`Kind/name`, by `workload_of`); `workload` is sent only with a Placement, since only a Hub that knows the field gives one (`blockers.sent`), and the Hub takes it before its own read's and refuses one not shaped `Kind/name`. A placed Workload's pause annotation is read each minute like a held one's (else a pause would not stop it); the Events go at the end of the minute, on their own, as the Blockers' report does (one per Workload, all again after a restart, before the Rollback watch would have delayed it); under the Kill switch the report doesn't say it places, so the Hub names no Workload to restart while nothing is placed. Chosen beyond the grill: the Hub now sends a Placement with no list (`workloads` None) where 15.2a sent none, so that None can mean off and the Actuator stops placing when the Contract's Placement is removed (15.2a was not released). Tests for every branch, each patch applied to the pod with `jsonpatch`; 33 mutations, all caught (one more, a pod's `placed` without a label, was equivalent: no key is empty, so the guard was dropped). No model-visible change: no eval rerun.
- 15.2a built 2026-11-15, not checked live (its live check comes with 15.2b's webhook): the glossary's *Placement* and *Pinned node* say it first. `Cluster.placement` (`label`, `key=value`; `taint`, `key[=value]:effect`), refused without the cluster's `actuator_token_secret`, naming the cluster. `usage.rooms` marks each node `pinned` by the label as kube-state-metrics names it (`label_` and the key with every other character `_`). `blockers.verdicts`: a Pinned node's verdict `pinned`, its Blockers kept with no change; each blocking pod's `placement` (the Workload it is placed by) and `note`; a blocked node's `restart` (`_restart`: the Workloads to restart, or `why_not`); `blockers.placing` the list, from the same verdicts as the page so the two can't disagree; `could_go` lands nothing on a Pinned node. `Desired.placement` (`Placing`: label, taint, Workloads), read by no Actuator yet; None for a cluster without Placement, without a read of its nodes or a usable report, and under the Kill switch. The report's `placement` per cluster on the Nodes page and in the Hub MCP's `nodes` (`placement`, a blocker's `placed_by`, a node's `restart`); `judged` counts `pinned` only where the cluster has Placement. No migration (the read and the report are stored as JSON). Chosen beyond the grill: a custom controller's pod is not placed, though Q2 listed it, since the webhook names a pod's Workload from its owners and labels and returns none for one (`actuator.workload_of`), so a Deployment, StatefulSet, Argo Rollout or CronJob only; the restart advice and a placed pod's note need the Actuator to say it places and which pods carry the preference, so the report gains `placing` and a pod `placed` (filled by 15.2b's Actuator; a Hub of 15.2a takes them first, as the report refuses an unknown field), and until then no node says "restart"; the restart's room counts every pod of those Workloads not on a Pinned node already (a restart moves them all), whatever the Pinned nodes' taints (a placed pod tolerates the Placement's), each node on its own; where no node shows the label the page says to allowlist it in kube-state-metrics; a read says the label it was made with (`placement`), and one made with another (the Contract's label set or changed since, or a read from before 15.2a) pins no node until the next hourly run, the page saying so; an Actuator posts `placing` and a pod's `placed` only when true (`blockers.sent`), so a Hub from before 15.2a, whose report refuses a field it doesn't know, still takes the report of an Actuator that doesn't place; the Hub MCP caps the new lists (20 placed Workloads, five to restart, three pods named in a `why_not`, the rest counted). Open, for 15.2b: check how kube-state-metrics names a label key with a capital letter (`eks.amazonaws.com/capacityType`) against the substitution, in its live check; and the list joins two hourly snapshots (the report's pods, the read's Workloads), so a Workload just rolled out may drop off it for an hour, its new pods not yet in the read: decide there whether the Actuator keeps a Workload it was told of until the list has left it out for a while. Tests for every branch, two Contracts' lists kept apart; 50 mutations, all caught; the existing tests that pin the desired state's, a pod's facts' and a node's shapes take the new fields. The Hub MCP's `nodes` description changed (no Scenario uses it: no eval rerun).
- 15.3a built 2026-11-14, not checked live: the glossary's Sizing says it first. `usage.pod_types` reads each of the Contract's pods' node and each node's instance type over the Sizing's window (behind the guard of every node read, and only for a cluster with an `actuator_token_secret`); `usage.usage` keeps a DaemonSet's container also per type (`ContainerUsage.groups`: its own pods' samples, requests and limits, its pods counted from the Running phase like the row's, the container's throttling and OOM kills carried over), dropped with fewer than two types; `sizing.rows` gives the row `groups`, each sized by `size` under the container's policy. The row keeps its identity and its pooled Sizing, so approvals, the Actuator, Waste, Cost and Reverts are untouched. Shown by `sizing show`, the API (`Container.groups`), the console and the Hub MCP's `sizings` (ten types, the rest counted); the Triage agent's `sizing` renderer picks its fields and doesn't carry them: no eval rerun. No migration (the row is stored as JSON). Changed from the grilling: Waste stays on the pooled Sizing in this slice and moves to the types with 15.3b, when an Actuator can apply them: before that nothing could reach that money, a type still learning would count nothing while its pods run on the pooled figure, and a type's Waste must be against its own pods' requests. Found while building: the pooled Sizing "fits the busiest" (the phase's grill) for memory, a peak, but not for CPU, a percentile over every pod's samples: with one busy node among thirty the pooled CPU target was under 100m while that node's pod used a core (a fixture of the tests), so today's Sizing of a DaemonSet asks too little CPU for a busy minority, and the per-type lines are where that shows; put to the Operator. Decided by the Operator on it (2026-11-14): a risk on the row, `type_needs_more_cpu`, where a ready type needs more CPU than the one Sizing by more than a change the policy would make (not: leaving it until 15.3b; not: never lowering the row below its highest type, which would change what Automation applies); it flags and changes nothing applied, and only shows where types are read. The Waste timing above stands. Chosen beyond the grill: a pod whose node isn't found is in no type (it would make one that is always learning) and stays in the pooled Sizing; a type's requests are its own pods'; no cap by the largest Room, as for the row; a failed read of the types means none that hour, never a failed cluster. Mutation check: 38 mutations, all killed after three tests were sharpened. Not checked live.
- 15.3a's read checked live 2026-11-14 22:55 to 23:10 UTC on a throwaway kind cluster on the Mac (v1.37, a control plane and three workers, two given the instance type `m5.large` and one `m5.4xlarge` by hand, the control plane none; kube-prometheus-stack with the lab's values, which allowlist the label; a DaemonSet whose pod works only on the large node), no Hub process and no model calls; cluster deleted after. `kube_node_labels` carried `label_node_kubernetes_io_instance_type` for the three labelled nodes and had a series without it for the control plane; `usage.pod_types` over the last hour was equal to the API server's (each pod's node, and that node's label), the control plane's pod with no type; `usage.usage` gave the container three groups (no type, `m5.4xlarge`, `m5.large` with its two pods), each with its own pods' samples (0.17 cores at most on the large node, none on the others) and requests; one run's row carried the three, all still learning with minutes of history, and none for the same cluster without an Actuator. Not checked live: the Sizing of each type and the risk, which need an hour of usage at the shortest policy (left for 15.3b's check on such a cluster); the page in a browser.
- *Done when:* on multi-node kind, placement empties a node the report named as blocked.

### Phase 16: Spot (6 slices, grilled 2026-11-12)

- 16.1 A spot ratio per Workload with an on-demand floor. 16.2 Placing replicas by capacity type. 16.3 Fallback on interruption.
- Grilled 2026-11-12 (four rounds; all recommended but CronJobs on spot, which the Operator chose to build in this phase rather than defer). "Floor" is Phase 14's term, so 16.1's on-demand floor is the *On-demand minimum* here.
  - **Scope.** Recommend first, then act: the Hub prices spot and shows candidates, then the Actuator's webhook places each new replica; nothing in a manifest changes (a topology spread balances two groups evenly, so a manifest can't hold a 70/30 split; Karpenter's documented recipe for it says itself that it "is not identical" to a ratio). The Operator sets it in the Contract, on the Sizing rule that picks the Workload: `spot: {ratio: 0.7, on_demand: 2}`; the Hub only shows where to look. Its own setting, not tied to Automation; it needs an Actuator and the cluster's spot label. Deployments and Argo Rollouts with 2 or more replicas, and CronJobs by their own rule; never a StatefulSet (its state and volumes) or a DaemonSet; a rule naming a kind that can't is refused.
  - **The numbers.** On-demand replicas: the larger of the On-demand minimum and the share that isn't spot, rounded up ("70% on spot, but never fewer than 2 on-demand": 10 replicas give 3 and 7, 4 give 2 and 2). The minimum is 1 or more, so no Workload is ever all on spot.
  - **Placing a replica (16.2).** The On-demand minimum is a requirement, "not on a spot node" (the one requirement the webhook adds; Placement's are preferences); the spot share is a preference, so a spot replica that finds no spot room lands on on-demand by itself and fallback needs no code, as Karpenter launches on-demand when a NodePool allowing both has no spot. On Cluster Autoscaler a preference isn't counted when it picks a node group to grow, so whether new nodes are spot is the client's expander setting: said in the guide. Spot is a node label the Contract names per cluster (`karpenter.sh/capacity-type=spot`, `eks.amazonaws.com/capacityType=SPOT`, `cloud.google.com/gke-spot=true`, `kubernetes.azure.com/scalesetpriority=spot`); on-demand is any node without it (`NotIn` matches a node that lacks the label); on AKS, whose spot nodes are tainted, the webhook also adds the toleration to spot replicas. Counted per ReplicaSet, so each new version reaches the ratio by itself (counted over the Workload, old pods would leave the new version short of on-demand ones), on-demand replicas first, the count corrected against the cluster each minute (the docs don't say a Deployment's pods are admitted one at a time).
  - **Keeping it.** Scale-down: on-demand replicas get a higher `controller.kubernetes.io/pod-deletion-cost` at creation, so spot ones go first and the minimum survives (best effort, Kubernetes says; the mix leans to on-demand until pods are created again); a Workload that sets deletion costs itself is left alone. Drift (a spot replica that fell back, pods created while the Actuator was down) is repaired by nobody until the next rollout or scale-up: the Hub shows intended against actual and a risk line below the minimum ("restart to repair"); the Actuator never evicts for it. The webhook stays fail-open, so while the Actuator is down the ratio and the minimum are best effort. The Kill switch and the pause annotation stop new placements; running pods stay. A Workload Phase 15's report names as blocking gets no spot replicas, and the Hub says why.
  - **Interruptions (16.3 as first written).** After one, the ReplicaSet creates new pods and the webhook places them again: nothing to build but 12.10 (Phase 12), without which a spot interruption in a Sizing's watch hour is a false Rollback. Notice is 2 minutes on AWS, 30 seconds on Azure, 0, 30 or 120 seconds on GCP.
  - **CronJobs on spot.** Only through a rule that names `kind: CronJob`, written `spot:` with no ratio and no minimum (one with either is refused): a batch run that starts again from zero is a judgement of its own. Only a CronJob whose Job allows a retry (`backoffLimit` of 1 or more; the default is 6), others skipped with the reason shown. Every pod of a run prefers spot on its first try; once the run has a failed pod its retries are required off spot, so a run is interrupted once at most. A Job of no CronJob stays skipped. Decided 2026-11-12, to build with 16.3: a run's pod that Failed because it was evicted to drain its node (`EvictionByEvictionAPI`, how Karpenter empties a spot node it is about to lose) is no Rollback either; 12.10 left it counting.
  - **Money.** Spot nodes are priced as on-demand today, so a cluster with spot shows too high a cost: first a discount typed per cluster in the Contract (one field, every cloud), later spot list prices (Azure's Retail Prices API, public; GCP's catalog with the API key it already needs; AWS's `DescribeSpotPriceHistory` with the Contract's own credentials, never its undocumented public feed). kube-state-metrics must allowlist the spot label, as it does the instance type. The Bill check counts spot nodes at their discount and includes the bill's spot lines, for all three clouds, or a cluster that moves to spot stops reconciling. Candidates, from what the Hub already reads: a Deployment or Rollout with 2 or more replicas on average, no persistent volume, not named as blocking; and CronJobs. Each shows a what-if (70% spot with a minimum of 2; a CronJob run entirely on spot) in its own figure, an upper bound, never summed with Savings: nothing is saved unless a spot pool exists and the autoscaler shifts nodes to it. No realized spot saving is computed: the cost pages show spot nodes at their real price.
  - **Terms.** *Spot ratio*: the share of a Workload's replicas the webhook steers to spot nodes. *On-demand minimum*: the replicas it keeps off them.
  - **Slices.** 16.1a: the cluster's spot label and typed discount, spot nodes priced. 16.1b: the Bill check with spot. 16.1c: `spot:` on the Sizing rule, the candidates, the saving figure, intended against actual. 16.2: the webhook places replicas, with Phase 15's Placement ADR extended by its one requirement; live check on kind with two "spot" and two on-demand workers, a spot node deleted twice: with spot room left (the ratio holds on real nodes) and with none (the minimum holds, the rest falls back), the phase's done-when. 16.3: CronJobs on spot; live check on kind (a run on a "spot" node, the node deleted mid-run, the retry on on-demand, the run complete). 16.4: spot list prices. Then one small, time-boxed AWS cluster with Karpenter for Phases 15 and 16 (a real interruption, and Karpenter's handling of a preference): under 5 USD for a few hours as a first guess, the exact estimate and the Operator's yes before anything is created.
  - Looked up for it (2026-11-12; cloud, Karpenter and Kubernetes docs, Cluster Autoscaler's FAQ, Kubernetes source): only AKS taints spot nodes by default; Karpenter prefers reserved, then spot, then on-demand; the ReplicaSet's scale-down order (Pending first, then the lower deletion cost, then nodes with more replicas, then newer pods); Argo Rollouts sets a ReplicaSet's replicas and leaves the choice of pods to its controller (inferred from its source); "CA does not consider soft constraints like `preferredDuringSchedulingIgnoredDuringExecution` when selecting node groups"; CAST AI's webhook applies its spot percentage per ReplicaSet.
- 16.1a grilled 2026-11-15 (one round, all recommended): `spot: {label, discount}` per cluster, the discount the share of on-demand a spot node costs (0.35: it pays 35%), as `list_prices.discount` is a multiplier, more than 0 and less than 1; list prices count each spot node's at it, and typed prices too, read then as the on-demand rate and blended by the cluster's spot and on-demand capacity each hour (a cluster's spot share moves, and a typed blended rate would go stale), only where a cluster sets `spot`; every pod costs the cluster's blended price, not its node's (cost by namespace and team, Waste and Savings keep one rate; 16.1c's what-if weighs spot per Workload); a node that could go at its own rate; where no node shows the label, every node on-demand and the price note and the Nodes page say to allowlist it; no Actuator needed (pricing reads Prometheus).
- 16.1a built 2026-11-15: the glossary's *Spot node* (and *List price*) first. `Cluster.spot` (`label`, `key=value`; `discount`). `usage.carries` (a node's labels as kube-state-metrics names them, shared with Placement's Pinned nodes) marks each node `spot` in both reads, the one list prices come from (`usage.nodes`, now also for a typed cluster with `spot`) and the Nodes page's (`usage.rooms`), each saying the label it was read with. `listprices.spot_paid`: a cluster's nodes as a share of their on-demand price, by size (`weight` GiB a vCPU, 7 without list prices); `_from_list` counts a spot node's list price at the discount; `_typed` blends a typed price (the cluster's or the Contract's), in `resolve` and, for a Contract with typed prices only, in `effective`, whose notes now come for its spot clusters too (the cost pages, `sizing cost` and `sizing prices` print them). A read made with another label (or before 16.1a) prices every node on-demand and says the next run reads it. `sizing.rates` and `_worth`: a node that could go at its own rate against the blended price. The Nodes page and the Hub MCP's `nodes`: `spot` per node and per cluster, with a note where no node shows the label. Asked of the Operator while building (2026-11-15): a deal (`list_prices.discount`) and the spot discount don't compound; the deal is an on-demand node's only (committed-use deals don't cover spot capacity), so a spot node pays its own discount of the list price and no more. Chosen beyond the grill: a node that could go is priced from what its cluster's pricing applied (`Contract._spot_shares`: the price as a share of the nodes all on-demand, and a spot node's rate against an on-demand one's), never from a second count of the mix, so an on-demand node costs the on-demand rate and a spot node its own; a list price's on-demand rate is the cluster's average, as every node was priced before. A read that failed says its problem in the price note; `sizing prices` prints the Contract's average price for list prices only (a typed Contract-level price stays the on-demand rate). Until 16.1b reads the bill's spot lines, a cluster that sets `spot` may show its estimate below its bill: said in the guide. Tests for every branch, two Contracts' nodes kept apart; 34 mutations, all caught (one more, a duplicate `model_config`, changed nothing and was dropped). The existing tests that pin a node read's shape take `spot`. No model-visible change but the Hub MCP's `nodes` (no Scenario uses it: no eval rerun). Not checked live: pricing goes by Prometheus' labels, the read checked live with Placement's.
- 16.1b grilled 2026-11-15 (one round, all recommended): every cluster's bill counts its spot lines, whether or not it sets `spot` (the bill is what the cluster costs; a cluster on spot without `spot:` shows its estimate above its bill, which points at the setting): GCP's query stops leaving out its `Spot Preemptible` SKUs; AWS's (`RECORD_TYPE` Usage) and Azure's (`ChargeType` Usage) filters are believed to take spot lines already, which the docs don't settle for AWS: unconfirmed until the AWS live check. The estimate splits the window's node-hours by the spot label, hour by hour (a join on `kube_node_labels`), on-demand hours at the on-demand rate and spot hours at their own, the rates 16.1a's pricing applied, so it follows the mix the bill charged, not the latest one. The bill stays one total; the row says what share of the vCPU-hours ran on spot (the bill's own spot part, the discount it charged, deferred to 16.4, when spot list prices give it something to check). A cluster that sets `spot` with no spot node-hours in the window keeps its verdict, and the row says to allowlist the label if spot nodes ran. Found while looking it up: an EA account's Azure spot rows are priced in USD whatever its currency, so a day billed in two currencies is refused (the read fails saying so), never one of them kept.
- 16.1b built 2026-11-15: the glossary's *Bill* first (spot lines counted, a Spot node's hours at its own rate). GCP's query no longer leaves out `Spot Preemptible` SKUs. `usage.spot_hours`: the window's capacity-hours of nodes carrying the cluster's spot label, each hour by the nodes that carried it then: both read by node and hour, scoped to the cluster, and joined in Python, not in PromQL, since a node's name may be in `exported_node` (kube-state-metrics scraped without `honor_labels`, as the Nodes page reads it, found in review while building); the key named as kube-state-metrics names it; asked only for a cluster that sets `spot`. `billing.report` undoes the blend its price carries (`Contract._spot_shares`) and prices spot hours at a spot node's rate and the rest at an on-demand one's; the row's note says the share of vCPU-hours on spot and the rate, or, with none, to allowlist the label, beside the sampled-hours note. `azure_parse` refuses a day in two currencies, on a page or across pages. Chosen beyond the grill: a cluster that sets `spot` now keeps its rates in `_spot_shares` whenever it is priced, at a share of 1.0 where its latest read priced no spot node (the read made without the label, failed, or with none on spot), so the Bill check prices a window's spot hours even then, and the Nodes page prices a spot node its own read shows and pricing's didn't at its rate against the on-demand price (16.1a left both at the cluster's price). Tests for every branch, two clusters with nodes of the same names in one store kept apart; 22 mutations, all caught (the capacity side's scope survived a first run: the test now has the other cluster's node of the same name), and 16.1a's 34 rerun, all caught. No model-visible change but the `bill` tool's note text (no Scenario uses it: no eval rerun); no API or console change. Not checked live: AWS's and Azure's spot lines are believed from the docs, unconfirmed until the AWS live check; the read of the spot hours was run read-only on the Mac lab's Prometheus (2026-11-15, its kind node started for it and stopped again): its node carries no allowlisted label, so a matcher that every node series meets (`label_absent=""`) stood in for a spot label; its spot hours equalled the node-hours exactly (180 vCPU-hours and 563.2 GiB-hours over 18 sampled hours of the week), and a label no node carries gave none, so the join by node and hour holds on a real Prometheus's answers.
- 16.1c grilled 2026-11-15 (one round, all recommended): `spot: {ratio, on_demand}` on a Sizing rule, both required (1 against 2 on-demand is a judgement of availability), `ratio` more than 0 and at most 1, `on_demand` a whole 1 or more; refused on a rule that sets `container:` (spot is a Workload's), names a kind that can't (StatefulSet, DaemonSet, Job; CronJob with 16.3), or reaches no cluster that names its spot label and runs an Actuator; a rule without `kind` touches only its Deployments and Rollouts; a Workload's is the first rule without `container` that matches it; any policy, `off` too, with or without `automate`. Candidates on every cluster with Prometheus (where to look, most useful before a cluster has spot): a Deployment or Rollout with 2 or more replicas on average in the latest Sizings, no persistent volume (kube-state-metrics' `kube_pod_spec_volumes_persistentvolumeclaims_info`), not named as blocking by its Actuator's latest usable report, not under a `spot` rule; CronJobs (with a retry allowed) with 16.3, which reads that. Each a what-if, 70% spot with a minimum of 2: its spot replicas times one pod's current requests at the cluster's on-demand rate times one less a spot node's rate against it, a month, an upper bound never summed with Savings, a total per cluster, ranked; money only where the cluster sets `spot`, else replicas and requests and "set the cluster's `spot` to price it". Intended against actual for each Workload under a `spot` rule: intended from its live replicas, actual from its pods' nodes in the Nodes page's read (no new query); one that blocks a node, has fewer than 2 replicas or is of a kind that can't, listed with why; "placed from 16.2" until the webhook places, no risk line until then. On the Nodes page and the Hub MCP's `nodes`, not in the digest; nothing new reaches the Actuator.
- 16.1c built 2026-11-15: the glossary's *Spot ratio* and *On-demand minimum* first. `SizingRule.spot` (`SpotRule`: `ratio`, `on_demand`) with its refusals, and the Contract's: a rule's `spot` needs a cluster it reaches with both a spot label and an `actuator_token_secret`. `SizingConfig.spot` (the first rule without `container` matching the Workload) and `Contract.spot_for` (only where the cluster has both). `usage.rooms` reads which pods claim a persistent volume (`pvc`, scoped to the Contract's namespaces) and stamps the read with it, so a read from before says it can't list candidates. `sizing.spot_split` (the ceiling taken after rounding: 10 times 0.3 is 3.0000000000000004), `candidates` and `spot_workloads`, in `nodes_report`; the Nodes page (a list under a ratio, the candidates folded with their total) and the Hub MCP's `nodes` (`spot_candidates`, `spot_workloads` capped at 20).
