Asked of the Operator while building (2026-11-15): a Workload with 2 replicas on average meets the grilled rule but the what-if keeps both on-demand; it is left out, so a candidate always moves one replica (3 or more, rounded), which also makes the grilled "2 or more" check redundant: dropped. Chosen beyond the grill: a candidate's average replicas are rounded half up (2.5 is 3), its pods the most of its containers' and its requests their sum; a rule naming a container matches no Workload as a whole by itself (no container is ""), so no extra check. Tests for every branch, two Contracts' nodes kept apart, the Desired payload without a spot field; 48 mutations, all caught (8 survived a first run: one equivalent, a guard `matches` already made, dropped; the rest found tests missing: a pod's volume read by name in a fixture of one namespace, now two, a cluster with only one of a spot label and an Actuator, a rule where its cluster can't place, the rank by saving rather than cores, and the Hub MCP's fields). A review before shipping found three more, fixed: the pricing note shown on a cluster with no candidate (now only with one), a live CronJob under a rule without `kind` said to never run on spot (now "with slice 16.3"), and the isolation test storing no Sizings (now each Contract has its own candidate). The Hub MCP's `nodes` description changed (no Scenario uses it: no eval rerun). Not checked live: nothing is placed until 16.2, whose live check on kind shows intended against actual on real nodes.
- 16.2 grilled 2026-11-15 (one round, all recommended): split as 15.2 was, 16.2a the Hub's side (`Desired.spot`: each Workload under a Spot ratio with its ratio and minimum, the cluster's spot nodes, its optional spot taint; the risk line below the minimum) and 16.2b the Actuator's (the webhook, the counts, the deletion cost, the Events, the live check on kind). The webhook goes by ReplicaSet: its `spec.replicas` read at admission and kept for the minute; the pods already meant on-demand from a label it puts on each pod it places (`squero.io/capacity: on-demand|spot`), listed each minute and kept up with admissions since; a pod it didn't place counts by its node, from the spot nodes the Hub sends (as it sends node types), a Pending one without the label as neither. On-demand first: while fewer than the split's on-demand count, a new pod is required off spot (`NotIn`); then it prefers spot (weight 100) with the taint's toleration. AKS's taint is an optional `taint` on the cluster's `spot`, tolerated by spot replicas only. On-demand replicas get `controller.kubernetes.io/pod-deletion-cost: "1"` and spot ones none, unless the template sets it; a pod whose own spec already settles its side (a `nodeSelector` or a required affinity on the label's key) is left alone. Events `SpotPlaced` and `SpotEnded` on a Workload joining or leaving the list; a risk line on the Nodes page and the Hub MCP below the minimum ("1 on-demand, below its minimum of 2: restart it to repair"), not in the Agenda or the digest (best effort until 16.3); "placed from 16.2" goes once its Actuator says it places.
- 16.2a built 2026-11-15: `Spot.taint` (`key[=value]:effect`, as Placement's). `actuator.Spotted` and `Spotting` on `Desired.spot` (an older Actuator ignores it): the label and taint, the Workloads (`sizing.spotted`: live, in the cluster's namespaces now, under a ratio their cluster can place, of a kind that can; one that blocks a node by a usable report with ratio 0, every replica kept off spot), the spot nodes (`sizing.spot_nodes`: None in a read made without the label); no list without a usable read and report (the Actuator keeps the last), nothing under the Kill switch, None for a cluster without `spot`. The report's `spotting` (sent only when true, as `placing`). `sizing.blocking_of`, shared with 16.1c's candidates. On the Nodes page and the Hub MCP, once a usable report says `spotting`: no "doesn't place them yet" note, and `risk` where fewer replicas run on on-demand nodes than meant (none where the read can't say). Asked of the Operator while building (2026-11-15): a Workload that blocks a node is kept off spot, sent with no share, as the grill's "gets no spot replicas" says, rather than left out of the list, where nothing would keep its pods off spot. Found in review while building: a stale report counted as placing (now only a usable one), the read's namespaces not filtered by the Contract's now (now they are), and "below its minimum of 1" for a Workload with fewer replicas than its minimum (now "0 of its 1 on-demand, where all are meant to be"). Tests for every branch, two Contracts' Actuators each sent only their own; 24 mutations, all caught (two survived a first run: no fixture had a Workload under no rule, nor one with fewer replicas than its minimum but 2 or more; now they do). Fixed in 16.2b's review: the risk compared a Workload's on-demand replicas with its intended share, not its minimum, so 2 of 10 under 0.7 with 2, its pods from before it was steered, said "below its minimum of 2"; now only below the minimum, or below every replica where all are meant on-demand (25 mutations, all caught), and 16.1c's rerun (its blocking check moved to `blocking_of`, covered here). The Hub MCP's `nodes` gains `risk` (no Scenario uses it). Not checked live: 16.2b's live check sends it to a real Actuator.
- 16.2b built 2026-11-15: ADR 0006 extended with its one requirement first. `contracts.on_demand_of`, the split the Hub's intent and the Actuator's count share (a ratio of 0 keeps every replica on-demand). The webhook (`steered`, `settled`, `sides`, `Actuator.steer_pod`, `steer`, `recount`, `counted`) as grilled: on-demand first, required off spot (`NotIn` into each of the pod's own required terms), with `squero.io/capacity` and a deletion cost of 1 unless the pod sets its own; then spot, a preference of weight 100 and the taint's toleration (Placement's `placed`); built last, against the pod as the webhook's other changes leave it (`added`, a few lines for the adds it makes, rather than jsonpatch, installed only as another package's dependency), so a Workload both placed and kept off spot gets both; only with a list, so a cluster without spot pays nothing for it. Counted per ReplicaSet under a lock (the webhook's threads): its replicas read at the first admission in the minute and again once the pods counted reach them (a rollout grows a ReplicaSet within the minute), both reads sharing one budget of 1.2 s (the API server gives the webhook 2, and a pod it creates unpatched must not be counted), a failure placing and counting nothing, the patch built before the count, a dry run never counted; a count read outside the lock is never laid over admissions counted meanwhile (`spot_seq`, per ReplicaSet), at admission or in the minute's recount. Asked of the Operator while building (2026-11-15): a ReplicaSet counted full is listed again at admission, by its template's hash, so a replacement for a pod lost since (a spot interruption) is decided by the fresh count rather than always on-demand. Each minute (not under the Kill switch) one list a namespace recounts the steered Workloads' ReplicaSets, a namespace it can't list keeping its counts. Events `SpotPlaced` and `SpotEnded`; the steered Workloads' pause annotation read; its report says `spotting` only with spot nodes from the Hub and never under the Kill switch. No new permission (ReplicaSet `get`, pod `list`, Events). Tests for every branch, two admissions racing for the last on-demand place and eight new ones listing at once among them; 36 mutations, all caught (a review before the live check found the stale-count race, the budget, the patch counted before it was built and the hook run without a list; one mutation, an insert at a list index, was equivalent: the branch was never used, so it is gone). The lock itself is not mutated: under the GIL a race without it rarely shows in a test, so it is a reviewed line, not a tested one. No model-visible change. Not checked live yet: the phase's done-when, on kind with two "spot" and two on-demand workers, waits for the Operator's yes.
- 16.2b checked live 2026-11-15, the phase's done-when, on a throwaway kind cluster (two workers labelled `karpenter.sh/capacity-type=spot`, two `on-demand`, kube-prometheus-stack allowlisting the label, the Actuator built from the tree, the working tree's Hub on the Mac with a scratch database; deleted afterwards): the Hub sent `cart` (0.5, 2) and `web` (0.7, 2) and the two spot nodes; before steering, `cart`'s 4 pods all on spot showed "0 on-demand, below its minimum of 2: restart it to repair" and `web`'s 6 and 4 no risk; `SpotPlaced` on both; `web` restarted landed 3 on-demand (on the on-demand nodes, deletion cost 1) and 7 spot (on the spot nodes); scaled to 5 it lost only spot replicas, back to 10 it gained 5 spot; a spot node stopped and deleted, its 4 replicas came back as spot on the other (recounted at admission: 3 and 7 held on real nodes); the last spot node gone, the minimum held and the 7 spot replicas fell back to the on-demand nodes, none Pending; the Nodes page then showed both all on-demand, no risk; paused, `web`'s new replicas were left alone; under the Kill switch `cart`'s too, the Hub sending no `spot`; after it, 11 replicas gave 4 and 7. The Actuator logged no error. Two things seen, fixed after: its report said `spotting` from its first pull, while its list was still unknown (a minute; now only once it knows its list); and with every spot node gone the price note and the Nodes page said to allowlist the label, as 16.1a can't tell no spot node from an unread label (now they say both: "none is on spot now, or the label isn't in its --metric-labels-allowlist").
- 16.3 grilled 2026-11-15 (one round, all recommended), on top of the phase's grill (a rule naming `kind: CronJob`, no ratio and no minimum; only Jobs that allow a retry; a run's first tries prefer spot, its retries required off it; a run's pod evicted to drain its node, `EvictionByEvictionAPI`, no Rollback): split as 16.2 was, 16.3a the Hub's side (the rule, CronJob candidates, CronJobs sent to the Actuator, its skips shown) and 16.3b the Actuator's (the webhook for runs, the Job and CronJob reads, the Rollback exemption, the live check on kind: a run on a "spot" node, the node deleted mid-run, the retry on on-demand, the run complete). Written `spot: true` on a rule with `kind: CronJob` (an empty map reads as a mistake), refused without it, and a CronJob rule with a ratio or minimum refused. A retry is told at admission from the run's Job (`status.failed` above 0: required off spot), read within 16.2b's budget, a failed read placing nothing. A CronJob whose Jobs allow no retry (`backoffLimit: 0`), or ignore disruptions (`podFailurePolicy` `Ignore` on `DisruptionTarget`, which never counts an interrupted pod as failed, so its retries would keep preferring spot), is skipped: the Actuator reads each listed CronJob once a minute (kube-state-metrics has no `backoffLimit`), posts an Event `SpotSkipped` with the reason, and lists the skipped ones with their reasons in its hourly report, which the Nodes page and the Hub MCP show. Candidates: every CronJob in the latest Sizings under no rule, its what-if a run entirely on spot (its average pods' requests at the on-demand rate times one less a spot node's rate, a month, an upper bound never summed with Savings), shown "if its Jobs allow a retry" unless its Actuator reported they don't, ranked with the Deployments by saving. Under `spot: true` the Nodes page says "its runs on spot, a retry off it" with its live pods by side or its skip reason, no intended split and no risk line (a run that falls back to on-demand is the design).
- 16.3a built 2026-11-15: the glossary's *Spot ratio* says a CronJob has none, its runs on spot by a rule of its own. `SizingRule.spot` takes `true` (a CronJob's: refused without `kind: CronJob`, and a CronJob rule with a ratio refused). The read of the nodes lists the Contract's CronJobs (`kube_cronjob_info`, scoped like the rest; `sizing.cronjobs_of` adds any whose run's pods it saw, so a read from before still lists the running ones). `sizing.spot_runs`: those under `spot: true` their cluster can place, in its namespaces now, sent in `Spotting.cronjobs`, a list of their own (an Actuator of 0.57.0, which takes `workloads` for Workloads with a ratio, ignores it). The report's `spot_runs` (it steers runs, sent only when true) and `spot_skipped` (`namespace/name`, checked as Kubernetes names, to a code, `no_retry` or `ignores_disruptions`, sent only when there are some): the Nodes page and the Hub MCP show the Hub's sentence for the code, a stale report's nothing, and "its Actuator doesn't steer its runs yet" until a usable report says `spot_runs`. On the Nodes page a CronJob under `spot: true`, running or not: "its runs on spot, a retry off it" with its live pods by side, no intended split and no risk; under a ratio of a rule without `kind`, told it needs a rule of its own. Candidates include CronJobs of the latest Sizings under no rule, not skipped, with a run seen: a run entirely on spot, its average pods at one less a spot node's rate. Asked of the Operator while building (2026-11-15): a CronJob whose run blocks a node is never left out (a run blocks only while it runs, which is the only time the Hub can see it, so leaving it out would flip the list run by run; and a spot node it holds is the cloud's to take anyway), nor left out of the candidates. Found in review while building: the page said runs were on spot from an Actuator of 0.57.0's `spotting`, which steers none (now `spot_runs`); a CronJob's average pods were stored to one decimal place, so an hour a night (0.04) was nothing, its cost and its candidacy with it (now three for Jobs and CronJobs); the reason was free text from the Actuator that would reach the Hub MCP's model (now a code); and the guide's example put the spot rules below a namespace-wide rule, which matches first (now above it, and the example is built in the check). Chosen beyond the grill: the report's skips are looked up only by the read's own CronJobs, so no filter by namespace (another namespace's key shows nowhere). Tests for every branch, two Contracts' Actuators each sent only their own CronJobs, the report posted to the Hub and refused with free text; 25 mutations, all caught (one survived a first run: the rule in the test named the namespace, which hid the filter of a namespace the Contract has left; now one doesn't), and 16.1c's and 16.2a's rerun, their lines that moved anchored again. The Hub MCP's `nodes` description changed (no Scenario uses it). Not checked live: 16.3b's live check sends it to a real Actuator, and reads `kube_cronjob_info`'s `cronjob` label from a real kube-state-metrics.
- 16.3b built 2026-11-15: ADR 0006's requirement extended to a run's retry first. `Actuator.check_runs`, each minute, reads each listed CronJob: `no_retry` where `backoffLimit` is 0, an Indexed Job's `backoffLimitPerIndex` is 0, or a `podFailurePolicy` rule fails the Job or its index on `DisruptionTarget` (an interruption ends the run: no retry either, chosen beyond the grill), `ignores_disruptions` where one ignores it; an Event `SpotSkipped` on a change, a CronJob it can't read keeping what it knew, and one not read yet not steered. `steer_run` at admission: the run's Job read within the 1.2 s budget (`Apps.read_namespaced_job`), a failed pod in its status (counted, or not yet: `uncountedTerminatedPods`) making the pod required off spot, else a preference for spot with the taint's toleration; no deletion cost (a Job scales by none) and no count. `steer` takes `cronjobs` (None keeps the last), with Events `SpotPlaced` and `SpotEnded` on the CronJob; its pause annotation read; the hook reached with CronJobs alone; its report says `spot_runs` and lists the listed ones it skips, never under the Kill switch or before its list is known. `drained`: a run's pod Failed by `EvictionByEvictionAPI` is no Rollback (12.10's test pinned it as one; now not, and a preemption or a condition not True still is). No new permission. Chosen beyond the grill: the report is hourly, so a change in what it tells (that it steers, a list now known, a CronJob's skip) brings the next one forward, rather than the Hub hearing of it within the hour. Found in review while building: the client guide still said a drain eviction counts as before (fixed, with the per-pod reading of "once at most" where `parallelism` is above 1), Indexed Jobs' own no-retry cases, the minute's recount and CronJob reads sharing one guard (now each its own), and the report's codes not validated before sending (now they are, and a test pins the three lists of codes equal). Tests for every branch; 29 mutations, all caught (one survived a first run: the test of a failing count had nothing to count; and one more was equivalent: the Event's API version, which `post` already takes from the kind, so the change that set it was dropped). Not checked live yet: on kind, a run on a "spot" node, the node deleted mid-run, the retry on on-demand, the run complete, waits for the Operator's yes.
- 16.3b checked live 2026-11-16, the phase's 16.3 done-when, on a throwaway kind cluster (two workers labelled `karpenter.sh/capacity-type=spot`, two `on-demand`, kube-prometheus-stack, the Actuator built from the tree, the working tree's Hub with a scratch database; deleted afterwards), with a rule `{kind: CronJob, spot: true}`: kube-state-metrics' `kube_cronjob_info` carries `cronjob`, as 16.3a read it; the Hub sent `nightly` (every minute, 150 s, `backoffLimit: 3`) and `fragile` (`backoffLimit: 0`); `SpotPlaced` on both, then `SpotSkipped` "its Jobs allow no retry" on `fragile`, whose runs were left alone; `nightly`'s first steered run labelled spot on a spot node; that node stopped and deleted mid-run, the Job counted the pod failed and its retry was admitted labelled on-demand with the `NotIn`, on an on-demand node though the other spot node had room (required, not a fallback: the Job's status showed its failed pod by the time the replacement came), and the run completed (1 failed, 1 succeeded); the next run preferred spot again; the Nodes data showed `fragile`'s reason and `nightly`'s pod on-demand, the report (`spot_runs`, the skip) arriving within minutes of the change; paused, a run was left alone, and under the Kill switch; after it, steered again. The Actuator logged no error. One thing seen, fixed after: a CronJob skipped got `SpotPlaced` a minute before its `SpotSkipped`, as the list arrives before the minute's read; now the read says one or the other, once, and a change again (31 mutations, all caught). Not checked live: the drain eviction's no-Rollback (it needs a Sizing held on a CronJob, which unit tests cover).
- 16.4 grilled 2026-11-16 (one round, all recommended): spot list prices win where found, the cluster's typed `discount` staying as the fallback (a price missing, a read failed, a typed-price cluster), so every Contract stays valid and priced, the price note saying which applied ("spot list on 2 of 3 nodes, x0.35 on 1"); only for clusters priced from `list_prices` (a typed price is a negotiated on-demand rate: set against a public spot price it compares two kinds of number), a typed cluster keeping its discount. AWS: `DescribeSpotPriceHistory` with the `aws` integration's own credentials (the client grants `ec2:DescribeSpotPriceHistory`; the guide names it), the region's average over the last 24 hours across its zones (a node's zone isn't read; per zone waits until a gap in the Bill check shows it matters), in USD only: a refused call or a Contract in another currency falls back to the discount, the price note saying why. Azure's Retail Prices API and GCP's catalog as on-demand prices are read. Stored as rows of their own beside the on-demand ones (the same key, spot), read daily, the last good price kept and its age shown. Split: 16.4a the spot list prices of the three clouds; 16.4b the bill's own spot part beside the estimate's (AWS `USAGE_TYPE` SpotUsage, Azure `PricingModel` Spot, GCP's `Spot Preemptible` SKUs), so a spot price can be checked against what the cloud charged (deferred from 16.1b).
- 16.4a built 2026-11-16: the glossary's *List price* first (a Spot node's own spot list price, else its discount). A table of its own, `spot_list_prices` (migration 0041; a spot row can never take an on-demand one's place, and an older Hub reads `list_prices` as it did), with `contract_id`. Asked of the Operator while building (2026-11-16): an AWS spot price is read with each Contract's own key and kept for it alone (no client's account prices another's nodes; its CloudTrail shows only its own), Azure's and GCP's shared, as their on-demand ones are; `store.spot_prices` gives a Contract the shared rows and its own, never another's. `listprices.azure` and `gcp` return the spot prices from the read they already make (Azure's meters named "... Spot", not Low Priority or Windows; GCP's `Preemptible` SKUs, a "Spot Preemptible" one winning over a legacy "Preemptible" one in either order); a group is read again when either of its rows is due, so a Hub upgraded with fresh on-demand prices reads its spot ones the next hour. `aws_spot`: `DescribeSpotPriceHistory` signed with the Contract's key (SigV4, `squero.servers.aws.authorization`), Linux/UNIX, `StartTime` a day back (the reply also has the price in effect then), its pages followed, averaged in each zone then across them; read with a pattern rather than an XML parser (a few known elements, and no parser error escaping the tick); only for a Contract on list prices with an `aws` integration, its clusters with `spot` read with the label, untyped, their spot nodes' types, in a region among the integration's `regions` (else refused, stored with why). `_from_list` prices each spot node at its spot list price (no deal on it) or its discount, node by node, the note saying which and why a fallback, and the spot rate against on-demand the effective one, which the Bill check, the candidates and a node that could go use. Chosen beyond the grill: the unreachable "AWS in USD only" fallback dropped (a Contract on AWS prices in USD, its validation says), and the own-row ordering (no key is ever both shared and a Contract's). Asked of the Operator too (2026-11-16): a refused AWS spot read (401 or 403, the permission not granted yet) waits a day, not an hour, so a client's audit trail gets one denied call a day rather than 24; other failures still wait an hour. Found in review while building: a size the cloud has no spot price for was retried hourly, its whole group with it (GCP's read is the whole catalog), now it waits the day like a price read; and a read of the nodes with a problem is skipped for AWS's spot read as `wanted_by_group` skips it. The IAM permission is in the Contract reference too. Tests for every branch, two Contracts in one region with the second's key refused (it falls back, and no request of its carries the first's key, nor the first's price reaches it); 28 mutations, all caught (one survived a first run: the spot and on-demand nodes in the test shared a type; now they don't), and 16.1a's and 16.1b's rerun, the deal's line anchored again. Checked live 2026-11-16, Azure: the real Retail Prices API read by `listprices.fetch` in eastus gave D2s_v3 0.096 on demand and 0.01919 Spot (0.20), D4s_v5 0.192 and 0.040531 (0.21), and B2s no Spot price (the B-series isn't sold as Spot: stored as none, the node at its discount); westeurope answered 429 at once, which the Hub stores and tries again within the hour. Not checked live yet, left for later by the Operator (2026-11-16) and still owed: GCP's catalog and AWS's spot price history, which need the sandboxes' keys, run by the Operator so the keys never reach the session: GCP, `listprices.fetch(http, "gcp", "us-central1", {"e2-standard-2": (2, 8), "n2-standard-4": (4, 16), "n1-standard-2": (2, 7.5)}, key)` with the catalog key, both maps printed (a Spot price, and the Spot SKU's naming, for each family); AWS, `listprices.aws_spot(http, "us-east-1", ["m5.large", "c5.xlarge", "t3.medium"], key_id, secret, time.time())` with `aiops-readonly`'s key once it has `ec2:DescribeSpotPriceHistory` (a 403 first is worth seeing too: the refusal's text and the day's wait).
- 16.4b built 2026-11-16: each billed day keeps what its spot lines cost (`billed_costs.spot_amount`, migration 0042; null for a day read before, the report then waiting for the next daily read), and each cluster's last spot read its failure (`billing_tries.spot_error`). Asked of the Operator while building (2026-11-16): the docs settle neither Azure's `PricingModel` grouping nor AWS's usage-type keys for spot, and as first built one grouped read gave both the total and its spot part, so a wrong guess would have darkened the Bill check that works live today; the spot part is now a read of its own after the total's, fail-soft: AWS's Cost Explorer grouped by `USAGE_TYPE` (a day's groups added across pages: one day's may continue on the next, the docs not saying otherwise), Azure's Cost Management grouped by `PricingModel` (a reply without that column refused), their failure stored and said ("its spot lines failed to read: ..."), the total saved as before and the last known spot part kept (one more call a day per billed cluster; Cost Explorer charges 0.01 USD a request). GCP's export query sums its `Spot Preemptible` and legacy `Preemptible` SKUs with the total: only fields it already reads, and a second query would scan the export twice (chosen beyond the Operator's answer, for GCP only). `billing.report` sets `spot_billed` (the window's) beside `spot_estimated` (the spot hours at a spot node's rate, as 16.1b prices them), in the API, the console's bill line, `sizing bill` and the Hub MCP's `bill`; no verdict of its own; a bill with spot lines on a cluster that doesn't name its spot nodes says so. Tests for every branch, two Contracts' spot parts kept apart; 21 mutations, all caught, and 16.1b's rerun whole. The Hub MCP's `bill` description changed (no Scenario uses it). Not checked live: the grouped replies of AWS's and Azure's spot reads and GCP's query, owed with 16.4a's (a wrong shape now costs the spot part only).
- Phases 15 and 16 checked live on AWS (Part A) 2026-11-16 04:12 to 12:32 UTC, about 2.3 USD of the Operator's credits (ceiling 5): a throwaway EKS 1.37 cluster in a sandbox account (two `t3.medium` system nodes, Karpenter 1.15.2 with one NodePool for spot and on-demand 2-vCPU types and its interruption queue, kube-prometheus-stack, the Hub 0.59.0 and its Postgres in the cluster on a synthetic Contract, the Actuator from `deploy/actuator`), every resource tagged for the sweep, torn down and swept empty (the spot service-linked role kept for Part B). 16.2b: `web` (10 replicas, ratio 0.7, minimum 2) had Karpenter put all ten on two spot nodes unsteered; restarted after the Hub's run, 3 pods required off spot made Karpenter launch an on-demand node, 7 kept the preference; scaled to 4, only spot replicas went. A real spot interruption, twice (AWS FIS, two minutes' notice): Karpenter read its queue about 25 s after the notice, tainted, drained and deleted the node; 16.3b's run evicted from it was retried required off spot and succeeded, its pod's `DisruptionTarget` reason `EvictionByEvictionAPI` (Karpenter's Event says "Forceful Termination"), the reason 16.3b's no-Rollback matches; `web`'s evicted spot pods fell back to the on-demand node and no spot node was launched (5 and 5 against 3 and 7 intended, shown honestly on the Nodes page: ADR 0006, a running pod is never moved). 15.1b: squero and Karpenter's `DisruptionBlocked` named the same `do-not-disrupt` pod. 15.2b, the phase's done-when on Karpenter: a managed node group of one `t3.small` labelled `pool=pinned` and tainted, the Contract's `placement` naming both; the Hub said "restart keeper to free" the on-demand node, the webhook gave the restarted pod the preference and the toleration (Event `Placed`), it landed on the pinned node, whose verdict was `pinned`. 16.4a's AWS spot price read gave m6a.large 0.02968, c6a.large 0.025071 and m5.large 0.026932 USD an hour, equal to the CLI's own read of the same day's history averaged per zone then across zones. Found: (1) 15.4's node fit sees no node constraints, so squero said a node could go where Karpenter refused, twice: a spot node whose pod preferred spot ("SpotToSpotConsolidation is disabled"; with only a pod without the preference left, Karpenter deleted the node) and the on-demand node whose pods had a `nodeSelector` ("Can't replace with a cheaper node"); Karpenter's consolidation runs its provisioning scheduler, a preference held as required while a new node could meet it (its source, `consolidation.go`, `scheduler.go`); spot-to-spot replacement needs the `SpotToSpotConsolidation` gate (alpha, off by default) and 15 cheaper instance types. (2) The node running the Actuator was "unknown (1 pod not visible)", its own pod outside the Contract's namespaces. (3) A CronJob running every 3 minutes under `consolidateAfter: 5m` kept its node from ever consolidating. (4) After a Contract change and a rollout, the hourly Blocker report named a pod already gone and gave no restart advice until the Actuator reported again. (5) With the Pinned nodes' label not allowlisted in kube-state-metrics (a setup slip, as `optimizer.md` warns), the pinned node was listed as one that could go. Not covered: a drain with a held Sizing (no usage history to make one).
- Part B checked live on AWS 2026-11-17 to 2026-11-21: a throwaway EKS cluster (us-east-2, Kubernetes 1.37, no NAT) with one on-demand t3.medium and one spot node (t3a.medium or t3.medium), every instance and volume tagged with a cost-allocation tag the Operator activated, Prometheus (kube-prometheus-stack) snapshotted and the cluster deleted after about 50 hours (about 8.5 USD of the Operator's 15); the Hub's own code then read the bill through the snapshot (`billing.refresh` and `billing.report`, the node facts and list prices as `sizing.store_run` and `listprices.refresh` make them), the key the Operator's, never in the session. **16.1b reconciled**: 19 November billed 1.2674 USD, estimated 1.2826 (+1.2%, 24 of 24 hours sampled); 18 to 19 November 2.5112 against 2.5652 (+2.1%); the on-demand node's BoxUsage line 0.9984, exactly 24 h at the 0.0416 list price, so the gap is the spot price. **16.4b confirmed on AWS**: Cost Explorer's grouped lines are `USE2-SpotUsage:<type>` (t3.medium and t3a.medium both seen) and `USE2-BoxUsage:<type>`, as the code reads them; spot billed 0.2690 against 0.2842 estimated on 19 November (+5.7%), 0.5144 against 0.5684 over the two days (+10.5%): the estimate takes today's spot price (0.01184 USD an hour for t3.medium, x0.285 of on-demand) where AWS billed about 0.0112 that day, and prices every spot hour at the latest spot node's type while 18 November ran partly on t3a.medium (node facts keep only the latest nodes). **16.4a on AWS**: the live spot price read works (0.01184 USD an hour for t3.medium in us-east-2), read with the sandbox's administrator user; the Hub's own read-only user lacked `ec2:DescribeSpotPriceHistory` and the Operator added it on 2026-11-21: IAM's policy simulator then allows it in us-east-2 and still denies it in us-east-1, where a region-restricting SCP of the sandbox's organization applies (the user's own policy allows both). Cost Explorer still marked the days Estimated when read (21 November 02:30 UTC). Found: (1) a setup slip: kube-state-metrics wasn't allowlisted `topology.kubernetes.io/region`, so the nodes had no region and list prices none; the check added the region from the zone label, for that read only; (2) the spot node was replaced twice by AWS in the 50 hours, Prometheus keeping the history; (3) the bill check script's command broke when copied over two lines (the Operator's terminal wrapped it): run from a file that is executable.
- *Done when:* replicas keep the ratio and the floor when a "spot" kind node is deleted.

### Audit fixes (13 slices, grilled 2026-11-16)

An audit of this roadmap on 2026-11-16 for anything found wrong and left open, with Part A's findings above. The Operator: fix every defect before continuing. Three rounds, all recommended. One slice each, in this order:

- **A1. One Prometheus under two names** (9.6b's guard compared URLs as text, `contracts._store`). The Hub asks each Prometheus who it is at startup (its external labels and runtime info) and treats a match as shared; where one can't answer, its host resolved and compared by address.
- A1 built 2026-11-16: `usage.ask_stores` asks each Direct cluster's Prometheus for `/api/v1/status/runtimeinfo` (5 s, all at once, each with its own cluster's token, redirects not followed) and takes `startTime` as its identity: Prometheus gives it to the nanosecond, Thanos Query too (no `hostname`); Mimir and VictoriaMetrics serve no runtime info (their sources and docs, read 2026-11-16), so they and a store that is down are compared by the addresses their host resolves to, with the same scheme, port and path. `contracts.regroup` checks the Contracts again: a match by identity is refused like a URL written the same, a match by address only marks the stores shared (the Operator's call, 2026-11-16: one ingress address often fronts several stores). Asking only joins: URLs written the same stay one store whatever they answer. Asked by the Hub at start and by `cli.contract` (the `squero` commands on one Contract); `contracts validate` stays offline and compares URLs. External labels dropped from the grill: they and `hostname` are often the same across clusters built from one chart, and `startTime` alone tells processes apart. A failure is logged as `listprices.why` writes it (no URL, no token), and stores joined by asking are logged by cluster (an HA pair's answer can change between starts). Tests for every branch, the two Contracts' tokens each sent only to their own store; 26 mutations, all caught; the full suite run with the real ask made to fail reached it nowhere. Known ceilings: replicas behind one name answer each for itself, so two names for an HA pair can still look like two; a Relay's cluster isn't asked. No tool description changed: no eval rerun.
- **A2. A title cut before masking** (`api.py`: a stored title cut at 200 characters, so a term cut in two went out as its fragment): mask first, then cut; a failing test first.
- A2 built 2026-11-16: a Question's title is now the Question whole (up to its 4,000 characters), cut where it is shown: the Hub MCP's `investigations` and `investigation` mask it first and cut at 200 after, as they already did an Answer at 500; the console's list and heading cut it at 200 (nothing masked there), and its exchange shows the first question whole, which it had cut at 200 too. Reaching beyond the Hub MCP, the same title went to the model in a Teaching's draft for an Investigation with no Report (masked on the wire, whole now). Migration 0043 gives the Questions stored cut their whole title back from their input. Agenda items keep the title whole, masked whole on the Hub MCP. Failing tests first (the Hub MCP sent `" ac"` out of `acme`; the console cut the question), then the fix; 7 mutations, 6 caught and one equivalent (the migration's `kind` guard: no other kind's input has a question). Masking per Contract unchanged, so the isolation tests stand. No tool description changed: no eval rerun.
- **A3. The node fit sees node constraints** (Part A finding 1). The Actuator reports each of the Contract's pods' `nodeSelector` and required node affinity, and under Karpenter its preferred node affinity (held as required: the Hub can't see whether a NodePool could meet it, so it errs strict), with the labels of the nodes those keys name; 15.4's fit places a pod only on a node that matches, a label it can't see counting as no match. Karpenter's rules list names the spot-to-spot gate and its 15 types.
- A3 split 2026-11-16 (the Operator's call): the Actuator can't read nodes today (its ClusterRole has pods and workloads only), so A3a is the Hub's side and A3b the Actuator's, which reads the pods' needs and, through a new optional ClusterRole `squero-actuator-nodes` (`list nodes`, built without the `get` the grill named: listing is all it does; asked of the Operator: Phase 15 had promised no new permission), the nodes' values of the label keys those name, nothing else of a node. Without it a cluster lists no node that could go and says why. The restart advice of Placement goes by the pods' requirements.
- A3a built 2026-11-16: the glossary's Node that could go first. An Actuator's report may carry, when the Hub asks (`Desired.constraints`, sent under the Kill switch too), `constraints` (`read`, `cut` or `nodes_refused`, a code: the Hub writes the sentence), each distinct `Need` once (its nodeSelector, its required node affinity's terms, its preferred terms with their weights: facts, not a verdict), `needed` (`namespace/pod` to its need) and each node's `labels` (only the keys the needs name); `sent()` leaves them out unless read, so an older Hub takes the report, and `unreal` refuses a key, value, node or pod name of no shape Kubernetes allows, or a need that isn't there. `blockers.fit` reads them as the scheduler does (one term of several, every expression of it, with the nodeSelector; a label the node lacks meets `NotIn` and `DoesNotExist` only; `Gt` and `Lt` on integers; `matchFields` on the node's name; an empty term meets nothing), adding under Karpenter its heaviest preferred terms, which its scheduler holds as required while any node, a new one too, could meet it (`NewPodRequirements`, read 2026-11-16: it sorts by weight with `sort.Slice`, unstable, so every term of the heaviest weight is held; it reads no `matchFields`, which are kept, the scheduler binding by them); a node whose labels weren't sent takes no pod that needs something. 15.4's fit places a pod only where it fits, and the restart advice by its requirements alone (a restarted pod is placed by the scheduler, where a preference only scores, whoever consolidates); a report without them (an Actuator from before A3b, one cut short, or one refused the nodes) counts no node, the Nodes page (`could_go.note`), the Hub MCP's `nodes` and the console saying why. Karpenter's settings to check name `--feature-gates SpotToSpotConsolidation` (alpha, false by default; on, a single spot node replaced by a cheaper spot node when 15 cheaper types could take its pods). Part A's three refusals are the first tests (a pod preferring spot under Karpenter and not Cluster Autoscaler, a `nodeSelector` on Karpenter's NodePool label, `keeper`'s `role NotIn [system]`), then every operator; two Contracts' reports stored apart; 48 mutations, all caught. The tests were written beside the code, the mutations standing in for a failing-first run. The Hub MCP's `nodes` description changed; no Scenario uses the Hub MCP, so no eval rerun. Until A3b ships, a cluster's Nodes page lists none that could go, saying its Actuator doesn't report what its pods need.
- A3b built 2026-11-16: when the Hub asks, the Actuator's hourly report carries what each of its pods that would move needs of a node (`actuator.need_of`: its `nodeSelector` and node affinity as written, required terms with their `matchFields`, preferred terms with their weights, the webhook's own among them; `stays` is now the one test of a pod that stays with its node, shared with the Blocker facts), each distinct need once, and every node's values of the label keys those name (`constrained`), listed a page at a time through the optional ClusterRole `squero-actuator-nodes` (`list nodes` only; no need, no node read). Codes, never text: `nodes_refused` (403), `nodes_unread` (any other failure: a new code the Hub now words, "its last read of the nodes' labels failed"), `cut` (over 10,000 pods' needs, 1000 distinct ones, 5000 nodes, 100 label keys, a need over its own limits, or the megabyte, the needs dropped before any pod); a namespace whose read failed sends none of its needs. `client-cluster.md` and `safety.md` say what a Node object holds and what of it leaves the cluster. Tests for every branch, Part A's NodePool case end to end from pod objects to the Hub's fit, the RBAC manifest's role and binding, and the integration test now lists a real cluster's nodes a page at a time through the client the Actuator runs with; 29 mutations, 28 caught (one by hanging the fake API's paging, which fails the run) and one equivalent (reading needs the Hub didn't ask for, sent nowhere). With A3a, the cluster's Nodes page counts nodes that could go again.
- **A4. Blockers reported when they change** (findings 2 and 4): the Actuator checks every 5 minutes and reports when the set it would report changes, the hourly report kept as the heartbeat; it reports its own pods as known, never Blockers.
- A4 grilled further 2026-11-16 (the Operator's calls, all recommended): the Actuator can't read its own pod (its pod reads are bound in the Contract's namespaces only), so it reads it through a new optional Role in its own namespace (`get pods`), and its pod is judged by the same rules as any pod's, not "never a Blocker": as shipped, its `tmp` emptyDir blocks under Cluster Autoscaler and `kubectl drain`, so the manifest names it in `safe-to-evict-local-volumes`.
- A4 built 2026-11-16: the glossary's Blocker first. The Actuator reads its Blockers' facts every 5 minutes (`BLOCKERS_CHECK`) and sends them when they differ, their time aside, from the last report the Hub answered (taken, or refused: an older Hub's refusal isn't sent again each read), and once an hour whatever they are; one that didn't reach the Hub goes again 5 minutes on. A rollout's new pods reach the Hub within 5 minutes (finding 4). When the Hub asks (`Desired.constraints`, as for A3), the report carries `itself`: its own pod (`SQUERO_ACTUATOR_POD`, from the downward API), read by name through the optional Role `squero-actuator-self` (`get pods` in its own namespace, where nothing else runs), with the facts any pod's has, its containers' requests and its need as an index into `needs`, never a key of `needed` (whose namespaces must be the cluster's). The Hub takes it from outside the cluster's namespaces, refusing one of no shape an Actuator sends (`itself`), and `blockers.known` counts it on its node where the node has a pod the Hub can't see and the read was made 5 minutes after it started at least (`SEEN`: an older read can't hold it, and counting it there would hide another pod), never for a pod of the cluster's namespaces; judged by the rules (finding 2), moved by `could_go` with its requests and its need. The manifest names its `tmp` in `safe-to-evict-local-volumes`, so under Cluster Autoscaler its node can go; under `kubectl drain` it blocks, 15.6's change saying how. Without the Role its node stays unknown, as before. Tests for every branch, two Contracts' own pods stored apart, the manifests' Role, binding, env and annotation; 31 mutations, all caught. The Hub MCP's descriptions unchanged: no eval rerun. A test joins the halves as finding 2 was seen: its pod object through `blocking`, `sent` and the Hub's model to a clear verdict, and without the Role to the same unknown. Known ceilings: a PodDisruptionBudget a client adds in the Actuator's namespace isn't read (the Role reads pods only); a read twelve times an hour lists the namespaces' pods and PodDisruptionBudgets (and the nodes, where a pod needs something of one); `started` is the cluster's clock and the read's time the Hub's, so `SEEN` absorbs a skew under 5 minutes only; a report up to two hours old places its pod on the node it last named, if the Actuator has since gone down and left it. Clients apply the manifests again (env, Role, annotation): a new image alone leaves its node unknown. Not checked live: owed on kind (free), the real Role and env, kube-state-metrics' real counts, and a rollout seen by the Hub within 5 minutes.
- **A5. No node could go without the Pinned nodes' label** (finding 5): a cluster with Placement where no node shows the label lists none, saying why. `optimizer.md` says finding 3 next to Karpenter's rules (squero can't read `consolidateAfter`; a note on every CronJob's node would warn without knowing it).
- A5 built 2026-11-16: the glossary's Node that could go first. Where a cluster has Placement and its read shows no Pinned node (no node carries the label as kube-state-metrics shows it, or the read was made before the label, or with another), `nodes_report` counts no node as one that could go, now or after the Sizings, and says why (`could_go.note`, shown on the Nodes page and by the Hub MCP's `nodes`), beside the Placement note that says how to fix it; this reason replaces A3's where both apply. Finding 3 is a line of Karpenter's settings to check (`consolidateAfter` counts from a node's last pod added or removed, Karpenter's docs for v1.15 read 2026-11-16: "Karpenter resets this timer whenever a pod is added to or removed from the node"; so a CronJob running more often keeps its node) and of `optimizer.md`: the Hub can't read a NodePool, so it names the setting and warns on no CronJob's node. Tests for both causes and for a cluster whose pinned node is read; 4 mutations, all caught. No tool description changed: no eval rerun. Not checked live: the tests build the read by hand, a read with no node carrying the label, as Part A's was.
- A5b decided and built 2026-11-16 (the Operator's call, the first of three options, after A5's review found it): with the label unseen, a Pinned node can't be told, so it read `blocked` and offered, for its placed pods' Blockers, the change that would undo what keeps them there. Now, where Placement is on and no node shows the label, a Blocker of a Workload the Hub places gets no change and the note "its node may be a Pinned node, which keeps it on purpose: no node shows <label>" (on the Nodes page and the Hub MCP's `nodes`); other Blockers keep theirs, and the states stay as they are (rejected: every node unknown, which hides ordinary nodes' real Blockers; and words in the docs only). The restart advice already said why not. Tests with the label unseen, seen and Placement off; 4 mutations, all caught. No tool description changed: no eval rerun.
- A6 grilled further 2026-11-16 (the Operator's calls, all recommended): split into A6a (the bill) and A6b (the Agenda); the hours compared are RAM GiB-hours, not cores (a shared-core E2 machine bills a fraction of a core while Kubernetes reports 2 vCPU); the Agenda leaves out every Workload with a current Approved Sizing on a cluster that runs an Actuator, applied or not.
- A6a built 2026-11-16: the glossary's Bill first. GCP's export query also sums each day's RAM GiB-hours (`usage.amount_in_pricing_units` of the `Instance Ram running` SKUs, GiB-hours as 10.5c's live check priced them: 192 for an e2-standard-2's day), stored per day (`billed_costs.ram_hours`, migration 0044; null for AWS, Azure and a day read before), and `usage.node_hours` gives each UTC day's sampled GiB-hours from the same read. A window with a day billed under 95% of its sampled GiB-hours, read (the cluster's last good read of its bill) less than 3 days after that day ended (`SETTLES`), is still settling: the gap shown, no verdict (so not on the Agenda; the CLI's "no verdict", which isn't a failure), a note naming each such day with both figures. Not when every day is short: that is a real gap, a label missing from nodes, judged as usual; nor a short day read later. Narrower than the grill's "a day under 95%" in those two guards, so that a missing label can't hide a gap for good (the first build, the newest day only and the day before it not short, was widened after review: the bill is read once a day, so two days read soon after they ended can both be short). Tests for each case at the edges, and one from BigQuery's answer through the daily read to the report; 10 mutations, all caught (one by a test added for it: exactly 95%). No tool description changed: no eval rerun. Known ceilings: a one-day window short is every day short, so judged; hourly samples count a short-lived node whole or not at all, so churn alone can make a fresh day read short (no verdict until the next read). Not checked live: the RAM SKUs' hours read from a real export (owed with 16.4a's GCP check).
- A6b built 2026-11-16: the glossary's Agenda first. The waste ranking leaves out each Workload with a current Approved Sizing (`store.approved_sizings`, so until the next hourly run reads its cluster) of any of its containers, on a cluster with an `actuator_token_secret`, applied or not; the next ones rise into the three. One line last (kind `approved`, no link) counts the wasting Workloads left out ("2 Workloads approved: their waste is read again within the hour"); an approved Workload with no waste was never ranked and isn't counted. `counts()` (the Hub MCP's `now()` across Contracts) leaves that line out: it counts Workloads, not something to look at. Tests for each branch and one with two Contracts; 10 mutations, all caught. The API's Agenda kind gained `approved` (console types regenerated). Only the Hub MCP's `now` description changed, which the Investigation agent never sees: no eval rerun. Known ceilings: an approval the Actuator won't apply (its container Reverted since, a kind the Actuator doesn't know, one changing only types to an Actuator that applies none) still leaves the ranking until the next hourly run; a Contract whose only item is that line no longer says "Nothing needs you now".
- A7 split 2026-11-17 into A7a (the reports) and A7b (what supersedes an approval), with a stop between: A7b keeps an approval current for longer, which the Operator sees before it is built.
- A7a built 2026-11-17: the glossary's Actuation first. `/actuator/reports` refuses, whole (422, naming them), a report of a kind that isn't one the Actuator resizes (`actuator.KINDS`): an honest Actuator reports only the kinds the Hub sent it, which are those (checked over every test that builds an Actuation: only one feeding the Actuator a Job Wanted directly, which the Hub never sends; the tests' fake Hub now asserts it). And it stores at most 1,000 reports from a cluster an hour (`HOURLY_REPORTS`), counted by `received_at` (the Hub's clock, never the Actuator's `at`) in each insert, so a report sent again is no new one and is never refused: past the 1,000 it stores a POST's first ones, logs and tells as before for those, then answers 429 with a warning line, and the Actuator (since 12.9a) keeps them all and sends them again; a POST of only stored ones is taken (204), so a lost answer can't stall it for the hour. An index serves the count (migration 0045). Tests at the edges (the 1,000th, the hour exactly), each cluster and Contract its own hour, and one from the Actuator's `report()` through the Hub's app and back; 11 mutations, all caught. No tool the agent sees changed: no eval rerun. Known ceilings: two POSTs at once from one cluster can each see room, so a few more than 1,000 may be stored; an Actuator keeps its newest 1,000 unsent, so an honest burst above 2,000 an hour (a mass approval or Revert on a big cluster) loses its oldest reports. Its full run found a race in a 12.9c test, fixed: a late Rollback's line says when the Hub received it, which the test took from its own clock a moment before, so at a minute's end the two named different minutes; it now reads the stored time.
- A7b grilled 2026-11-17 (the Operator's call, recommended): Sizing runs are pruned (hourly kept 14 days, then each day's last for 400) and approvals never are, so a supersession computed from stored Sizings would come back once its run was pruned: it is kept on the approval instead.
- A7b built 2026-11-17: the glossary's Approved Sizing first. An Approved Sizing is superseded by a later Sizing of its container, never by a run that read its cluster without it: each run, as it stores its rows, marks the earlier approvals of the containers it sized (`approved_sizings.superseded_run`, migration 0046, indexed for those still current), and one approved from a run whose container a later run already sized is stored superseded. Approvals before it keep their answer: the migration marks each with the first later run that read its cluster. Current approvals are listed in a fixed order (approved, then cluster, namespace, kind, name, container), which ties left to the plan before. Tests: each run that supersedes nothing (going, failing, without the container, the same name in another cluster, another Contract's), pruning every run after, an older run approved late, the migration's backfill; three Actuator tests that superseded by runs without rows now store the run as the tick does. An approval whose container no run sizes again stays current for good, so A6b's Agenda now matches approvals per container: one of a container its Workload no longer has hides nothing (tested). The Actuator says such an approval once and keeps it as done by its run (tested over four minutes, the Workload deleted and the container renamed). 13 mutations, all caught; two equivalent, said: `run_id <` against `<=` (a run's own approvals come after it is stored) and keeping the first superseding run (only whether it is null is read). No tool the agent sees changed: no eval rerun. Known ceilings: an approval committed while a run's rows are being stored can miss both marks, and stays current until the next run that sizes its container; an approval whose container is never sized again (its Workload deleted or renamed, or the container gone from its template) stays current and in its Actuator's desired state; the Actuator reports it once ("no such Deployment") and keeps it as done, by its run, so it is never applied twice, nor to a Workload made later under the same name while that Actuator keeps its ConfigMap.
- A8 built 2026-11-17. **A Step-up's pods left**: a pod brought to a Step-up whose node had no room (its resize left pending) is put back and left, as 13.1c made it, and now tried again every `STEP_EVERY` (10 minutes, from when it was left) while the Step-up lasts, so within its cap and before its term ends; a retry is unsaid, put back again or given it, as is a pod left again (`tried`, forgotten with the pod): said once, when first left. Tested over the ten minutes' edge, a retry put back, one given at last, and the pod gone; 9 mutations, all caught (`>=` against `>` at the 10 minutes is equivalent at a clock's resolution). **A Triage's line said once**: not built, the Operator's call (Q27, recommended): no path ends a Triage twice (a follow-up is a Question's only, the store's claim checks the kind; a Triage leaves `running` only paused or ended; it is saved before it is told), and one that did would post its Report twice as well, so the guard, if one is ever needed, belongs around the whole reply. No tool the agent sees changed: no eval rerun.
- A9 grilled 2026-11-17 (the Operator's calls, all recommended): each node's percentile by node, not by pod (kube-state-metrics' `kube_pod_info`, the Contract's own pod series, so none of the node reads' guards; a pod's node unknown, its own); 15.3a's risk `type_needs_more_cpu` removed, since a type can no longer need more than the pooled Sizing.
- A9 built 2026-11-17: the glossary's Sizing first. A DaemonSet's container keeps its CPU samples by node (`ContainerUsage.nodes`; each instance type's the same), and its CPU target is the largest of each node's own weighted percentile; memory, a peak, already fit the busiest. Every other kind is pooled as before. The pod-to-node read is made only where a DaemonSet has samples, once per window. `type_needs_more_cpu` and `_short_of` removed with their tests (stored rows carrying it show it until the next run; the API's risk kind is a string, so no schema change). 15.3a's fixture of thirty nodes, one busy, now sizes the pooled CPU at the busy node's 1150m (it was 58m). Tests: by node against by pod and pooled (a quiet fleet, a busier node, a pod a rollout replaced after minutes at a core, a pod with no node), each type the same way, the read scoped to the Contract's namespaces, other kinds unchanged with no read; 9 mutations, all caught. The agent's `sizing` tool shows the new numbers but its description and schema are unchanged: no eval rerun. Known ceiling: a node that ran a DaemonSet's pod for only minutes (one just added) sets the target if its minutes were busy: the busiest node's, by design.
- A10 grilled 2026-11-17 (the Operator's call, recommended): the half applies only on a Hub with two Contracts or more (counted at start, when Contracts load); one Contract keeps every slot, at the default 3 its Triages and Checks otherwise running one at a time with no one to keep the others for.
- A10 built 2026-11-17: each Contract has its own semaphore of half `SQUERO_MAX_CONCURRENT_TRIAGES` (rounded down, at least one), taken before a shared slot, so its Investigations beyond its share wait in its own queue and hold no slot another Contract could use; Triages, Questions and Checks alike (they share the slots). Tested at 4, 3, 2 and 1 slots with two Contracts and 3 with one, the agent's start held open so each Contract's running count is read; 6 mutations, 5 caught, one equivalent (without the "at least one", one slot gives a share of none, which turns the share off, and the one slot already holds every Contract to one). No tool the agent sees changed: no eval rerun. Known ceiling: per process, like the slots themselves.
- A11 grilled 2026-11-17 (the Operator's calls, all recommended): split into A11a (the memory cap, a pod's Workload, a CronJob's newest run, the stale line) and A11b (a rollback in `recent_changes`, proven by a real `rollout undo` in CI's kind job, since which of the Deployment's condition times moves on one can't be shown with fakes). The memory suggestion with a `max` at or below what it was killed at gives no numbers, only why: a suggestion that raises nothing after an OOM kill is wrong. A pod's Workload comes from its owners through a new `workload` field on `list_pods`, the name rule kept for an older Relay. A CronJob between runs takes its newest run's requests and limits for everything (Waste, cost and Realized Savings), as live pods already do.
- A11a built 2026-11-17. **The agent's memory suggestion** after an OOM kill is capped at the policy's memory `max`, said in its `why`; with the `max` at or below the limit (or peak) it was killed at it has no numbers, only "raise the max, or look for a leak" (13.1's stale comment gone). **Deploy verification's Workloads**: `list_pods` names each pod's `workload` as kind/name from its owners and labels, with the Actuator's own `workload_of` (a ReplicaSet's pod its Deployment's or Rollout's, a CronJob's run its CronJob's; otherwise its controller, or the pod itself), and `deploys.unhealthy` uses it, so a StatefulSet's or DaemonSet's pod is no longer cut to a wrong name; a Relay without the field is read by the old name rule. **A CronJob between runs** asks what its newest run asked (by `kube_pod_created`, then name) and counts as read from live pods, so a request lowered from now on realizes after its next run (one lowered before isn't counted: no backfill); any other container with no live pod keeps the window's largest. `development.md`'s run 4→5 note now names `two-clusters-alert-picks-one`, which passed in every run of the 2026-11-10 comparison. No tool's name, description or schema and no prompt changed (`list_pods` returns one more field in its entries), so no eval rerun. Tested over the cap (below, at and above it, with and without a limit, and through the tool's policy), each owner shape (a CronJob's run among them), and a CronJob's newest run against a lone Job's and a Deployment's, never another namespace's or cluster's pod of the same name; 17 mutations, 16 caught, one equivalent (`to > cap` against `to >= cap + 1`, apart only under a byte). A11b next.
- A11b built 2026-11-17. **A rollout undo** shows in `recent_changes` as `change: "rollout undo"` under its new revision, with its diff from the revision it replaced. The ReplicaSet it reuses is told by the controller's `deployment.kubernetes.io/revision-history` annotation, not by being older than the revision below it (that one may be pruned), and keeps its first creation time, so the current one is timed by its Deployment's `Progressing` condition's update time while that names it, quoted (its transition time stays put while the condition stays True); the Deployment is matched by its owner uid, listed one allowed namespace at a time like the ReplicaSets. An older undo, superseded since, keeps its creation time, as does one whose condition names another ReplicaSet or has no update time. The value is `rollout undo`, not the grilled `rollback`, because Rollback is the Actuator's own undo of a Sizing (`CONTEXT.md`'s Rollback now says so). Deploy verification gets a new revision, so it queues a Check for an undo as for any rollout. `contracts.md`'s known limit and the ponytail comment are gone. No tool's name, description or schema and no prompt changed (one more `change` value), so no eval rerun. Tested with fakes (the condition naming it, another ReplicaSet, one whose name only starts with it, another Deployment of the same name, none, one with no status, a superseded undo, no update time, and the Deployment list never outside the allowlist or on another Contract's cluster) and by a kind test in CI's integration job: a throwaway Deployment's real `kubectl rollout undo` onto a ReplicaSet over a minute old shows in a one-minute window as revision 3 (passed on the Mac's kind cluster, 112 s). 9 mutations, 9 caught. A11 done; A12 next.
- A12 built 2026-11-17 (the Operator's calls: all recommended, then effort medium, then Opus). Diagnosed from the 2026-11-10 recordings for free: Sonnet 5.5 named the Lambda's timeout cut without why 1 s fails (the 1.2 s lookup sits past the log result's cut, behind `read_more`), and on Questions kept calling tools after it could answer; one run lost a call to `read_more`'s `fields` as a string, one ended without a report and the eval had dropped its calls. Built: a Triage-prompt line (say why a changed setting's new value fails, from what the workload needed before), two Question-prompt lines (runbooks and past incidents only for a fix or a past incident; answer once you can), `read_more` taking `fields` as one comma-separated string, and `TriageError` carrying its call log so the eval keeps it. Sonnet's four failing Scenarios rerun three times: at high, Lambda and `question-unhealthy-workloads` 3 of 3 but `question-checkout-rollout` 0 and `gcp-who-deployed` 1 (up to 28 calls); at medium 5 of 12. So **Opus 5.5 at medium is the Hub's default** (`config.py`, `.env.example`, `deploy/base`, `hub.env.example` with its price), the roadmap's fallback: 22 Scenarios × 3 passed 66 of 66 (5.4 calls, 18 s). Tested: `read_more` with a string of fields, a Run without a report keeping its calls through `squero eval`; 6 mutations, 6 caught (the prompt lines are proven by the evals). Spent about $45 to $65 in evals (no token counts recorded). Why Sonnet loops was not found (two efforts measured). Effort isn't pinned: Opus runs at its own default, medium, and `SQUERO_MODEL_EFFORT` would apply to any model a Hub names. A Hub whose env names Sonnet keeps it until changed; one relying on the default runs Opus after upgrading and refuses to start while a budgeted Contract has no Opus price. A13 next.
- A13 built 2026-11-17. The weekly digest has one line on the week's Actuations, as decided 2026-11-10: `Actuator this week: 5 resized, 1 rolled back.` Each outcome that changed something (applied as resized, rolled back, reverted, failed, a Step-up, a Burst) counts the containers it reached in the 7 days up to the digest's time, so an Actuation reported again or by a later run counts once and the same name in another cluster apart; skipped, an OOM kill or throttling seen, a Step-up's or Burst's end and handed back are left out, and with none of the counted ones there is no line. Read from the Contract's own stored Actuations (`store.actuations`). No tool, prompt or API changed, so no eval rerun or console check. Tested: every outcome, a repeat, another cluster, the line's order, none at all; the week's edges and another Contract's Actuations through the store; 11 mutations, 11 caught. **The audit fixes are done (A1 to A13).**
- **A6. GCP's bill still settling** (10.5c's possible follow-up): a day with fewer billed hours than sampled hours shows as still settling, not outside. Then **the Agenda** (11.2b ranked by waste cost alone, changed): a Workload whose current Sizing is Approved and waiting for its Actuator leaves the waste ranking, counted on one line ("3 approved, waiting").
- **A7. The Actuator's reports hardened** (12.9c's offer, built): `kind` checked against the kinds it resizes; at most 1,000 stored Actuations per cluster an hour, the rest refused with 429 and an audit line. Then **what supersedes an approval** (the reviewer's note, changed to the glossary): a later Sizing of that container, never a run where the container is missing.
- **A8. Reflex leftovers** (13.x's "not done"): a Step-up brought back in place is tried again each `STEP_EVERY`, within its cap and expiry; a Triage's line is said once.
- **A9. A DaemonSet's CPU fits the busiest** (15.3's finding): its pooled CPU target is the largest of each node's own percentile, as its memory already fits the busiest peak; 15.3c's per-type Sizings keep the quiet types' share small.
- **A10. A Contract takes at most half the Triage slots** (`AIOPS_MAX_CONCURRENT_TRIAGES`, at least 1), its others waiting in their own queue, so one Contract's alert storm can't hold every slot.
- **A11. Ceilings whose code names their fix**: the agent's memory suggestion capped by the memory max (`servers/sizing.py`, 13.1 has shipped); deploy verification's Workload from the pod's owner references, not its name (`deploys.workload`); a rollback's time from the Deployment's conditions (`servers/kubernetes.py`); a CronJob's realized Savings from its newest pod (`sizing.py`); `development.md`'s stale line on choosing between clusters.
- **A12. The eval regression on the default model** (`aws-lambda-timeout-cut` failed 3 of 3 on Sonnet 5.5, the Hub's default, which also missed `gcp-who-deployed` and the two Questions; Opus 5.5 at medium passed all): diagnosed from the recorded runs first (free), the prompt or tool descriptions fixed, then Sonnet rerun with the cost asked first; Opus at medium as the default only if that can't close the gap.
- **A13. The week's resizes in the weekly digest** (12.4's "resizes in the daily digest", decided 2026-11-10 as one line in the weekly digest and not built since; added 2026-11-16): one line summing the week's Actuations.
- **A14. Stale notices** (added 2026-11-20, from a live gap: the lab cluster the mini PC's Hub reads was down from 15 to 21 November, and only the weekly digest of 16 November said so). Grilled 2026-11-20 (two rounds, all recommended): when one of the Agenda's stale kinds (a cluster's Sizing reads failing, a Relay offline, a cluster's Bill reads failing) has lasted 3 hours without a break (three missed hourly runs), the Hub posts a **Stale notice** to the Contract's `operator_channel` (none without Slack: the Agenda stays); one thread per cluster and kind, so each closes on its own; the first post names the Contract, the cluster, what is failing, since when and the error as the Agenda shows it (never a secret or a credential URL), with a link to the Now panel when `SQUERO_CONSOLE_URL` is set; a reminder a day in its thread while it fails ("still failing, 3 days now"), and a reply when it reads again ("reads again, after 5 days 8 hours"); each post claimed in Postgres, so a restart neither repeats nor loses one. A new glossary term, since an Alert is what the Hub receives. The Hub itself going down is not covered: Deferred.
- A14 built 2026-11-20: `notices.py`: `failing()`, the Agenda's source of stale items too (moved there, its titles unchanged), so the Now panel and the notices agree; `tick()` per Contract with Slack, every 10 minutes beside the scheduler's tick while the Hub has a bot token (`due_notices`, one Contract failing stops none: tested). Migration 0047: `stale_notices` (Contract, cluster, kind, since, the thread's ts, when last told); a Bill's failure counts from when the Hub first saw it (its store keeps no start), a Sizing read's and a Relay's from their own. The first notice once 3 hours have passed (escaped, with the console link), a reminder in its thread a day later, a reply when it reads again, a row whose cluster is no longer read ended quietly; each post claimed before it is sent (a conditional update), a refused one undone and tried at the next look. As built, not "never lost": a crash between a claim and its post delays it a day, and a refused recovery reply is not tried again. Isolation: two Contracts failing on a cluster of one name keep their channels, threads and rows apart (tested, and each statement's Contract clause mutated on its own). No API change. Not tested: the 10-minute cadence of the scheduler loop. 23 mutations, all caught. It reaches the mini PC's Hub with the next release and upgrade.
- Not code: the owed live checks (this roadmap's "not checked live" and "owed" lines) are batched per cloud after these, each with its cost; the Backtest waits for its date (14.4).

### Phase 17: GPU and LLM inference (5 slices, 17.1 and 17.2 grilled 2026-11-20)

- 17.1 DCGM ingestion. 17.2 Idle GPUs and per-pod attribution with cost. 17.3 vLLM metrics and memory-reservation Sizings. 17.4 GPU replica floors from queue and KV-cache signals. 17.5 GPU node consolidation. 17.6 (GPU sharing) and 17.7 (batch inference) went to Deferred in the grill.
- *Done when:* an idle synthetic GPU Workload is flagged with its cost from fixtures; real GPUs only in a paid sandbox the Operator approves.
- Grilled 2026-11-20 (three rounds, all recommended), 17.1 and 17.2 only; 17.3 to 17.5 are grilled once real DCGM data has been seen:
  - **NVIDIA only.** What a container asks for from kube-state-metrics' requests (`resource="nvidia_com_gpu"`), what it uses from NVIDIA's DCGM exporter. Other vendors' exporters wait for a Contract that has them. Both forms of a metric are read: the exporter's current default counters give 0 to 1 ratios (`DCGM_FI_DEV_GPU_UTIL_RATIO`, `DCGM_FI_PROF_GR_ENGINE_UTIL_RATIO`), older ones `DCGM_FI_DEV_GPU_UTIL` as 0 to 100. Its pod, namespace and container labels are read as written and with the `exported_` prefix, as for kube-state-metrics; their exact names are confirmed by the paid check below.
  - **Idle GPU, a term of its own, not Waste.** A GPU a container requests whose busiest hour in its Sizing policy's window stays under 5% busy: the profiling metric (graphics engine active) where the exporter has it, GPU utilization otherwise. Memory used is shown beside it, never counted. Measured on the busiest hour so a nightly batch is not called idle. The window and history are the container's policy's; `off` excludes it. Waste keeps its meaning (requests above Sizings), so CPU and memory totals don't move; an Idle GPU's cost is a line of its own beside it.
  - **Counted per GPU, grouped by Workload** ("3 of 4 GPUs idle, 1,240 USD/month"), so a partly idle Workload shows. A GPU shared by time-slicing, and a MIG instance, is not judged and is said so ("shared: not judged"); pricing a pod's share belongs with GPU sharing. GPUs on a node that no container requests are 17.5's.
  - **A GPU's price**: a typed `gpu` (per GPU-hour, one per cluster) wins; otherwise from list prices, the node's price less its vCPUs and GiB at the rates of the cluster's nodes without GPUs, divided by its GPUs, so GPU nodes leave the CPU and memory blend. A cluster of GPU nodes only, without a typed `gpu`, shows its GPUs unpriced with a note to add `prices.gpu`; one with several GPU models uses list prices. The Bill check's estimate adds the GPU-hours at that price, so each node's price still adds back exactly.
  - **Report only.** Idle GPUs with their cost on the Workloads and Cost pages, in the digest and through the Hub MCP server; nothing is actuated (an in-place resize covers CPU and memory only). A Proposed change lowering a GPU request waits until the flags are trusted. The agent's `sizing_for_workload` gets them in a slice of its own, with its eval rerun.
  - **Checked** on fixtures (`FakePrometheus` with DCGM's metric names and labels), then one short paid check to confirm the labels before the phase counts as done: a spot `g4dn.xlarge` with the GPU Operator for about 2 hours, about 2 USD, asked for separately.
- 17.1 built 2026-11-20: GPU use read, with each GPU attributed to its pod's container (the attribution moved here from 17.2, since scoping the read by namespace needs it); 17.2 keeps the verdict, the cost, storing and the surfaces. A container's GPU request from the requests read already made (the window's largest; nothing more is asked where none requests one), widened while building from the grilled `nvidia_com_gpu` to the resources that count GPUs: `nvidia_com_gpu_shared` (time-sliced, renamed by the device plugin, marked shared) and MIG profiles (`nvidia_com_mig_*`, one instance each, marked shared by DCGM's `GPU_I_ID`), so those containers are said "shared: not judged" rather than unseen; a sharing scheduler's memory and core quantities (HAMi's `nvidia_com_gpumem`, `gpucores`) are not GPUs and stay out; then DCGM's hourly means (one range query per metric name and label case at a one-hour step), the busiest hour taken in code, by the graphics engine where DCGM has it, else GPU utilization, the deprecated percent and MiB forms scaled; peak memory, shown only; the hours read and the pod last seen on each GPU; MIG instances and virtual GPUs marked shared. Facts from the exporter's code and chart, not recall: current counters are 0 to 1 ratios (`_RATIO`) and bytes, the deprecated ones percent and MiB (the engine's older name, `DCGM_FI_PROF_GR_ENGINE_ACTIVE`, is from the docs' example, a ratio too); its pod labels are `pod`, `namespace`, `container` (old: `pod_name`, `pod_namespace`, `container_name`); its chart's ServiceMonitor has `honorLabels: false`, so they arrive as `exported_*`; the chart turns the Kubernetes mapping on (`kubernetes: auto`), the binary's flag being off by default. Isolation: three label cases, each scoped by its own namespace label, plain `namespace` only with neither of the others on the series; tested, with both the `exported_*` and the old names, with a Contract that owns the exporter's namespace and a GPU pod there. A failed read is a note, the Sizings kept. A Relay passes the queries on as they are. `squero sizing usage` shows them. 22 mutations, all caught (one, the request's largest value, survived until its test asked for 2 GPUs; each isolation matcher mutated on its own). Not stored and not in the API, so no console types and no eval rerun. Not checked live: the label names on a real exporter (the paid check above).
- 17.2 split while building (2026-11-20) into three slices, each a commit: 17.2a the verdict, stored, priced by a typed `gpu`, in `sizing waste` and the digest; 17.2b the API, the console's Workloads and Cost pages and the Hub MCP server (their models leave unknown keys out, so it needs new fields and regenerated console types); 17.2c a GPU's price from list prices and GPU-hours in the Bill check's estimate.
- 17.2a built 2026-11-20: each GPU of a GPU-requesting container stored with its Sizing row (jsonb: no migration) and a verdict: `idle` (busiest hour under 5%), `busy`, `learning` (fewer hours read than the policy's history), `shared`, `unread`; computed under the container's policy and window, never for an excluded one, and counted only for ready containers. Decided while building: a Workload's "N of M" and its money count each judged GPU by the share of the window it was held (hours read over the window's hours, capped at 1), so a pod that moved across three GPUs counts one GPU, as Waste counts average pods. `prices.gpu` (per GPU-hour, at least 0, optional) on the Contract's or a cluster's prices, a cluster's own without one taking the Contract's; it survives the spot blend, unblended (ponytail: until 17.2c). Its monthly cost is a line beside Waste: Waste, its cost and the ranking unchanged (tested); Savings and the Bill check read no GPU field. GPUs not judged (shared, unread, learning) are said beside the count, by the same held share, so none idle never reads as all busy. A priced Contract with one cluster unpriced for GPUs shows no total, never a partial one. `squero sizing waste` (a Workload with idle or unjudged GPUs and no waste listed too, `--json`'s `gpus`) and the digest's line. The agent's `sizing_for_workload` unchanged (tested), so no eval rerun; the OpenAPI unchanged, so no console types. Until 17.2c a list-priced cluster prices its GPUs only by typing its prices in full (`resolve` passes them through `_typed`, which keeps `gpu`: checked by reading). Phase 17's done-when on fixtures: a test from DCGM's series through `sizing refresh` and a stored run to `sizing waste`'s idle GPU and its cost. Two Contracts' idle GPUs kept apart through the report and the digest. 22 mutations, all caught (one, counting every verdict, survived until a test summed a mix).
- 17.2b built 2026-11-20: the API's `Workloads` (the Contract's `gpus` and each Workload's), each `Container`'s GPUs with their verdicts (`GpuUse`), and the Cost page's `Allocation.gpus` (the report's, beside the Savings; kept when comparing); `report()` writes each count's `text` once, as `sizing waste` says it, so the console and the Hub MCP server never word it themselves. Console: the total under the waste, an Idle GPUs column only on a Contract with GPUs, each GPU in a Workload's detail (verdict, busiest hour, hours, share held, peak memory), the total on the Cost page; all as text. Hub MCP: `sizings`' `idle_gpus` for the Contract and each Workload, `cost`'s `gpus`, and both descriptions say so; no node or pod name in that text, so masking has nothing new to cover. Decided while building: counts show up to two decimals (0.25 of a GPU had read as 0.2 beside a cost worked out from 0.25). OpenAPI and console types regenerated. Isolation: one Contract's idle GPU reaches its own `sizings`, `cost` and Workloads page, never another's. 9 mutations (6 in the Hub, 3 in the console), all caught.
- 17.2c built 2026-11-20: node facts carry each node's GPUs (`nvidia.com/gpu` capacity; the key only on a GPU node). With list prices, in a cluster with nodes with GPUs and nodes without, the CPU and memory rates and the spot shares are the GPU-less nodes', and each GPU node's price above its vCPU and GiB at the rates the Bill check prices them at (unblended, a spot one at the spot rate) is its GPUs', summed over GPU nodes and divided by their GPUs; the price note names it (AWS and Azure, whose instance prices include the GPUs). GPUs on every node: the rates are the whole nodes' as before, the GPUs unpriced, the note saying to type the cluster's prices in full; GPU nodes no dearer than their CPU and memory: unpriced too, never priced at 0.
