The Bill check's estimate adds the cluster's GPU-hours at its `gpu` (typed or listed), read only where there is one, so each node's price adds back whole; tested with nodes on spot and on-demand, with and without GPUs, and a GPU node's spot list price at another ratio than the others'. ponytail: one GPU rate for spot and on-demand GPU nodes, the latest mix's, outside the spot part (`spot_estimated`); MIG profiles' capacity isn't counted. The grill's "typed wins": on a list-priced cluster that is typing its prices in full, as before (a `gpu` alone in `list_prices` would be a new setting, not asked for). Decided while building, against the grill's Q10 ("several GPU models use list prices"): one GPU rate per cluster, so with several models each is priced at their average; the nodes' total adds back, not each node's. GCP gated: its list price is a machine's cores and RAM and its bill is read as its core and RAM lines, so its GPUs stay unpriced (noted) and its Bill check leaves GPU-hours out, typed `gpu` or not. Known effects, recorded in the docs: a mixed list-priced cluster's CPU and memory rates drop on upgrade (the GPUs' share was in them), so its money for waste, requests and Savings drops too; time-sliced GPUs advertised as replicas are priced per replica; the nodes that could go are priced without their GPUs. Tested through stored node facts, `effective()` and the Bill check itself (an on-demand m5, a spot m5 and a GPU node reconciling whole); `gpu_hours` kept to the cluster's labels (two clusters on one store). Nothing new stored but the node facts' key, and no API change. 16 mutations, all caught (one, the GPU nodes in the spot shares, survived until a GPU node's spot list price was at another ratio).
- 17.3 to 17.5 grilled 2026-11-20 (three rounds, all recommended), on vLLM's docs (no real exporter: the paid GPU check is Deferred). vLLM's metrics: `vllm:num_requests_running`, `vllm:num_requests_waiting`, `vllm:kv_cache_usage_perc` (a 0 to 1 fraction despite its name), each with `model_name`; `vllm:cache_config_info` carries `gpu_memory_utilization`, what vLLM reserves of the GPU up front, so DCGM's memory used reads near it whatever the load.
  - **Order: 17.5, 17.3, 17.4.** vLLM only (others when a Contract runs them), report only, checked on fixtures and said "not checked live".
  - **17.5, GPU node consolidation.** GPUs a third resource in the nodes that could go (15.4): a node's GPU pods fit on other GPU nodes with free GPUs and, where nodes carry GPU Feature Discovery's `nvidia.com/gpu.product`, the same product (else by count, said "GPU model not checked"); a node that could go priced with its GPUs. **Unrequested GPUs** (a new term) counted per node and priced at the cluster's GPU rate, apart from Idle GPUs and Waste: the Nodes page, a digest line and the Hub MCP server's `nodes`. Still report only: the autoscaler's drain removes nodes.
  - **17.3, the KV cache reservation.** A Risk on the vLLM container, kind `kv_cache_light`, when its busiest hour's KV cache usage over its Sizing policy's window (and history) stays under 50% of the blocks: the usage, the current `gpu_memory_utilization` and the block count, read as "a lower setting or a smaller GPU or MIG slice would likely hold it". No new setting computed: the model's weights sit in the reservation and no metric gives their size. Shown wherever Risks show; nothing applied (an argument, which the Actuator never changes).
  - **17.4, Floors from vLLM.** A vLLM Workload's hourly Need: that hour's replicas x its busiest KV cache usage / 0.8, rounded up, and at least one replica more in an hour when requests waited. Phase 14's Floor, Weekly profile and Backtest reused whole, only the Need's source changed. A Workload without an HPA gets a Floor too, against its replica count, never below 2 unless it already runs fewer; its saving is the replicas above the Floor x their GPUs x the GPU price. Its Weekly profile: the blocks, and the cron triggers with a note that they need a KEDA ScaledObject for it.
  - **Glossary:** Unrequested GPU added; Need and Floor extended (a vLLM Workload's Need from its KV cache and queue; a Floor without an HPA).
- 17.5 split while building (2026-11-20), as 17.2 was: 17.5a the read, the fit, the money and the digest; 17.5b the API, the console's Nodes page and the Hub MCP server's `nodes` (their models leave unknown keys out).
- 17.5a built 2026-11-20: GPUs read apart from cpu and memory (a node's `gpus`: allocatable, capacity, requests summed over every pod by the cluster's labels only, and GPU Feature Discovery's `nvidia.com/gpu.product`; a Contract pod's GPUs read in its namespaces, or `gpus_unread` for a time-sliced GPU or a MIG profile), so no reader of the cpu and memory dicts changes (tested). The nodes that could go: a moving pod's GPUs need as many free where it lands, of the same product where both nodes say theirs (else by count, the could-go line saying "GPU model not checked"), and what lands is no longer free; a pod moved twice keeps the product of the node it first left; a `gpus_unread` pod lands nowhere, so its node stays (a GPU pod packed by CPU and memory alone before, so some clusters' could-go lists shrink). It holds after the Sizings (`after` keeps a pod's GPUs: tested). A node that could go is priced with its GPUs at the cluster's GPU price, one rate spot or not as the Bill check prices them, so a list-priced GPU node is worth its list price; without a GPU price no cost, never a part. Unrequested GPUs per cluster (allocatable less every pod's requests, the nodes with some, their cost) and the digest's line; overlapping a node that could go, said so in the docs. A read from before 17.5a goes as before. Isolation: the GPU node sums by the cluster's labels (two clusters on one store), the pods' GPUs by the Contract's namespaces (the query count test). The OpenAPI unchanged. 18 mutations, all caught. Not checked live (the paid GPU check is Deferred).
- 17.5b built 2026-11-20: the API's `Node.gpus` (`NodeGpus`: allocatable, capacity, requests, product), a pod's `gpus` and `gpus_unread`, could_go's `gpus` and `gpu_note`, and each cluster's `unrequested_gpus`; OpenAPI and console types regenerated. The Nodes page: a chip on each GPU node (`1 of 4 GPUs asked, Tesla-T4`), the GPUs of the nodes that could go (and "no GPU price" where a cost is missing for them), the GPU note, and a line of Unrequested GPUs; all as text. The Hub MCP server's `nodes`: each node's `gpus`, could_go's, and `unrequested_gpus`, with the description saying so. Isolation: one Contract's GPU node reaches its own Nodes page and `nodes`, never another's. 9 mutations (5 in the Hub, 4 in the console), all caught.
- 17.3 built 2026-11-20: where a container requests a GPU, vLLM's `vllm:kv_cache_usage_perc` read as the window's hourly peaks (the highest kept, and the hours read) and `vllm:cache_config_info` (`gpu_memory_utilization`, `num_gpu_blocks`), in the Contract's namespaces; a series counts for its own container (`container`, asking a GPU) or the pod's one container that requests a GPU (two such and no `container` label: neither). Decided while building: the hour's peak rather than its mean, since a cache that fills for minutes in an hour isn't light. The Risk `kv_cache_light` when the peak stays under 50% with the policy's history read, its text the peak, the blocks and the reservation, no new setting computed. It reaches every Risk's surface, the agent's `sizing_for_workload` too, as data in the tool's existing `risks` (no tool name, description or schema changed, and no Scenario runs vLLM, so no eval rerun). A failed read is a note, the Sizings kept. No API change (a Risk's kind is a string). Isolation: another namespace's vLLM series never read. 12 mutations, all caught. Not checked live (the paid GPU check is Deferred).
- 17.4 split while building (2026-11-20): 17.4a a vLLM Workload's Needs, read and stored in place of its HPA's (so one with an HPA gets its Floor, Weekly profile and Backtest through Phase 14's path); 17.4b a Floor for a vLLM Workload without an HPA against its replica count, the saving at its GPUs' price, and its Weekly profile's note.
- 17.4a built 2026-11-20: `usage.vllm_needs`, where a container of the cluster requests a GPU: per Workload and UTC hour, the pods that reported `vllm:kv_cache_usage_perc` that hour times the busiest one's hourly peak over 0.8, rounded up, and one replica more in an hour when `vllm:num_requests_waiting` went above 0; pods by their owners as Sizings resolve them, in the Contract's namespaces (another namespace's pod of the same name never counts: tested). Merged over the HPA's Needs in `collect` (a vLLM Workload's own win), read from the same hour on; its failure is logged and keeps the HPA's. ponytail: replicas are the pods that reported in the hour, so a rollout's hour counts the old and new pods. Not checked live. 12 mutations, all caught.
- 17.4b grilled further while building (2026-11-20, all recommended): Q16, a fixed-replica Workload's saving is Phase 14's (each hour, its replicas less the larger of the Floor and that hour's Need: what an autoscaler holding the Floor would save), not Q12's every-hour count, said on its line; Q17, a replica count lowered by hand is never realized Savings; Q18, GPUs count in every Floor's saving and its Backtest's too many, HPA or not, never priced in part.
- 17.4b built 2026-11-20: `usage.fixed`: a GPU Workload without an HPA (a Deployment's or a StatefulSet's replica count, in the Contract's namespaces; another kind's not known; 0 in the lookback: scales to zero) joins the HPAs' bounds as `fixed`; an HPA wins. `floors` gives it a Floor only with stored Needs (not this run's read: an hour missed keeps it), and its texts say replicas; every Floor's saving and Backtest carry their replicas' GPUs (`gpu_saving`, `excess_gpus`); `floors_report` prices them with the GPU price or gives no cost, the total too; `floor_text`, the digest line (`can run fewer replicas`, or both kinds), `scheduled` (the cron triggers a KEDA ScaledObject would need, and a note) and the console's Floors section say "no HPA"; the realized drops leave fixed Floors out (tested both ways). API: `Floor.fixed`, `gpu_saving`, `Backtest.excess_gpus`, the total's `gpus` and `fixed`; console types regenerated. 24 mutations (20 in the Hub, 4 in the console), all caught. Not checked live.

### Phase 18: Platform (3 slices, grilled 2026-11-21)

- 18.1 A signed Helm chart (OCI) for the Relay and the Actuator. 18.3 A version-skew rule between Hub and cluster parts. 18.4 Fleet views across Contracts and clusters. 18.2 (OpenShift values) went to Deferred in the grill.
- *Done when:* a fresh kind cluster goes from `helm install` to a connected Relay in one command, in CI.
- Grilled 2026-11-21 (three rounds, all recommended), from what exists: the Relay and the Actuator install from kustomize (`deploy/relay`, `deploy/actuator`); CI builds the image for amd64 and arm64 on tags and pushes it to ghcr, unsigned; a Relay reports its version when it connects, an Actuator only the kinds it knows (`kinds=`, 12.8a).
  - **Order: 18.1, 18.3, 18.4.** The done-when needs only 18.1.
  - **18.1, the chart.** One chart, `squero`, with `relay.enabled` and `actuator.enabled`, each in its own namespace as today. It replaces the kustomize directories: `deploy-smoke` and the RBAC tests move to the rendered chart, so the RBAC lives in one place (two copies is how one gets widened). Tokens only by `existingSecret` names, never in values (Helm keeps values in the cluster and they end up in shell history). Published at `oci://ghcr.io/alexboscolo954/charts/squero`, its version the release's, by the tag run's `publish` job beside the image. The image and the chart signed with cosign keyless (GitHub's OIDC identity, logged in Sigstore's public log), with the SBOM and provenance attestations `docker/build-push-action` adds; the docs show `cosign verify`.
  - **18.3, Protocol** (a new term). A number per part (the Relay's, the Actuator's), from 1 for today's wire, raised only by a change an older part would misread (a field removed or renamed, or its meaning changed); an added optional field keeps it (as `kinds=`, `eviction` and Blocker reports did). The Hub accepts its own and the one before; an older or a newer one is refused, with an Agenda item saying which side to upgrade ("Actuator in eu speaks Protocol 3, the Hub needs 4 or 5: upgrade it"; "Relay in eu speaks Protocol 6, this Hub 5: upgrade the Hub"). The Actuator starts reporting its version and Protocol, as the Relay reports its version.
  - **18.4, Fleet** (a new term). A console page with a row per Contract: its Kill switch, its Agenda's counts by kind, its waste and requests cost in its own currency, its Idle and Unrequested GPUs; totals per currency only, never converted; a row links to its Contract's pages. The Hub MCP server's `now()` across Contracts already covers it there: nothing added.
  - **Checked in CI** by `deploy-smoke` (kind): the packaged chart `helm install`ed with the Relay and a token Secret, the Hub started in the same job, the Hub reporting that Relay connected at the Protocol it expects, and the rendered RBAC no wider than today's.
  - **The Hub's own install unchanged**: Docker Compose (`deploy/hub`). The chart is only for what runs in a client's cluster.
- 18.1 split while building (2026-11-21): 18.1a the chart, replacing the kustomize directories (tests, `actuator-smoke`, the RBAC checks and the docs on the rendered chart); 18.1b the done-when (`deploy-smoke` installs the chart into kind with a Relay and asserts the Hub sees it connected); 18.1c publishing the chart to ghcr OCI and signing the image and the chart (cosign keyless, SBOM, provenance). Decided while building (Q14, recommended): the client creates each part's namespace and token Secret, the chart neither, so `helm uninstall` never deletes a token or the Actuator's ConfigMap; the done-when reads "after its namespace and token Secret, one `helm install` brings a connected Relay".
- 18.1a built 2026-11-21: `charts/squero` (version and appVersion the release's, the image's tag the appVersion unless set), `relay.enabled` (on) and `actuator.enabled` (off), one `namespaces` list for every RoleBinding, the Relay's `SQUERO_RELAY_NAMESPACES` and the webhook's selector (empty: refused at render); the Relay's settings as values (`hub`, `cluster`, `prometheusUrl`, `writes`, `gitopsNamespaces`, `alertsPort`, `callSeconds`, `existingSecret`), the Actuator's (`hub`, `interval`, `existingSecret`) and its three optional grants as switches, on as before (`nodes`, `nodeEvents`, `self`); no token is ever a value and no Secret is ever rendered; `values.schema.json` refuses an unknown key; names fixed as the code expects (one release per cluster); the webhook's `caBundle` never templated, so an upgrade keeps the Actuator's own. `deploy/relay`, `deploy/relay-actions` and `deploy/actuator` removed; what they rendered kept in `tests/fixtures/rbac-0.61/`, and the chart, given their values, renders exactly their objects but the two Namespaces (whole objects compared: kind, namespace, rules, roleRef, subjects, the Deployments and the webhook), with writes, GitOps and each optional grant switched on its own. The tests that read those files read the rendered chart; they need `helm` and fail without it (pinned v3.17.3 in CI's lint-test and integration jobs, listed in AGENTS.md). `demo/actuator-smoke.sh` installs the chart with Helm (its namespace and Secret first) and checks that a `helm upgrade` leaves the webhook's certificate: passed in CI's integration job (cd63322), with its two pod checks. The docs: an "Installing the chart" section in `client-cluster.md`, every reference to the removed files moved, the release list in AGENTS.md. 21 mutations of the templates and the schema, all caught (one, the namespaces check, survived until its test gave the Hub's URL so only that check could refuse).

### Phase 19: Integrations breadth (about 8 slices, grill before building)

- PagerDuty and Opsgenie, ServiceNow, Microsoft Teams and Google Chat, generic outbound webhooks, managed Prometheus auth (AMP, Azure Monitor, GMP, Grafana Cloud), Datadog and New Relic as metric sources, JVM heap Sizing, Spark and batch sizing.
- *Done when:* each integration has a probe, a recorded Scenario and a passing eval, through the Phase 4 framework.

## Deferred

| Item | Why not now | Trigger to revisit |
|---|---|---|
| Full agent inside a client network | A Relay keeps credentials there already | A Contract forbids tool output leaving its network |
| Specialist sub-agents (IncidentFox) | Allowlists and good tools come first | Evals fail on cross-domain Scenarios |
| Anomaly detection (Prophet) | Needs history and tuning. Alerts already cover it | Repeated misses that no alert catches |
| Hierarchical retrieval (RAPTOR) | Full-text search over a small corpus is enough | Runbooks outgrow search plus embeddings |
| Multi-user RBAC, SSO | One Operator | A second person joins |
| Sandbox and credential proxy (IncidentFox, BSL) | A Relay keeps credentials out of the Hub | A Direct Contract's threat model demands it |
| Agent actions that run without approval | Not without months of evidence | Never by default. Sizings under Automation are approved as a policy ([ADR 0004](adr/0004-standing-approval-for-sizing.md)) |
| Fix PRs on GitHub/GitLab (HolmesGPT) | Needs code access per Contract | After Jira, when a Contract grants repo access |
| Splunk and other log sources | Phase 19 covers the metric and paging integrations first | After Phase 19 |
| KWOK load test of the Actuator (thousands of fake Workloads) | No Contract has that many Workloads yet | A Contract nears a thousand automated Workloads |
| The Actuator writing a Floor to an HPA or ScaledObject (Phase 14 only recommends) | A wrong minimum costs availability, GitOps puts a patched HPA back, and it is a new Actuator write | Backtests within budget on a real Contract four weeks in a row; then its own grill and an ADR |
| Compaction by the Actuator: evicting pods to empty a node (15.5) | The autoscaler's own drain does it once its setting is right, the default scheduler spreads an evicted pod back, and two controllers emptying nodes can work against each other | A cluster where the proposed autoscaler setting was applied and the nodes still stay |
| Node sizes proposed from list prices ("smaller instances would cost X less") | Karpenter picks sizes itself; on Cluster Autoscaler it means redesigning node groups the Hub can't read (grilled with Phase 16, 2026-11-12) | A Contract on Cluster Autoscaler asks for it |
| A cluster-wide read for the Blockers report, as a manifest a client may apply | It would be the first cluster-wide grant in a client's cluster | "Not visible" pods hide too much of a real report |
| CRDs for the Actuator's state | The Hub is the only source of truth; kubectl shows Events and its ConfigMap | A client wants to read Automation state with kubectl |
| GPU sharing by adopting an existing scheduler, KAI or HAMi (17.6) | The largest and least certain GPU slice; idle GPUs come first (grilled 2026-11-20) | 17.2 flags idle GPUs on a real Contract that time-slicing or MIG would pack |
| Batch inference (17.7) | Needs an inference server's metrics, which 17.3 reads first (grilled 2026-11-20) | 17.3 and 17.4 built on a real Contract |
| Paid GPU check: Phase 17's DCGM labels on a real exporter (a spot g4dn.xlarge on EKS for 2 hours, about 0.90 USD) | The sandbox's G-instance quotas (spot and on-demand) are 0 in us-east-2; skipped 2026-11-20 by the Operator rather than requesting a raise | The quota is raised, or a Contract runs GPUs |
| OpenShift values for the chart (18.2) | No OpenShift sandbox: values written without one would be guesses (grilled 2026-11-21) | A Contract runs OpenShift |
| A watchdog for the Hub itself: an hourly ping to an outside dead man's switch (`SQUERO_HEARTBEAT_URL`), which alerts when the pings stop | A stopped Hub can post nothing, and a ping goes to an outside service the Operator picks; Stale notices (A14) cover the gap seen live, the Hub up and a cluster down (grilled 2026-11-20) | The Hub itself went down unnoticed |

Phases 0 to 8 are done. On 2026-10-21 the Operator chose to grow the Hub into a resource optimizer as well (Phases 9 to 19, ADR 0004). 9.1 to 9.4 are built and checked on kind, and v0.3.0 (2026-10-23) stores Sizings hourly on the Mac Hub. v0.4.0 (2026-10-23) has the console app (9.5a) and v0.5.0 (2026-10-23) the weekly Sizing digest (9.5b). v0.6.0 (2026-10-23) gives the agent Sizings (9.6a) and per-cluster PromQL (9.6b). v0.7.0 (2026-10-23) has Masking (6.4), v0.8.0 (2026-10-24) unit prices (10.1), v0.9.0 (2026-10-24) cost by namespace (10.2a), v0.10.0 (2026-10-24) cost by team and label (10.2b), v0.11.0 (2026-10-24) Savings and compare periods (10.3a), v0.12.0 (2026-10-25) Realized Savings (10.3b), v0.12.1 (2026-10-25) dependency alert fixes, v0.13.0 (2026-10-26) cloud list prices (10.4), v0.14.0 (2026-10-26) the AWS bill check (10.5a), v0.15.0 (2026-10-26) Azure's (10.5b), v0.16.0 (2026-10-31) node-hour coverage in the bill check (10.5d) and v0.17.0 (2026-10-31) GCP's (10.5c). Phase 10 is done: the GCP sandbox's bill for 2026-11-02 reconciled at -1.0%. Phase 3 (Relay) was the last of Phases 0 to 8. Phase 11 is done (2026-11-08, v0.18.0 to v0.29.0): the Hub MCP server for Claude Code, Approval decisions, Approved Sizings and the Kill switch through it, Proposed changes as pull requests, the Agenda, and the whole console in the React app. Phase 3 is done (2026-11-09, v0.30.0 to v0.32.1): grilled 2026-11-08; 3.1 and 3.2 built 2026-11-08, 3.3a (v0.30.0), 3.3b (v0.31.0) and 3.4 (v0.32.0, its Relay's port setting fixed in v0.32.1) built and checked live on the kind lab 2026-11-09; ADR 0002 accepted with one cluster per Relay. Phase 12 (Actuator) was grilled 2026-11-09 into 8 slices; 12.1 (v0.33.0), 12.2 (v0.34.0), 12.3 (v0.35.0) and 12.4 (v0.36.0) are built. On 2026-11-10 the project was renamed from aiops-sre-agent to **squero** (the Venetian yard where gondolas are built and repaired; the logo is a gondola's ferro): the repo, image, Python package, `squero` command, `SQUERO_*` settings, `squero_*` metrics and Kubernetes names, in one release (v0.37.0). Entries above keep the old names as they were then. The eval recordings' node and resource names were renamed too (`squero-control-plane`), and the 18 Scenarios that carried them rerun on Haiku (`evals/results/2026-11-10/`): 16 passed, `aws-lambda-timeout-cut` failed as on its last Haiku run, and `teaching-restores-from-secret` chose `bad_config` once, then passed twice (as it does with the old names). The same day the license changed from MIT to PolyForm Noncommercial 1.0.0 (no commercial use by others; releases up to v0.37.0 stay MIT) and `TRADEMARKS.md` reserves the name and the ferro logo. The README became a short overview with six guides under `docs/`. Phase 12's live check on kind was done the same day, and its two findings are built (12.4b); 12.5 (Automation) and 12.6 (Revert, through the Hub MCP server and the console's Automation page) are built, 12.5 and 12.6a released as v0.41.0, 12.6b as v0.42.0; 12.7 (PDB-safe eviction behind the Contract's flag, in windows) and 12.7b (never a pod of a Deployment with one replica or that no PodDisruptionBudget covers) released as v0.43.0; 12.8a (StatefulSets and DaemonSets) released as v0.44.0 (an integration test still calling `pods_of` without its kind fixed first); 12.8b (Argo Rollouts in place) is built and checked live; 12.8c (CronJobs through the webhook only) is built and checked live; Phase 12 complete and released as v0.45.0. An audit of the Actuator the same day (three reviews: its rollout state, eviction and webhook, the Hub's side) found no cross-Contract leak and no eviction against its rules, and failures-and-restarts bugs: the five that can harm a Workload are fixed in 12.9a, the narrower ones in 12.9b (both built and checked live, which found two more, fixed); 12.9c (the Hub hardened against a compromised Actuator's reports) is built; all three released as v0.46.0 (2026-11-12, migration 0030 on the Mac Hub: 3 Actuations, none untold). Phase 13 (Reflexes) was grilled 2026-11-12 into 6 slices, Phase 14 (Horizontal) into 5, Phase 15 (Nodes) into 6 and Phase 16 (Spot) into 6, the same day; 12.10 (a vanished node is never a Rollback, found in Phase 16's grill) is built and checked live on kind, which found one more false Rollback, a node that restarts and comes back: fixed in 12.11, built and checked live. Their CI run failed in the integration job on something else, a race in the Kubernetes server's `pod_logs`: for a crashed run whose log file the kubelet had already removed it answers `failed to try resolving symlinks in path "/var/log/pods/..."` in place of logs, which reached the caller as the pod's logs; it is now the same race as `unable to retrieve container logs` (tried again, then the latest run's logs with a note; the tool's name, description and schema unchanged, so no eval rerun). 13.1a (OOM kills of automated containers seen, told apart and reported, nothing changed) is built and its container-limit half checked live on kind; the gap in telling a kill at a container's own limit from one at a limit above it is looked up and resolved for 13.1b. 13.1b (the Step-up: in place, at a pod's creation, for a CronJob's next runs, its caps and its interplay with Rollbacks; ADR 0005) is built and checked live on kind, and 13.1c (its term and renewal, the Slack lines, the Automation page, the Triage thread's line) is built and its Actuator's side checked live on kind. The OOM Reflex (13.1 and 13.2) is complete. 13.4 (the Startup boost: a new pod's CPU limit times its rule's factor until it is Ready or 5 minutes old) is built and checked live on kind. 13.3a (burst CPU on the Hub's side: the last hour's throttling and the CPU peak, told to the Actuator, which does nothing with them yet) is built and checked live, and 13.3b (the Actuator makes the Burst, keeps it and ends it; Slack, the Automation page and a Triage's thread show it) is built and its Actuator's side checked live on kind. Also 2026-11-10: `SQUERO_MODEL_EFFORT` (the model's reasoning effort) and `SQUERO_EMBEDDING_BASE_URL` / `_API_KEY` (embeddings on their own gateway, so the model can be called directly on Anthropic, which has no embeddings API; set, the model's key and headers never reach it), for a model comparison on the evals (Haiku 5.5, Sonnet 5.5, Opus 5.5, three runs each) and an effort sweep on Opus 5.5, paid from one-off Anthropic credits. Results (`docs/development.md`, Model comparison): Opus 5.5 at medium effort passed 66 of 66, Sonnet 5.5 58, Opus at low 62 and at high 64, Haiku 5.5 38 (through an answer tool, LangChain not knowing it yet). The comparison found a bug: at the tool call limit an answer given as a tool got two results and Anthropic refused the conversation; the limit no longer counts or blocks the answer (`ToolBudget`). Decided 2026-11-10 and built in audit A13: one line in the weekly Sizing digest summing the week's Actuations ("Actuator this week: 5 resized, 1 rolled back").
